.topic 2420

We may at times send email with files for your store. These may be new license files, reports, database files etc. When these are sent in an email, the email downloads to your machine and displays in Outlook or Thunderbird.  To save the file, follow these steps.
Open the email by double clicking on it in your email manager (Outlook) to display the email.


Right click on the attachment file (attached file highlighted in blue above), select the option Save As.


Navigate to the location in which you wish to save it.  In this case we will save it to our L:\e-Bilitydownloads\ folder (as always). You could save it to Documents or Desktop if you wish (but other users will not be able to access the files if you do).
Once you find the location, click the Save button.


.topic 2430

After receiving a new license file from sales/support via email, and then saving the email attachment into a location accessible to other computer (Be it the L:\e-Bilitydownloads\ or a USB memory stick).
On each computer that runs Bookscan, open the location the new license file has been saved (e.g. Computer -> L:\e-Bilitydownloads\ or your USB memory drive once plugged into the PC).




Right click on the file Bookscan.lf, then select the Copy option, icon, or use CTRL-C.  By preference, the license file is saved to your database folder, which can be seen at the top of the Bookscan window.  This means you only have to replace it once for all PCs rather than on each one individually.



Navigate to the location shown, (or if your licenses are local on each PC, the location of C:\Program Files (x86)\Bookscan\ (Or C:\Program Files\Bookscan\ for a 32 bit system.  You can also just type this in the Address field.)



Right click where the files are listed and select Paste option.  When there is an existing license in either location you will be prompted to replace your existing file, click the Replace the file in the destination button.

You must then repeat the process on all other computers running Bookscan, if saving them locally.


.topic 2440

All register computers should have the same User Account and Password for use on the Points of Sale.  This same account must also be created on the server.  On the register computers the account may be a limited account, but on the Server it must be a Power User or Administrator.
To create a new User account, go to [Start Button] -> Control Panel -> Add or remove user accounts






Select Create a new account


In this example I will create the User called "POS". Enter the new Username when prompted.
Then click Create Account button.


Now click Create a password

You will now be back at the User Accounts screen.  Click on the user POS icon/name to select it and you will see the window above.  Select Create a Password option.


Now you need to enter the password you want to use for the account (and enter it a second time to confirm), enter a password hint and click Create Password button.


You will now have a screen showing the password protected account.  Click on the "X" in the top corner to close the window. The account has now been created on this computer with the password set. You can repeat these steps on the other register computers.


.topic 1020

Bookscan provides three methods of handling your ordering:

Minimum stock method
With this method you set a minimum stock level for each product in your database.  Draft purchase orders are created at end of day when you Close & Post the days sales and:
·	stock level for a product have fallen below the minimum stock level, or
·	when a special order or backorder is created.
You can manually add a draft order:
·	when creating a new title,
·	in the title master, using a context menu selection,
·	in the Draft Purchase Orders window.
The system will recommend a re-order quantity for each title and you can amend these quantities (and change the supplier) before having the system create your purchase orders.

Sales-based replenishment method
This method uses period sales to trigger re-ordering. Before starting an ordering session, you need to generate draft purchase orders in the Draft Generate window.
Draft purchase orders will be generated for products which have had sales in the nominated period or since the last regeneration depending on your selection. It will also list any special orders or backorders.
You can also manually add titles to the draft.
The system does not recommend a re-order quantity.
You can process your orders in either the Draft Purchase Orders window or the Generated Draft window.

Purchase Order Entry option
The Purchase Order Entry window allows you to create purchase orders without going through the draft purchase order system.

Importing from a csv file
If you have created a list of draft purchase order lines in some other software such as Excel, you can import them into Bookscan. The file should be in csv (comma-separated) format  and must comprise only two columns, ISBN and quantity. Both columns should be text format and if there is a header row, you should remove it. Open the Draft Purchase Orders window and use the right mouse button to open the context menu. Select "Load from csv file", select the file to be imported and the file will load into draft purchase orders.
Some notes:
The program will look up the master file for each product and set the order agianst that product's primary supplier.
If the master record does not have the primary supplier set, the default dummy supplier code ' ~~XX ' will be used. You can then change the order's  in the Draft PO window.
If the product code is not found in the master, the draft order line is not loaded. A log file (DPOImportLog.log) will be created in the same folder  as the csv file. This will list the products (and quantites) that could not be loaded.

.topic 1030

Minimum stock levels
The default minimum stock level is set on the Titles tab of System Options. When adding new products to the title master, the minimum order field will display this default level. The default level will also be set when importing products using the Import Title window and when importing direct from a web site.

Draft Purchase Orders window
The Draft Purchase Orders window can be opened from the main menu: Purchasing/Draft Order/Add/Change.
When you open the window, the drafts will be filtered by the first supplier, sorted alphabetically, to have orders in the draft. To view different suppliers, select them from the Supplier combo box.
Shortcut
If you have the browser filtered by supplier, you can navigate quickly to other suppliers that have draft orders, using the arrow buttons on either side of the supplier combo box.



Unfiltered view
To remove the supplier filter, untick the Supplier Filter (F6) checkbox; the browser will display all draft titles. Now as you click different lines in the browser, the Supplier combo box will display the supplier of the currently highlighted draft order.
To reset the filter either:
·	tick the Supplier Filter (F6) checkbox. The browser will filter to the supplier in the Supplier combo box,
·	select a new supplier in the Supplier combo box.

Supplier details
The currently selected supplier's details are displayed in the left, top of the screen.
The supplier's setting for EDI ordering is displayed in ared box next the supplier's name. The options are:
·	Pacstream – uses the Pacstream service to transmit orders,
·	Email – orders will be emailed to the supplier,
·	No EDI – you will have to print and fax or post the order.

Browser
The browser displays either all the draft orders or just those for the currently selected supplier.
The browser lines are displayed in alphabetical order either by title or by author, depending on which option has been selected in the Purchasing tab of System Options.
Shortcut
If you right click on the browser and select Skip to Author/Title, a window will open where you can enter the title or author you require. Just enter the first few letters of your search e.g. 'Harry' or 'Rowl', and then click the OK button. The browser cursor will then move to the selected title/author.

Browser options
Put a title on hold
Putting a title on hold marks it as not to be included on the purchase order. You can do this by double clicking in the Hold column on the browser.

Delete a draft order line.
Highlight the lines in the browser you wish to delete, open the context menu (right click on the window) and select Delete from the drop down menu.

Filter the draft orders by origin
Products can appear on the draft from a variety of Origins.
To filter titles by their source, click on the Refined Filter (F9) button or open the context menu then select 'Filter by source'. Click on the filter you require and click OK.



Source codes include:
·	Sp	Special
·	Bi	Bisac files
·	Bo	Routed from purchase order
·	Re	Regenerated
·	In	Invoice backorders
·	Te	Academic module
·	Op	Operator
·	PO	Entered via the Purchase Order entry window.
Other non source code filter items are:
·	Non-specials only
·	No academic titles
·	Filter by imprint
·	Only titles on hold
·	Only unheld titles
·	Lift filter

Change Supplier
To change the supplier you wish to order from, highlight the Lines in the browser you wish to move to a new supplier. Click on the Swap Supplier (F7) button. A pop up window will open where you can select the new supplier. Click one of the save buttons or press the Enter key to complete the move.

Department ordering
You can also prepare your purchase orders by department although you will need to switch back to supplier mode to finalise them.
·	Right click on the window to open the context menu and select Department ordering.
·	The supplier drop-down box will change to listing all the departments, with the first department automatically selected.
·	Select the department you require; the browser will display on lines for products from that department.
·	Process each line just the same as you would for ordering by supplier. All the filtering options also work the same way.

Draft Purchase Orders: Edit window
You can examine each draft order line in detail. Highlight a line in the browser and click on the Edit button (or just double click on the line) to go into detailed view.
For a more detailed view, click on a title and then the Edit button (or double click on the title) to go into detail view.



Here you can amend the following fields:
·	Change the order quantity.
·	Add purchase order comments which will appear on the purchase order against that line.
·	Change the minimum and maximum stock levels.
·	Place the draft order on Hold.
·	Mark the draft order as a consignment order (consignment module only)
·	Change the title's status.
·	Change the titles master comment.
·	Change the title to a firm.

Browsers
Receiving History browser
The receiving history window displays each time the title was received into stock, transferred to another store, returned to supplier or made into a kit.

Sales History browser
The sales history window displays the individual sales history of the title including when and to whom, the quantity and the sale price.

Branch Stock browser
Multi-store users can view the stock figures at other branches. Click on the Show Branch Stock (F8) button.

Navigation and saving changes
Use the Save & Next (F12) and Save & Previous (F9) buttons to scroll through the titles, saving your changes as you go.
Use Save To Browser (F11) button to save your changes and return to the list view.

Finalising a draft purchase order
When your draft order is ready to be finalised, make sure you have the supplier selected in the Draft Purchase Orders window.
Click the Finalise (F12) button to open the Finalise PO window.



This window will show you all the product lines that will be ordered if you finalise the order for this supplier now – i.e. any lines from the Draft PO list that have a non-zero order quantity and are not on hold.

SAN
Note: this field will only be displayed if you have set up an alternate SAN number in System options .It does not affect most users,
Bookscan caters for you trading using two SAN numbers (i.e. you will have two accounts with some of your suppliers, one for each SAN). You can set the two SANs on the Licensee tab of System Options. With two SAN numbers set, the Finalise PO window will display a drop down box where you can select which of your SANs to use on the current purchase order.

PO Number
The PO sequence field shows default purchase order sequence (you can have up to five set in the Purchasing tab of System Options.
The Next PO# field shows the next available purchase order number.

Budget Month
If you are using the budgeting module, you can change the effective month of the order in the Budget Month box.

Special instructions
This is where you can enter instructions to appear on the purchase order.
You have three options:
Select from a list of existing instructions
·	In the Special Instructions drop-down list select the required instruction.
Create a new instruction by editing an existing instruction
·	Select an instruction from the drop-down list.
·	Click on the Edit button. This will open the Add field for editing.
·	Amend the Add field as required.
·	When the order is finalised the new instruction will be saved for future use.
·	Note that the existing instruction will not be overwritten - you will just create a new one.
Add a new instruction
·	Type a new instruction in the Add field(such as Backorder if out of stock) that you want to appear on the order.
·	When the order is finalised the new instruction will be saved for future use.

Delete an instruction
	Select the instruction you want to delete from the drop down list
	Right click in the window, a context menu will apprear with the options Delete PO Instruction & Save Instructions.
	Click Delete PO Instruction & then click YES in the Confirmation dialog box that pops up.

Options
·	The Replace checkbox causes to PO instructions hard coded on the PO form, as opposed to adding to these instructions.
·	The Large Font checkbox causes the instructions to be printed in a larger font. Only use this for short messages such as "URGENT", otherwise the full message will not display.
·	The backorder instructions are pre-set instructions to the supplier on how to handle your backorders. The selected instruction will print on the purchase order form.

Totals
The total box displays the following:
·	Lines - the number of lines (products) that will be ordered for this supplier
·	Qty - the quantity of products being ordered.
·	Value - the total cost for the order (you need to take into account that products may have no cost in the title master)
·	Supp min - the supplier minimum order level.

Print Options
·	EDI Orders - if the supplier is setup as an electronic order supplier, this option will be checked. If the supplier is setup as a Pacsteam supplier, this flag will be automatically set. You can, untick the option if you wish to fax/telephone the order instead.
·	Print EDI Orders - if you wish for the electronic orders to print, you can also check this option (it will send electronically, but also print)
·	Copies - if you are printing the order you can select the number of copies to print
·	Print preview – preview the purchase order form on the screen before printing it.
·	Batch Print - this option will cause the purchase order forms to print at the end of processing (when you select the 'Finalise and Close' button) instead of printing the orders as you finalise them.

Purchase order creation
After you have checked your order in the browser and made any changes to the purchase orders settings, you can create the purchase order.
Note - if you notice that expected lines are missing from the browser, click the Cancel button to return to the Draft PO window. Check to see if those products have a zero order quantity or are set as 'hold'.

To finalise the single order, click Finalise & Close (F12).
If you wish to finalise multiple orders, click Finalise & Next (F11).
If you wish to skip to a different supplier, use the Back (F8) or Skip (F9) buttons.

If you wish to process (finalise) all suppliers orders, check the option ' Process All' and then click one of the finalise buttons.




.topic 2210

Using the sales based replenishment method, draft orders are generated in three ways:
·	As special orders and backorders are generated, a draft order for the item is immediately created.
·	Draft purchase orders can be created manually in the title master, draft purchase order window and when creating a new title.
·	In the Draft Purchase Orders Generate window, you can batch create draft orders based on sales over a specified period or on sales since you last generated drafts. These draft orders have a zero re-order quantity. You will need to allocate a quantity against each line while processing it.

After generating draft purchase orders in the DPO Generate window, you have a choice of processing them in the Draft Purchase Orders window or in the Generated Draft window. See below for details.

Draft Purchase Orders Generate window
In this window you can generate a list of products that have had sales in a nominated period. Open the window from the main menu: Purchasing/Draft Order/Regenerate.



There are two date range options:

Sales by date range
You would only use this option occasionally. It creates draft orders for each product sold between the dates selected. You would use this option for say, looking at what you sold leading up to last Christmas or last Father's Day.

Sales since last review
This is the option you would use regularly for your re-ordering. It will generate a list of products sold since the last review date. The default date and time is set to the last time the Regeneration was run. You can change this setting if you wish.

With both options, the system will generate sales and stock movement data for the last 13 months for each item with sales in the nominated date range.

WARNING - When you regenerate draft orders using the data range you will overwrite the draft orders with origins of Sa and Av.

Draft Purchase Order window
You can now use the draft purchase orders window in the same way as for the minimum order method, although the data displayed in the edit window is slightly different.

Note: the order quantity is set at zero for sales-generated draft orders. You will need to enter an order quantity for each line you wish to order.
.
For products where sales and stock movement data have been generated via the DPO Generate window, the edit window will display a browser showing a summary of the sales and stock movements over the last 12 months.



Clicking on the Show by Month button pressing the F7 key will toggle through the four different displays:
Stock movement summary
Sales by month
Sales for the last 14 days
Sales history
Clicking on the first column header in the browser will return to the original view.
The diagram below shows the view of sales and stock movements for each of the last 13 months.



Finalising a draft purchase order
Finalise your orders in the same way as outlined for the minimum order method.


Generated draft window
This window is an alternate way for processing your draft orders. While it gives much less data to work with, it is faster and simpler.
The window displays all draft orders generated using the DPO Generation window. Special order lines will show in this window also. They will appear as a white line (with a quantity already entered) when 'Show only zero order quantity (F6)' is not checked.

After running the regeneration, open the Generated Draft window from the main menu: Purchasing/Draft/Generate Draft.



The lines showing in Yellow are the lines that have not been reviewed (and still have a value of 0 in the Qty Ord field).

Note: Lines entered manually in the DPO window will not show here but will be included when you create the purchase order.

Reviewing the List
To review the list, start at the top.
·	Click in the Qty Ord field, so it highlights the value.
·	Key the value you wish to order.
·	Press the Enter key on the keyboard, which then moves to the next line.
·	If you do not wish to order a line, when you come to it, you can click the Delete Line (F5) button, or use the F5 key on your keyboard, which will then move on to the next line.

Note: The lines shown in this window are also available in the regular Draft Order window (Purchasing -> Draft -> Add/Change), with a Origin code of Sa.  What this window allows is a fast way to review the list of regenerated titles only. Your manually added lines and Special order lines will still need to be processed as normal.

Show only Zero Order Quantity (F6)
If you wish the lines they have been reviewed (shown in white in the image above) to not show, simply check this option.

Finalising a draft purchase order
You can Finalise Orders in this window by using the Finalise Orders (F12) button. Alternatively you can do it from the Draft PO window. It both case the Finalise PO window will display and you can finalise your orders as outlined in the minimum order section.



.topic 1040

The Purchase Order entry window allows you to create purchase orders without going through the draft purchase order system.
The window can also be used as a quick way of adding lines to the draft.
When finalising you will be given the option of saving to draft or creating the purchase order.

Step 1: Open the window
From the main menu select Purchasing/ Draft Order/ Purchase order entry.

Step 2: Select a supplier:	
To select a supplier, click the arrow     in the supplier box and select the new supplier.

Step 3: Header instructions

PO number sequence 				
If you use more than one purchase order number sequence, click on  the down arrow       of the PO # Sequence box and select the sequence you require.
	
Budget month
If you are using the budgeting module, you can change the effective month of the order in the Budget Month box.

(Special) instructions
In the Instructions box you can enter a comment (such as Backorder if out of stock) that you want to appear on the order.
Click on the down arrow       in the box and select the comment you require.

Print Instructions
If you are printing the order you can select the number of copies and choose to by-pass the print preview.

Electronic ordering		
Alternatively if you are using electronic ordering you can click on the EDI check box
You can also select the order instructions (such as Backorder if out of stock or NYP) that you wish to appear on the order.

Quick order
Check this box if you just want to enter ISBNs and the quantities without changing the pricing etc.



Step 4: Input line
Enter the details for each line of the order and then click Save Line ( or press F12 key)

Consignment
If the stock for this line is to be on consignment check this box (or press F7 key).

Price
This shows the current cost price for the title that is stored in the title master.

Royalties (Wholesale version only)				
If the title attracts royalties, check this box and enter the royalties agreement number in the next field.

Step 5: Ordering from a different supplier
If you enter an ISBN in the input line that does not normally come from that supplier you will get a warning message.
Click on the appropriate button in the message window:
Change supplier
Changes the primary supplier in the title
master and adds the line to the order.

Place order	
Adds the line to the order but doesn't
change the supplier in the title master.

Cancel	
Do not add the line to the order.

Step 6: Editing
To make corrections to saved lines, double click on the line in the browser to copy it into the input line. Then type in your changes and click Save Line.
To delete a line, right click on it in the browser and select Delete from the drop down menu.

Step 7: Posting or saving to draft
To post the order, click the Finalise button.
You will getting a dialogue box asking whether you want to finalise the order or save it to draft.
Select the required option ( or Escape to continue adding lines).

Finalise
If finalising, the system will post the order and either display a window showing the purchase order number or (it print preview has been selected) show the purchase order.

Save to draft
If saving to draft  the lines will be copied to draft. In this case the header information is not saved ( you will re-set it when finalising in draft).
The lines will be saved with an origin code of PO.

.topic 2320
This section is for members of Leading Edge group who place their orders through the Leading Edge system and then download an import file from it's web/FTP site to then import that order (without having to re-key it)

Note: It is assumed that you have downloaded the Leading Edge Purchase Order file (e.g. Lonley_Planet_1234.Lee) from the leading Edge website and have saved it to an accessible place on your computer such as C:\e-Bilitydata\EDI\LeadEdge\.

Open the Purchase Order Import window from the main menu: Purchasing /Draft Order/Web PO Import.



Importing a Leading Edge file
Click the Import File (F11) button and navigate to the folder holding your Leading Edge import files.



Select the required file and click the Open button. The file will update two Bookscan tables:  webpoline.dbf and webpohead.dbf. The order will be displayed in the browsers of the Purchase Order Import window.

Suppliers and SANs
Leading Edge orders files link to the suppliers in Bookscan using the supplier's SAN. So you need to have SANs set up for each supplier in the Address tab of your supplier master.
If you don't have a SAN set for a supplier, the Purchase Order Import window will display '????' in the code column of the upper browser. For an example see the second line in the PO Import window diagram above. SAN 9012826 needs to be added to the supplier's SAN field. You will need to fix this before proceeding. Open the supplier master and enter the missing SANs. Then, back in the PO Import window use the right mouse to open the context menu and select 'Update suppliers'. The browser will now display correctly.

Products not on file
If any lines in the lower browser are shown in yellow, it means that the product is not in Bookscan. To add products to the master file, highlight them in the browser and open the context menu (by right clicking the mouse) and select 'Add selected ISBNs'. The products will be added to the master with these fields completed: ISBN, title, author, sell price, RRP and supplier.
All items must be in the title master before you can create an Bookscan purchase order.

Select a purchase order number
The Next PO number field will display the next available PO number. You can change the PO sequence here if you wish, and set a budget month if you use the budgeting functionality. Tick the check box to print the purchase order as it is created.

Creating the purchase order
In the upper browser, highlight the order you wish to process into Bookscan.
Click the Save PO (F12) button to process it.
The purchase order will be created and will be displayed like any other purchase in the Purchasing Order Enquiry window.









.topic 1070

Purchase orders can be viewed in the Purchase Order Enquiry window. Open the window from the main menu: Purchasing/Final/Enquire.
Purchase orders can be viewed by either supplier or by PO number. From the window you can print various reports and amend or delete purchase orders



Looking up purchase orders by supplier
Select a supplier in the supplier box. The browser will display all the outstanding orders on that supplier.
The Supplier POs combo box will fill with just that supplier's purchase order numbers. Select a PO in the combo box and the browser will display the order.

Looking up purchase orders by number
Enter the required PO number into the All POs combo box lists to display it in the browser.

Consignment stock
Consignment stock lines on order are shown in the browser in yellow.

Summary Details:
·	Value at cost/sell - shows the total value of the outstanding titles at cost/sell.
·	Items on order - total number of items not yet supplied.

Editing purchase orders
Click on the edit button to display the various editing buttons. Alternatively, you can call these functions using the context menu. Most of the buttons are self explanatory but the function of the Edit Item and Edit All buttons is outlined below:



Edit Item
Highlight a line you wish to edit and click the Edit Item button. A pop up window will open:



Here you can edit the master comments and status, the quantity on order, the shipping date and the purchase order comments.

Edit All
To edit all lines in the browser, click the Edit All button. A pop up window will open:



Here you can edit the master comments and status, the shipping date and the purchase order comments.

Cancellation report
The cancellation report function will allow you to create a letter to the supplier canceling an order, either print or email that letter, delete the purchase order from the file and re-direct the order to the draft (if required).
Click on the Cancellation Rpt (F6) button, and the window will go into Cancellation mode, with 'Cancellations' displayed in red at the top of the window. Two columns, 'Cancel' and 'Select' will be added to the browser.



·	To select a line to be cancelled double click on the Select column.
·	To change the cancellation quantity, double click on the Cancel column. A pop up window (see diagram above) will open where you can type in the new figure and click OK.
Click the Print Report (F12) button. The report will print (and be stored for emailing by e-Comms) and another pop up will display



Make sure the report has printed correctly before proceeding. Clicking 'yes' will delete the lines from the purchase order. If you wish you can re-route the lines to draft purchase orders to re-order from a different supplier. Clicking 'No' leaves the order lines unaffected.

Enquiry Report
The enquiry report function will allow you to create a letter to the supplier enquiring on order. If in the supplier master, the supplier has been set up for emailing, the letter will be emailed by e-Comms.
Click on the Enquiry Report (F8) button, and the window will go into Enquiry Report mode, with 'Enquiry Report' displayed in red at the top of the window. A new column 'Select' will be added to the browser.
·	To select a line to be enquired on, double click on the Select column.
·	Click the Print Report (F12) button. The report will print (and be stored for emailing by e-Comms).

Special Orders
The Special Orders function will allow you to create a letter to the customer for whom a purchase order has been placed. Click on the Special Orders (F8) button, and the window will go into Special Orders mode. Only items which have special orders on them will be displayed in the browser. Two new columns will be added to the browser – 'customer' and 'select'. The customer column will display the name of the customer the item has been ordered for.
·	To select the line to be reported on, double click on the Select column.
·	Click the Print Report (F12) button and the report will.

Alternate PO
If an alternate PO number has been added to an order it will be displayed in the Alternate PO field. The edit this number or add an alternate number, click on the Edit Alternate PO button. Alternate PO numbers relate to Leading Edge orders; see the Purchase Order import section below.

Reports
To view the available purchase order report, click on the printer icon on the main toolbar.
This will display the reports:
·	Reprint - reprint a purchase order.
·	All - print a summary of all outstanding purchase orders.
·	Outstanding – print all POs placed with the currently selected supplier.
·	Back orders – print all POs on backorder with the supplier.
·	Back orders with (supplier) - print all backorders with the currently selected supplier.




.topic 1080

Summary of receiving procedures

Receiving is done in the Receiving Stock window. Open the window from the main menu: Purchasing\Incoming\Receive.

Set up to receive stock
·	Select the supplier.
·	Enter the supplier invoice details.
·	Select the purchase orders

Entering the stock
For each item received:
·	Scan the barcode of each item received.
·	Enter the quantity invoiced and quantity received.
·	Enter the amount you have been charged for the stock (your cost price).
·	Set your selling price, if not already set.
·	Mark the products to have barcode labels printed, if required.

Post into stock
After entering all lines of the receipt:
·	Print the goods received note. Any items ordered for special orders will have special order slips printed.
·	Click on the Post button.

Effects of posting
For each line of the receipt
·	If the barcode label flag has been set, barcode labels will print.
·	The last cost price in the title master will be overwritten with the unit cost of this receipt line.
·	The selling and retail prices in the title master will be updated.
·	Stock will be assigned to special orders.
·	The on hand stock level will be increased.
·	The purchase order lines relating to this receipt will be marked as received. Completed purchase orders will be deleted.
·	The total of the supplier invoice will be posted to the accounts payable file and can be viewed in the Creditors window (if you are using the Creditors module).
·	The supplier invoice details will be saved to the receiving history files, ready for export to MYOB.
·	A line will be added to the stock history file for each item. This can be viewed in the Stock History tab of the title Master window.
·	Any credit claim will also be printed, posted to the creditors file, stored in the returns history file ready for export to MYOB and saved to the stock history file for viewing in the Title Master window.

					
Related items
Receiving second hand goods

	   Detailed Steps

.topic 1090

Receiving is done in the Receiving Stock window. Open the window from the main menu: Purchasing\Incoming\Receive.

Entering the supplier invoice details
The steps outlined below assume you have received the stock and an invoice from your supplier. If you do not have the invoice, you can still book the delivery into stock using a dummy invoice number which can be amended when you receive the actual invoice: see 'Receiving without an invoice' below.
The receiving window links directly with Bookscan's creditors module (if you are using that function) and exports the supplier invoice data to MYOB. So it is important that the supplier invoice fields are completed accurately.

Products are received into stock using the Receiving window. Open the window from the main menu: Purchasing/ Incoming/ Receive.

Supplier selection
·	Select the supplier in the Supplier combo box by one of the following methods
·	Type the supplier code in the field and press the Enter key.
·	Use the mouse to select the supplier from the drop down box.
·	Click on the F3  button (or press the F3 key) to open the supplier search window.
·	Click on the Alt F3  button (or press the Alt and F3 keys) to load the last supplier that you used in the program.
The supplier's settings will load in the window.

Supplier invoice details
Then enter the following information in the appropriate supplier invoice fields:
·	Invoice number,
·	Invoice date,
·	Invoice due date,
·	Invoice total (including tax),
·	Invoice tax (GST or VAT).
	
Purchase order selection
You will need to receive your delivery against the purchase orders with which you ordered the stock. Purchase Orders combo box drop down list will display all the selected suppliers unfilled purchase orders. Use the mouse to select purchase orders from the drop down box or type them one at a time in the field and press the enter key.
As you select each purchase order, its number will display in the multi-line box below the combo box and the outstanding lines on the PO will display in the browser.

You can receive stock without a purchase order, by ticking the None (F4) checkbox to the right of the PO fields.
(Be warned that if you do this, and the goods were ordered using Bookscan purchase orders, the purchase orders will remain as outstanding on the system. By receiving against purchase orders, the program can mark the orders as fulfilled as you post the receipt into stock).



Shortcut
If you don't know what purchase order a product is on, you can click on the PO's on Code button (F6). The edit line will clear, leaving just the barcode filed. Scan the products barcode into the Barcode field. A pop up window will be displayed, listing all the purchase orders for that product. Double click on the PO to select it.

Entering the stock
There are three ways of entering the lines of a receipt:
·	Scanning the barcodes,
·	Double clicking a line in the browser,
·	Receiving off the invoice or packing slip.

Scanning
In this scenario you scan the barcode of each item in the delivery.
Scan the barcode into the Barcode field
(If you don't know the product code you can search for the product by clicking the F2 button to open the Title Search window. You can also click the Alt F2 button to load the last product you used in the program).
The edit line will display in scan mode:
·	Inv Qty: this will default to quantity you have ordered on the selected purchase order. If the supplier has invoiced you for a different amount overtype this figure. In the example you have been invoiced for the full five copies of Jerilderie Letter that you order on PO #50058.
·	Add: the Add field defaults to '1'. (You can overtype this if you want). This is the number you are adding to the receipt on this scan.
·	Rec'd: this is the quantity you have already scanned in. In the example, three have already been scanned in and one more is being added.
·	Claims: this shows the number of items that have still not been received in. If it is not subsequently scanned in, it will become a credit claim for a short-supplied item.

Click the Save Line (F12) button, or press the F12 button, to save the line. Then scan the next item and so on.

Double clicking
Double click a line in the browser, loads the line into browser in edit mode:
·	Inv Qty: this will default to quantity you have ordered on the selected purchase order. If the supplier has invoiced you for a different amount overtype this figure.
·	Rec'd: type in the number of this item that you have received.
·	Claims: this shows that one item has still not been received in. If the Rec'd quantity is not amended, it will become s credit claim for a short-supplied item.

Click the Save Line (F12) button, or press the F12 button, to save the line. Then double-click the next item and so on.

Receiving off the invoice
If you have checked off the delivery against the invoice or packing slip, then you don't have to enter the receipt line by line. Use the right mouse button to open the context menu and select 'Receive all'. All the lines in the browser will show as fully received.
You can then edit any line by double clicking on it to load it into the edit line.

Receiving errors
You can also record where stock has been received damaged or faulty, the wrong product has been sent or you have been short supplied.
(Note: Depending on your supplier, you may not be able to make a claim this way. You may have to receive the stock in and then make a returns authorisation request for the damaged products. This is done in the returns area: from the main menu select Purchasing/ Returns/ Add/Change).

Load the relevant line to the edit line and click on the Claims button. The Claims pop up window will open:



The default is for the items to be set as 'short-supplied'. Type a new quantity in the appropriate field to change this. In the example above, the supplier has invoiced 3 of Gulliver's Travels. However they have only supplied one and one of those is damaged. So there are two claims, one short-supplied and one damaged.

Other settings
Barcode labels: tick the Barcode checkbox to have the program print barcode labels when posting.
Consignment: tick the Consignment checkbox to treat the stock as consignment stock. You must have purchased the consignment module to be able to use this feature.

Pricing
In the Receiving window you need to record the cost of each receipt line. You can also set the sell price and the recommended retail price (RRP) here. There are many pricing options, including receiviving stockinvoiced in a foreign currency. Click on this link for full details on pricing.

Cost prices
For each product received, the cost that you enter when processing the receipt will be set as the last cost price in the title master. It will also be used to re-calculate its average cost price.
Note: the average and last cost prices in Bookscan are stored exclusive of tax. In most cases your suppliers' invoices will display prices that are inclusive of tax.

Selling prices
If you are selling products at the recommended retail price, you will probably already have your selling price set in the title master and there will be no need to change them here. However if the price you set is to be determined by price charged to you, or if you have not yet set a selling price, you can set it here in the Receiving window. There are various price calculators built into the window to help you determine your selling price. These are detailed later in this chapter.
Note: the selling price and RRP in Bookscan are stored inclusive of tax.

Setting cost and selling prices
There are many configurations for handling prices during the receiving process. We show the simplest way first and look at some alternate methods. For the next example, the settings on the Details tab of the supplier master are as follows:
·	GST Inclusive:  the supplier's invoices show prices and totals are inclusive of tax.
·	Tax exempt flag: this is set to false
·	Pricing method: this is left as 'Not set'.
·	Currency: this is set to the local currency.



With these supplier settings, the edit line of the Receiving window is configured as displayed below:



As you load a product into the edit line for the first time, the cost, sell and retail fields will display the values set in the title master (when you re-load a line, the values will be those you have saved).



Note that the cost price displayed in the master file is exclusive of tax; the cost in the Receiving window is inclusive.

If the amount you have been charged for this product on the invoice is different from the price stored in the Cost (Inc) field, type the new amount into the field. The program will calculate the margin on this line and will display it in the Margin field.

If you wish to change your selling price, type the new price into the Sell (Inc) field. Similarly you can change the RRP field here. When you post the receipt, the selling and retail prices in the master file will be updated.

Post into stock

When you have entered all lines of the receipt, click on the Save to List (F7) button. Then you can either:
·	Move on to receiving the next delivery by clicking the Next Delivery (F7) button, or
·	Move to the received goods list by clicking the List (F9) button. From here you can post deliveries into stock.

Receiving window: List view
In List view, the receiving window will display two browsers:
·	The upper browser will list all receipts waiting to be posted into stock.
·	The lower browser will display the lines of the receipt currently selected in the upper browser.



To edit a receipt, double click on it in either browser. This will close list view and return the window to the original view, with the selected receipt loaded, ready for editing.

Goods received note
Before posting a receipt into stock, you will need to print a goods received note.
You can also choose to print at the same time the following reports:
·	Special order slips – these slips are placed with the stock to identify it as for a special order.
·	Special order letters – these are letters to customers advising them that their special is ready for collection. If an email address is stored against the customer, this letter can be emailed to customer by Bookscan e-Comms.
·	Title not supplied – this is a list of the specials orders for which there was insufficient stock received.

Posting
To post a receipt into stock, select it in the upper browser and then click on the Post (F9) button. This will open the Barcode & Post window.



Here you can choose which receipts to post:
·	the selected receipts,
·	all suppliers, or
·	all receipts for the selected supplier.

You can set some barcode labels printing options:
Print Barcode for all items: Forces the printing of barcodes, overwriting any earlier setting. You can also choose the label type.
Preprinted labels: Tick this checkbox if you have labels with a pre-printed logo.
Release web backorders: Tick this to release stock to fill e-Web backorders.



Click on the Post button to proceed.
If the total value of the received stock, including the value of any credit claims, is equal to the supplier invoice total, the receipt will be posted into stock and you will have finished.
If the amounts are not equal, the Check Invoice Totals window will display:



To ensure that the cost of goods matches your accounts payable, the total cost of your receipt should equal the supplier invoice total. If they a different, you should return to the Receiving window to amend your costs, though you can proceed with posting if you wish.
You can also amend the invoice and tax totals here.
Click the Post (F12) button to post the receipt or click the Cancel button (or press the Escape key) to return to the Receiving window.

Effects of posting
For each line of the receipt
·	If the barcode label flag has been set, barcode labels will print.
·	The last cost price in the title master will be overwritten with the unit cost of this receipt line.
·	The selling and retail prices in the title master will be updated.
·	Stock will be assigned to special orders.
·	The on hand stock level will be increased.
·	The purchase order lines relating to this receipt will be marked as received. Completed purchase orders will be deleted.
·	The total of the supplier invoice will be posted to the accounts payable file and can be viewed in the Creditors window (if you are using the Creditors module).
·	The supplier invoice details will be saved to the receiving history files, ready for export to MYOB.
·	A line will be added to the stock history file. This can be viewed in the Stock History tab of the title Master window.

Other settings in the receiving window

Barcode Printing
You can choose to print barcodes either progressively, as you enter each line, or later when you post the receipt into stock.
For progressive printing, tick the 'Progressive Barcode Print' checkbox located at the centre right of the Receiving window. With this flag set, barcodes will print as you press Save Line (F12) for each product.
Note: The default setting for printing of barcodes is set on the Purchasing tab System options. Here you can choose to always print or never print barcodes.

List view - upper browser
Errors column
In the upper browser, where deliveries had errors on them, the Errors column will display a tick. When you post, these lines will create a credit claim. To produce a Request for Goods form instead, double click on the Claims field to untick it.

List view - lower browser
Over Supplied
It will mark a line of an invoice in green if the quantity coming in is greater than the order quantity. (Where you receive lines against no Purchase Order; all these lines will be indicated in green).

Special Orders
Where there are special orders attached to a product in the browser, the 'Spec' column will display a tick.

Price Change
Products where you have changed a selling price during the receipt, will display a tick in the 'P_Ch' column of the browser. Also, when processing a line, if you change the selling price and there is stock on hand, the program will warn you that you may need to r-label your existing stock.

	

                    Return to summary

.topic 2690
Pricing calculation options
The default pricing calculations method can be set on Details tab of the Supplier Master window for each supplier. You can also set the standard discount or margin for each supplier in the Standard Discount field. However, you can set, change or remove the pricing calculator, and change the discount/margin, as you process each line in the Receiving window. The default pricing method is "not set".
The pricing calculator is used when you need to calculate the either the sell or cost price. For some settings it will change the order in which the price fields are displayed.

No Auto Calculations
There is a checkbox called 'No Auto Calculations' on both the Details tab of the supplier master and in the Receiving window. With this option ticked, the edit line of the receiving window will display according to the pricing method set, but it will not automatically calculate the Cost or Sell fields; you will need to overtype them when required.

Not Set
This option will suit most users for most suppliers.



·	The order of the pricing fields is: Cost, Margin, Sell, Retail.
·	When you first select a line of a receipt, the Cost, Sell and Retail price fields will load the values stored in the title master against the product.
·	You will need to manually key in Cost, Sell and Retail Price where necessary.
·	As the cost and sell price are changed, the Margin field is re-calculated.
·	The 'No Auto Calculations' setting has no effect on this value.

Discount off retail
The cost price will be calculated at retail less the supplier's standard discount.



·	The order of the pricing fields is: Retail, Discount, Cost, Sell.
With 'auto calculations'
·	When you first select a line of a receipt, the Retail and Sell price fields will load the values stored in the title master against the product. The Discount field will load with the standard discount. The Cost price will be calculated using the retail less the discount.
·	If you overtype the Retail field, the cost price is re-calculated and the Sell price is set to the retail price.
·	If you overtype the Discount field, the cost price is re-calculated.
·	If you overtype the Cost field, the Discount field is re-calculated.
'No auto calculations'
·	When you first select a line of a receipt, the Retail, Cost and Sell price fields will load the values stored in the title master against the product. The Discount field will be calculated using the retail and cost prices.
·	Where necessary, overtype the Retail, Cost and Sell price fields. The Discount field will be calculated as you change either the retail or cost prices.
·	If you overtype the Discount field, the cost price is re-calculated.

Discount off sell
The cost price is calculated by discounting off the sell price for that supplier. It is very rarely used. It is identical to Discount off Retail above except that the Retail and Sell fields are interchanged.

Markup on Cost
The sell price will be calculated by applying the markup percentage to the cost price. The markup used is the setting in the 'Discount' field.



·	The order of the pricing fields is: Cost, Markup, Sell, Retail.
·	When you first select a line of a receipt, the Cost, Sell and Retail price fields will load the values stored in the title master against the product. The Markup field will be calculated using the cost and sell prices.
With 'auto calculations'
·	If you overtype the Cost field, the Markup will display the standard markup. The Sell price is re-calculated using the Cost and Markup fields.
·	If you overtype the Markup field, the sell price is re-calculated.
·	If you overtype the Sell field, the Markup field is re-calculated.
·	If you need to change the Retail field, overtype it.
 'No auto calculations'
·	The Markup field will be set to 'read only'.
·	You will need to type in any changes to Cost, Sell or Retail fields. Changes the Cost or Sell field will cause the Markup field to be re-calculated.

Margin
The selling price will calculated by applying the set margin to the cost price. The margin used is the setting in the 'Discount' field.



·	The order of the pricing fields is: Cost, Margin, Sell, Retail.
·	When you first select a line of a receipt, the Cost, Sell and Retail price fields will load the values stored in the title master against the product. The Margin field will be calculated using the cost and sell prices.
With 'auto calculations'
·	If you overtype the Cost field, the Margin will display the standard margin. The Sell price is re-calculated using the Cost and Margin fields.
·	If you overtype the Margin field, the sell price is re-calculated.
·	If you overtype the Sell field, the Margin field is re-calculated.
·	If you need to change the Retail field, overtype it.
 'No auto calculations'
·	The Margin field will be set to 'read only'.
·	You will need to type in any changes to Cost, Sell or Retail fields. Changes the Cost or Sell field will cause the Margin field to be re-calculated.

Receiving in foreign currency
Where a supplier's invoices are in a foreign currency, you should set them up as a foreign currency supplier. This is done on the Details tab of the Supplier Master window. Set the currency the supplier uses in the Currency dropdown box. This will cause the red 'Foreign Currency' field to display. The available pricing calculators will be:
·	not set,
·	markup on cost,
·	margin
By setting the supplier as foreign currency, the money amounts displayed in the Creditors windows and on Credit Claims will be the supplier's currency.
Note: you will need regularly to set the exchange rates in the Currency window as the Receiving window will use these to calculate your cost and sell prices. The Currency window is opened from the main menu: Tables/ Authority Tables/ Currency.

Receiving
When a foreign currency supplier is selected in the Receiving window the display will change as illustrated below:



The exchange rate is set to the rate stored in the Currency file. You can overtype the rate if you wish.
In the edit line an extra field is displayed. The program always calculates the cost price from the foreign currency cost price.



·	The order of the pricing fields is: O/S Cost, Cost, Margin, Sell, Retail.
·	When you first select a line of a receipt, the O/S Cost, Sell and Retail price fields will load the values stored in the title master against the product.
·	The cost price will be calculated from the O/S Cost and the exchange rate..
·	The Margin field will be calculated using the cost and sell prices.
With 'auto calculations'
·	If you overtype the O/S Cost field, the cost price is re-calculated. The Margin will display the standard margin. The Sell price is re-calculated using the Cost and Margin fields.
·	If you overtype the Cost field, the Margin will display the standard margin. The Sell price is re-calculated using the Cost and Margin fields.
·	If you overtype the Margin field, the sell price is re-calculated.
·	If you overtype the Sell field, the Margin field is re-calculated.
·	If you need to change the Retail field, overtype it.
 'No auto calculations'
·	The Margin field will be set to 'read only'.
·	If you overtype the O/S Cost field, the cost price and margin are re-calculated.
·	You will need to type in any changes to Sell or Retail fields. Changes the Cost or Sell field will cause the Margin field to be re-calculated.

If the 'mark up' pricing method is set, the program behaves the same as for 'margin' except that a percentage markup is used in the sell price calculations. The 'not set pricing method behaves the same as the 'margin' setting with 'no auto calculations' set.


.topic 1100

The buyback option allows you to buy back books from customers in a point of sale environment with full cash and sales reporting.

ISBNs
Barcodes of second hand titles in Bookscan are recorded with the prefix 988 replacing the standard 978. So for ISBN  978040930309, second hand copies will be recorded as 988040930309.

Setting up the system for second hand titles
· Second hand titles all have supplier code BUYB. If it is not already on your database, add it in Supplier Maintenance.

· You may wish to set up a separate department , or departments,  for second hand titles. This would allow financial tracking of second hand stock. If you do add a second hand department, we recommend that you give it the code BUY. (See the shortcut below).

· All titles for which you intend to stock second hand titles, can be entered manually in the title master. In the Barcode field, you will have to enter 988 and then the ISBN (less the check digit) as described in the paragraph above.  Set the Cost Price field to be the average price you expect to pay to buy back the title.  Set the Sell Price field to be the average price for which you expect to sell the title.

Important short cut: If you do not have the 988-prefixed barcode set up, the system will automatically create it for you when you first scan the 978-prefixed barcode into the Buyback window's barcode field. All the fields from the 978-prefixed barcodes master record will be copied to the second hand one, except that:
·	"SECOND HAND COPY" will be appended to the end of the title,
·	 you will be able to select the BUY department, 978-prefixed barcode's department or a different department
·	the Minimum Stock field will be set to zero.
·	the Firm Sale  field will be set to true (i.e. no return to supplier allowed),
 To operate this feature the Auto create ISBN checkbox (just above the Barcode field)  must be selected

Buying in second hand books
Step 1:
Open the Buy back window: from the main menu, click on Purchasing, then point to Incoming and then click on Second hand.

Step 2:
Scan the barcode on the book. The system will do one of two things:
·  	If the title has a 988-prefixed barcode, i.e. the title was previously sold as a second hand title, it will accept it into the system and display the title on the screen with the median cost price.
·  	If the title has a 978-prefixed barcode, i.e. a normal barcode, Bookscan will translate it to a 988 prefix and look for the title in the title master. If it finds it, it displays the title and median cost price.
·	If it finds the 978-prefixed barcode but doesn't find the 988-prefixed barcode it, the system will offer to create the 988-prefixed barcode. You will need the Auto create ISBN checkbox selected for this to occur. (See the shortcut above).

Step 3:
Determine from the condition of the book how much you wish to pay for it and overtype this amount n the Price field and click the Add button.
When all titles from this customer have been entered, click the Finished button. The total price will be displayed. Click the Finish button again to open the Tender Type window. Select the Tender Type and click OK.

Step 4:
Print the goods received note for the BUYB supplier and then post the stock and print the barcodes as when receiving standard stock. Cover the existing barcodes with the new 988 barcode labels. The labels will display the median price. As the sell price will vary with the condition of the book, you will need to strike through the price on the barcode label and write the appropriate sell price beside it..

Selling second hand titles
When second hand books are sold, the only difference is that you may need to overtype the sell price when it varies from the median.

Reconciling buy backs and sales
The end of day sales reports will detail buy back operations as follows:

All Sales Report
If you created buy back departments, sales of second hand titles will be reported under them.

Cashbook	
Titles purchased through the Buy back facility will display as tender type Second Hand Buy Back. They are "Cash Out" figures i.e. debt entries which are taken off the normal "Cash In" transactions so you can balance the cash drawer.


.topic 2290
EDI invoices are electronic copies of your suppliers' invoices that you would normally receive on paper. The advantage of soft copy invoices is that you can receive your stock without keying the data. This saves time and improves accuracy.

To use Bookscan's EDI facilities you must have a Pacstream account. See the EDI chapter for details. Once you have signed up for the Pacstream service, many of your suppliers will forward their invoices to you via Pacstream. Bookscan e-Comms will interface with Pacstream and download the invoices. The EDI invoices can be viewed in the EDI invoices window which is opened from the main menu: Purchasing/ Incoming/ EDI Invoices.



The upper (invoice) browser lists the header information for all outstanding EDI invoices. The lower (lines) browser displays the lines of the invoice selected in the upper browser.

Processing an EDI invoice receipt
·	Select the invoice in the Invoice browser.
·	Click on the Scan Stock (F11) button to open the pop up scanning window.
·	Scan the barcode of the stock. Change the quantity if required. (Alternatively have the Singles (F7) checkbox ticked and just scan the barcode of each product).
·	If you receive more than the invoiced amount of any product, the program will prompt you before you save the line. If you scan a product not on the invoce, the program will display a warning message.
·	To edit the quantities or to set stock as received damaged etc, double click on the either the Qty, Rec or To Go column in the lower browser. The following window will open where you can edit the quantities.

·	As a shortcut, you can select "Receive all this invoice" from the lower browser's context menu. All lines of the sleected order will be set to the invoice quantity.
·	If you don't want to enter the stock at all, just tick the box marked "Don't scan stock", then click the Receive (F8) button.
·	Once all items are received, click the Receive (F8) button. The receipt will be loaded into the received stock file.
·	The program will offer to open the Receiving window where you can post the receipt.
·	Click OK and the Receiving window will open in list view with your new receipt loaded.
·	Make any amendments here, print the GRN and post into stock as usual.



Identifying suppliers
Pacstream uses SANs to uniquely identify suppliers. When Bookscan e-Comms is retrieving the invoice from Pacstream, it matches the SAN with SANs stored in the supplier master. Where a match is made the supplier code is displayed in the Supp column of the Invoice browser. Where you haven't yet set the SAN against the supplier, this column will display '****'.



To correct this, identify the supplier concerned, open the Supplier Master window and enter the SAN there. Then use the right mouse to open context menu in the EDI Invoices window and select 'Update suppliers', as shown in the diagram above. The Supp column will now display the supplier code and you can now process that invoice.

Setting prices
In the lines browser, the 'Cost' and 'Retail' columns are the prices quoted on the supplier invoice. The Sell column displays your sell price from the master file. Whether the program automatically updates your sell price when you post your receipt into stock, depends on the setting of this supplier's 'No auto calculations' flag.
Whether you set that flag will depend on your work practices:
·	If you have set your sell prices already, set the supplier's 'No auto calculations' flag to true.
·	If you are setting prices as you receive the stock, set the flag to false and set the appropriate pricing calculation. This will cause the sell price to be calculated when the EDI invoice is loaded into the Receiving window.

Unknown product codes
If a product on an EDI invoice is not in your database, the Title column will display in the lines browser as "Item not on master file". You will need to add the title to the database to be able to receive it.



To add the product, use the right mouse to open context menu in the EDI Invoices window and select 'Add selected ISBNs', as shown in the diagram above. The program will first search the archive, and if it finds the product there re-load it into the master. Next the program will do a web search (Title Page, Nielsen Bookdata or Global BIP) to find the title. Alternatively you can just add the title manually through the New Title window.

Viewing Processed EDI Invoices

You can view processed EDI invoices by ticking the Show Processed checkbox on the EDI Invoices window. When the box is ticked the browsers will display both processed and unprocessed invoices. Processed invoices will display a tick in the 'P' Column.


.topic 2610
Your receipts from suppliers can be viewed in the Good Inwards History window. To open it, select Purchasing/Incoming/Receiving history from the main menu. Double click on a line to edit the invoice details of any transaction.


.topic 1333
RETURNS TO SUPPLIERS

There are two situations where returns are done:

No fault returns
This is where stock has been supplied as "sale or return" and it is now time to return it. You can have the system generate a draft returns list of all such returnable stock. The system will automatically allocate the original invoice numbers and cost prices to each item on the Draft Returns List.

Other returns	
These are where stock has been either:
·	wrongly supplied,
·	supplied damaged,
·	supplied with faulty printing or binding,
·	superseded by a new edition or
·	recalled by the supplier.
These returns will need to be added manually to the draft returns list. You will need to allocate original invoice number to each item as you add it to the draft.

Returns to supplier - procedures summary

Generate the draft list
Create a list of draft returns in the Returns Generation window.

Groom the draft
Manually groom this draft returns list in the Draft returns window.

Pick the stock
Print the draft returns list for each supplier and use this to pick the stock.
Then edit the draft for items not found or in unreturnable condition.

Add other returns
Add wrongly supplied, damaged or faulty stock to the draft.

Returns authorisation
Print a Returns Authorisation Request for each supplier.

Return stock and post returns
When authority to return the stock is received, re-edit the draft, if necessary.
Post the return. This removes the items from stock and a credit claim is generated.
Return the stock with the credit claim to the supplier.


	Details




.topic 1150

Generate a draft returns list
Open the Returns Generation window from the main menu: Purchasing/ Returns/Add-Change & select Regenerate F9.



You can choose to generate returns by Department, Supplier or Imprint. The default is by Supplier.
Generating returns by department.
This is a way of reducing your stock holding within a certain area. It is not a routine, regular procedure like normal monthly returns.
For example, if you had built up your children's area for Children's Book Week, you may now want to reduce stock to more normal levels afterwards.
Generating returns by supplier or imprint
These are the options to set to do your regular no fault returns. It is imperative that this is done routinely to ensure that old stock does not become non-returnable and that your stock holding does become not become excessive.
Select departments, suppliers or imprints
Select one of departments, suppliers or imprints in the 'Select by' combo box. The list box on the right of the window will then display a corresponding list of departments, suppliers or imprints.
Select the suppliers (or imprints or departments) in the list box. To make multiple selections, hold the Control key down while clicking on further suppliers etc. Click the Select F12 Generate to create a list.
 Setting the returns parameters
There are two different methods that can be use in the generation:
Generate from First Invoice
Generate from Last Invoice

The minimum and maximum number of days since Last/First invoice would normally be set to the supplier's terms.
For example, if the supplier's terms were that stock must be kept for three months, but can only be returned up to six months after supply, then set the parameters as follows:

Minimum days 	90
Maximum days	180

The example applies to both options however it depends on which invoices (the oldest invoice date or the last invoice date) that the supplier allows to be used in calculating eligibility for return.

Select which method the supplier selected.
Generate from First Invoice: is used for those suppliers whose Sale or Returns condition (SOR) applies from date of publication (as the first invoice is close to the pub.date).
Generate from Last Invoice: is used for those suppliers who will allow returns after 3 months up to a specified period eg 180 days (6 months) from when the stock was invoiced, irrespective of publication date.
	
Title Options
Set the following flags:

Ignore sale or return flag	
This refers to the Firm Sale flag on the title master.  If you are confident of the accuracy of your title master leave this unchecked. Then only returnable titles will appear in the draft. In many shops, where various people have done data entry, the Firm Sale flag may be unreliable. In this case check the box to ignore the flag. This means that any non-returnable lines will have to be separated out manually.

Include consignment titles (if enabled)
This, when ticked, will allow consignment titles to be included in the returns list that will be generated.

Include ordinary titles
This is the default criteria selected and refers to all titles except consignment, firm sale or stock on hold.

Delete existing hold titles	
This refers to any titles which may be on hold in the Draft Returns Lists. Depending on your practices, there may be say, damaged stock on hold awaiting returns authorisation. In that case leave the box unchecked, as you don't want these removed from the draft.

Note: any items in the draft returns file which are not 'on hold', will be deleted from the file when you re-generate.  If there is anything in files you want kept there, make sure its 'hold' flag is set to true before re-generating.

Generate list
Click the Generate button to generate the Draft Returns List. Alternatively, click the Cancel button, or press the Escape key, to exit.
After generation, all stock lines that meet the criteria will be placed into Draft Returns, where you can check,process and post them.

Allocation of invoice numbers
Each time you receive goods from a supplier, the invoice number, invoice date and the cost price at the time of receiving are stored in the receiving history database.

When you generate returns, the system first finds what stock is available to return, and adds them to the returns draft.

It then allocates the original supplier invoice numbers against each title being returned.

It is assumed that the oldest copies receive first and so allocates the invoice number in reverse date order. Where the quantity being returned exceeds the quantity on the first relevant invoice for the date period, the excess is allocated to the next invoice and so on until all returns have an allocated invoice number.

For example, say you are returning stock of a title where you received a delivery of four in March and a further four in April. Say you sold two and wanted to return the six unsold copies. The system will return four copies against the March invoice and two against the April invoice.

The browser of the Draft Returns window will show two lines, one for each invoice number quoted.


Draft Returns List
The draft returns list is used to pick the stock from the shelves. From the main menu, select Purchasing/Returns/Reports and then click on Draft. The Draft Returns Parameters window will open.



Set your parameters
Optionally check or uncheck the 'Include hold titles'.
Uncheck the 'Include authorisation request' and Email Request boxes.
Select the supplier or tick the 'For all suppliers' check box.

Click the OK button to run the report.

This report displays the titles listed by Department. After printing the report, pick the stock from the shelves and then edit the draft (see the instructions in the next section) for items not found or in unreturnable condition.

Note: You can choose to leave this stock on the shelf until the Returns Authorisation is received from the supplier, then print this list and pick what they approve.

Draft Returns window
The Draft Returns window is where you process your returns to supplier. Open the window from the main menu: Purchasing/ Returns/ Add-Change.




View all draft returns
If the Supplier Filter checkbox is not ticked, all draft returns will display in the browser. As you highlight a line in the browser, the details of that line's supplier are displayed in the upper part of the window. To filter on that supplier, tick the Supplier Filter (F6) checkbox.

Filter by supplier
Alternatively to filter on a supplier, select the supplier in the combo box. The draft returns list for that supplier will be displayed in the browser. Use can use the F3 button to open the Supplier search window.

Find suppliers with draft returns
To view a list of all suppliers with draft returns, click the Draft Suppliers (F4) button. A pop up window will display listing all suppliers with items in the draft. Select the supplier by highlighting it in the pop up list and clicking the OK button (or just double click on the supplier) and the new supplier's draft returns will display in the browser.

Other filters
To further filter the lines displayed, you can select, in the Other filters combo box:
Invoice generated titles only: display only titles falling within the suppliers return parameters.
Titles added from Title Master context (right mouse) menu 'Add to Draft Returns' option.
Operator titles only: display only those titles added manually, rather than by the generation process.
Returns unheld titles only. Titles generate on hold in the draft and are released to process on a return.

Adding a line to the draft
You can manually add a line to the draft. Just scan (or type) the barcode into the Add Line field. If the item is already in the draft, you will be asked whether to go to the existing record or to add a new line. If adding the new line, the Edit Draft Return window will open where you can enter the number to be returned, select the original invoice and amend other settings: see the Edit Draft Return window section below.
Alternatively, to add a new line, click on the F2 button or open the context(right mouse) menu and select 'Add new line'. The title master and Title Find windows will open where you can select the required product.

Deleting a line from the draft
To delete lines from the draft, select them in the browser and press the Delete key (or open the context (right mouse) menu and select 'Delete'

Draft returns browser

Print Draft: is an alternate means of  printing RAs and picking lists (instead of going to the tool bar printer icon).

Items on hold

Also see context (right mouse) menu options.

Posting

Edit Draft Return window
To open the Edit Draft Return window, double click on a line in the browser of the Draft returns window. Here you can view the history of the title and enter the number to be returned, select the original invoice and amend other settings.



Editable fields
Draft returns fields
?	Qty: quantity being returned (can not exceed On Hand qty)
?	Hold (F6): toggle the draft return line on and off hold.
?	Consignment (F4): (if enabled) set the line as a consignment stock return.
?	Return reason: select a return reason from the drop down box. (Click the Repeat (F3) button to use the last reason used. Click the None (F7) button the clear the Return Reason field).
Note: Returns Reasons can be added/deleted and amended to ebility via Tables/Authority Tables/Returns Reasons. Returns reasons are primarily used to identify damaged, wrong supplied etc. SOR returns do not have to be labeled line by line in this manner.

Invoice details
When generating a draft returns list, the program will allocate the original supplier voice for each line of the draft. You can edit this invoice number here. While you can overtype the invoice number and date, it is easier to find the invoice in the Receiving History browser at the top right of the window. Double click on the browser line. The invoice number, invoice date and invoice cost price fields will be overwritten.

Master fields
You can change some title master fields here
Minimum stock
Maximum stock
Firm sale
Title master comment

Information fields
The various fields on this window will give you the data you need to decide on the quantity of stock to return. Most of the fields are self explanatory except the sales history numbers displayed at the bottom of the window:
The upper line is the monthly sales quantity for the previous 13 months.
The bottom line is the sales quantity for the last 12 sales periods, displayed from the left. These periods, designated in the system set up, are usually set at seven days.


Printing a returns authorisation request
After preparing your draft list of returns, you will need to print a returns authorisation request for each supplier.  This will list all the titles to be returned to the supplier, the return reason and the original invoice numbers, dates and pricing. You can have e-Comms email the requests to your suppliers if your supplier is setup with an email address and defined for EDI Pacstream or email. Pacstream does not support sending RAs but if there is an email address Bookscan will send these via email.

Open the Draft Returns Report Parameters window from the main menu:
Purchasing/ Returns/ Reports/ Draft.




Set your parameters
Uncheck the 'Include hold titles'.
Check the 'Include authorisation request'
Optionally check or uncheck the Email Request and Print Request boxes.
