Click the OK button to run the report. If you select the email option, the requests will be emailed to the suppliers if their email address is set in the supplier master, when
e-Comms runs.

Finalising your draft returns
When you have received your returns authorisation from your supplier, if you have not already picked your stock edit your draft returns for lines which the supplier disallowed the return or where the stock has been sold in the meantime. This is done ion the Draft turns window (see the Draft Returns window section above).
You can then print a list for picking the stock as per section above 'Picking list for Returns' and pick stock. You may have to edit the list again if items can not be found.
Once you have your list completed you can use Context (right mouse)menu in browse list to "Value" the draft. This will value unheld items at cost , showing GST , number of items etc for you to verify your action with the RA before posting.
Returns are posted in the Draft Returns window. This will create a credit claim on the supplier.

Posting returns
Open the window from the main menu: Purchasing/ Returns/Add/Change.
Select the supplier on the combo box.
Lines that are not to be included in the credit claim should be set to 'On hold' or deleted.
Click on the Post (F12) button. The Post Returns window will open.




Where applicable, enter the returns authorisation number, consignment note number and carrier, and any freight to be charged to the supplier.
If you wish the program to set the minimum stock level for the products in the claim to zero, tick the 'Zero minimum stock (F5)' check box.  This will prevent the items being added to the draft for re-ordering, if you are using 'Min Stock ordering' and haven't returned all the stock. Be careful using this function if you are doing damaged goods type returns as it will stop the item re-ordering un the future.
You can choose to have the program email the credit claims (if the supplier's email has been set in the supplier master).

Note: It is probably more appropriate to print and send the credit claim with the goods as the Supplier will not process the claim until the goods are received. Set the flag to print the claim. You should always retain a copy of a credit claim to ensure it is followed up with the Supplier (administration).

Click the OK (F12) button to create the claim.



Effects of posting
Posting has the following effects:
A credit claim is created (optionally, emailed to the supplier by Ebility e-Comms).
The stock on hand in the title master is reduced by the quantity of the claim.
The posted lines in draft returns are deleted.
The stock movement is added to the stock history file. This can be viewed on the Movement History tab of the title master.
The credit claim is stored in the returns history files from where it can be exported to the MYOB accounts package.
Optionally the minimum stock level in the title master is set to zero.
The credit claim is posted into the creditor transaction file (see diagram below)




Notes on the Creditors window:
·	The credit claim is highlighted in green in the browser.
·	The credit claim number ('7889') displays in the Reference column.
·	The returns authorisation number displays in the Description column.

.topic 2820


This is the window where you can enquire on past Credit Claims and edit some of the details if there is a variance in what was generated by the claim & what the supplier grants you in credit.

You can edit details in the header by clicking on the Edit Header(F7) button ot by pressing [F7].



Here you can edit the Return Date, Return Authorisation number, Consignment note number, Carrier details and Freight amount.

You can also double click on a line to edit, the Date, Invoice number, Quantity returned, Invoice cost of the item, Tax rate, Retail price, Return reason and Comment fields.

.topic 2220
Note this area is only accessible for distributor/publisher configurations. It is not relevant for retailers. The procedures are written for the book and music industry, but can be simply adapted for any industry.

Book publishers and distributors usually sell their new titles to bookshops on a sale or return basis. The bookshops are able to return unsold stock only after a certain period since the release date (say, 90 days) and before a cut off date (say, 270 days after the release date). At some stage, say 180 days after the release date, the title is no longer considered new and moves to the backlist. Bookshop orders for backlist titles will have a different return rule, or may be non-returnable.

The Bookscan program allows you set up your products to handle this customer returns policy model or for variations on it.

We also need to cope with customer returns for other reasons:
·	wrongly supplied,
·	supplied damaged,
·	supplied with faulty printing or binding,
·	superseded by a new edition.

Customer returns - procedures summary

Setting up your customer returns policy
The customer returns policy is set on the Options tab of System Options.


Setting a title's returns status
As titles are created in the New Title window, users should set the Returns Status to either:
·	Sale or return
·	Backlist
·	No returns.
Where the return status is set as 'not set', it is treated the same as backlist titles.

The returns status can be amended in the Extra Fields tab of the Title Master window.

Globally managing return statuses
You can globally manage the return status of your titles in the RA Global Updates window.

Invoicing
As you invoice a customer's order, the returns status of each title at the time of invoice (or picking slip) creation is recorded against the invoice line. When a customer later sends in his returns authorisation request to return this product, it is the return status recorded in the invoice file that determines its eligibility for return, not the title current returns status. This allows you to alter the returns status of a product as it ages from 'sale or return' to backlist.

Returns authorisation
Returns authorisation requests from customers are processed in the Returns Authorisation window. As you enter each line of the request, the product's eligibility for return to checked against the returns policy. The program will advise you where a return is ineligible and you will have the choice of declining the return or authorizing it anyway.
Return request for other reasons such as 'received damaged' can also be processed.
Where the customer does not quote an original invoice number, the program will display all eligible invoices, allowing the operator to choose.
At the end of processing, the program will produce a returns authorisation form and the authorisation will be recorded against the original invoice and the title.
The program will prevent operators from authorising the return of an invoiced line more than once.

Processing credits
When authorised products have been returned to you, you can process the credits without re-keying. In the Order Entry window, on credit mode, you can append the RA into the credit note, amend it as required and post the credit. The credit will be recorded against the original invoice in the invoice lines table, preventing the chance of crediting the item twice.

Customer returns – detailed procedures

Setting up your customer returns policy
The customer returns policy is set on the Options tab of System Options.



The program allows two invoice date ranges:
·	'sale or return' items (new releases),
·	'other' (or backlist) items.
To allow returns on sale or return items, tick the checkbox. Then set the minimum number of days and the maximum number of days.
Similarly, set up returns on other items. If returns are not allowed other than on new releases, leave the second checkbox unticked.
You can also set the number of days after which an RA expires.

Setting a title's returns status
Each title will have one of four return statuses:
·	Sale or return
·	Backlist
·	No returns
·	Not set
Where the return status is set as 'not set', it is treated the same as backlist titles.

When an invoice is created, the current return status of each title is recorded against the invoice line in the invoice table. When a returns authorisation request is processed, it is this return status in the invoice table that is used to check whether a product is eligible for return, not the title's current return status.

The Returns Status is set using the Returns Status combo box in the New Title window and the Extra fields tab of the Title master.



Where the return status is set as 'not set', it is treated the same as a backlist title.

Globally managing return status
As new releases age, you will need to change their returns status from 'new release to 'backlist'. You could just change the setting in the Title Master window. Alternatively you can globally manage the return status of your titles in the RA Global Updates window. The RA Global Updates window is opened from the main menu: Purchasing/ Returns/ Returns authorisation/ Global updates.



When the window opens it will display all new release titles. To change the returns status of a line in the browser, select the new return status from the radio buttons on the left of the window. Now double click on the Ret column of the lines you wish to change. As you double click on a line, its return status will change and it will disappear from the browser.
Faster is to select all the lines in the browser you wish to change, open the context menu (by clicking the right mouse) and select 'Set selected to 'backlist'' (or 'not set' or 'firm sale') from the menu.

Using categories
Using Bookscan categories is a useful way to manage your new releases. Set each month's new release titles to a new Bookscan category – called say,  DEC08, JAN09, FEB09, MAR09 etc. As a month's new releases are ready to be removed to the backlist, you can load then into the RA Global Update window by selecting the category using the Load Category combo box in the top left of the window. The browser will fill all the new release titles from that month and you can easily change the status to 'backlist' as outlined above.

Returns authorisation
Returns authorisation requests from customers are processed in the Returns Authorisation window which is opened from the main menu: Purchasing/ Returns/ Returns authorisation/ Returns authorisation.



RA Enquiries
Select a customer in the customer field and the window will display:
·	A summary of the customer's outstanding RAs,
·	Their account summary,
·	Two browsers showing their current RAs.

Actions
·	Click on an RA in the top browser to display its lines in the lower browser.
·	You can view their old RAs in the browser by clicking the Show Old (F4) button.
·	If the customer has many RAs, you can type the RA number into the 'Find RA' field and press the enter key, to display that transaction.
·	Clicking the Expand Browser (F7) button will expand the lower browser to show more details.
·	To edit the lines of an RA, double click on it in either browser. The window will change to edit mode. See the Processing returns authorisation requests section below.
·	Using the context menu (shown open in the diagram above) to reprint, cancel or delete selected RAs or RA lines. You can also purge old RAs from here.
Note: to cancel an RA is flag it as 'no longer valid'. The delete it is to remove the RA completely (and irretrievably) from the RA table.
·	Reprint an RA form by clicking the Print RAs (F6) button. The RA printing parameters window will open, where you can reprint RA by setting a variety of criteria.


		
Processing returns authorisation requests
With a customer selected in the Returns Authorisation window, click on the New Authorisation (F12) button. The window will now display in RA input mode.



·	Enter the barcode of the first line of the RA request. The title details will display jus above the browser.
·	Enter the customer reference number (optional). This number will repeat for subsequent lines.
·	The RA quantity will default to '1'. Amend the quantity if required.
·	Enter the original invoice number which your customer should have provided. If this hasn't been provided, leave the field blank.
·	Select the return reason. The default is 'Sale or return'. Options are:
o	Sale or return – returning a product because they could not sell it.
o	Damaged - the product arrived damaged.
o	Faulty - faultily manufactured
o	Picking error - the correct product was invoiced but the wrong product despatched.
o	Order error – the wrong product was invoiced.
·	Enter any comments you wish e.g. 'Return title page only'.
·	Click on the Save (F12) button.

If you have entered the invoice number and the return reason is 'Sale or return', the program will check whether the line is eligible for return. If it is eligible, the line is saved to the browser, the edit line will clear and you can enter the next line or finalise the RA.

Invoice selection window
If you have not entered an invoice number or the invoice is not in the eligible date range or the return status of the product was set to 'no returns', the program will open the Invoice Selection window.



The left browser shows all this customer's invoices that have not been credited. Invoices outside of the allowed date range are show in yellow. Eligible lines are white.
The browser on the right shows the invoices you (or the program) have selected for this RA. If you had not entered an invoice number in the edit line, the program will select the oldest invoice (or invoices) for you.
Double click on a line in the right browser to de-select it; the line will move to the left browser.
Double click on a line in the left browser to select it and move it to the right browser.

Quantity fields
·	Requested – shows the original RA request quantity.
·	Allow – displays the total quantity for which invoices have been selected in the right browser.
·	Refused - displays the total quantity you have refused to accept.
·	To go – the requested quantity less the quantities allowed and refused.
To save the line, the To Go quantity must be zero.

The following diagram shows an RA where only part of the request has been allowed:



The customer wants to return 5 copies of this title. Invoices #12300 to #12303 are within the date range but #12359 is not. So the program has selected the four eligible invoices and has shown '4' in the Allow field and '1' in the To Go field.
The operator has clicked on the Refuse These (F7) button, causing the program to set the Refused field to '1' and the To Go field to zero. Because the Refused field is not zero, the operator must choose a refusal reason in the combo box before the line can be saved.

Invoice selection window- other features
·	Clicking the Categories (F6) button will display a pop up window listing all categories this title belongs to.
·	Clicking the Histories (F7) button will display a pop up window listing a complete list of all the customer's invoices for this title.
·	Clicking the Clear Invoices (F9) button will clear the 'selected invoices' browser.
·	For picking errors you will need to enter the barcode of the product that was actually sent. This will allow the warehouse to process the return properly.


Returns Authorisation window - Other features
Not our product
Sometimes a customer's RA request includes a product which you do not supply and so is not in your product master file. In that instance, as the operator enters the product code into the barcode field, a dialogue window will open:



Here you can enter the title and quantity. Clicking Save will add a refusal line to the browser with a refuse reason of "not our publication".

Appending invoices and RAs
If a customer is returning all of an invoice (perhaps an order was invoiced to the wrong customer), you quickly create an RA by appending the original invoice. Select the customer in the Returns Authorisation window, click on the Append Invoice (F8) button. A drop down list will be displayed of that customer's invoices. Select the invoice from the list and all the lines of the invoice will be copied into the browser. Amend any line you by double clicking it in the browser to load it into the edit line. Then finalise the RA in the usual.
You can similarly append an existing RA for a customer by clicking the Append RA (F9) button.

Finalising an RA
After all the lines have been entered, click on the Finalise (F12) button. This will open the RA Finalise window.



Options
·	You can select whether to print the RA form now. The program allows you to print by date range, filtered on the operator name, so you can batch print when you are ready to.
·	The expiry date is set using the default number of days entered in System options. You can change the expiry date for this RA by selecting a new date or overtyping the Days field.
·	You can set a contact to display on the RA form.
·	In the Saved Messages combo box, you can select one of your saved RA messages to display on the RA form. The message will display in the multi-line field. See the RA messages section below for creating new messages.
Click on the Post (F12) button to create the RA.


RA messages
RA messages which can be appended to your RA form are intended for you to write your standard returns conditions etc. You can add or edit these messages in the Saved Messages window which is opened from the main menu: Tables/ Authority tables/ Saved messages.



To add a message:
·	Click on the Add Message (F3) button. The header and the message fields will clear.
·	Type the name of the message into the header field. E.g. 'Standard RA conditions'.
·	Type the message in to the multi-lone field. The message can be of any length but remember that there needs to be enough room on the RA form to print it.
·	Click on the Save Message (F3) button to save the message.

Other features
·	Use the Next (F9) and Previous (F8) buttons to scroll through the saved messages.
·	You can hide a message so it will temporarily not be available to operators. This could be handy for say, Christmas messages which you can then 'unhide' in December.
·	Ticking the Show Hidden (F7) checkbox will allow you to view hidden messages.

Title master
In distributor/publisher configurations, the Details tab of the Title Master window has a returns authorisation field. This displays the number of current, re-sellable RAs for the product. Clicking the corresponding RA button will open the RA tab which has a browser displaying the all current RAs for this product.
Note: that the RA total on the details tab is a total of RAs which can be returned to stock. It does not include RAs for damaged or faulty stock.

Integrity check
Running the Stock Fields option in the Integrity Check window will recalculate RA totals on the title master. Remember that only the total of re-sellable stock will be displayed here. Faulty or damaged goods are not counted.

Processing credits
When authorised products have been returned to you, you can process the credits without re-keying. Open the Order Entry window from the main menu: Sales/ Invoice/ Invoice, credit, approval entry.
·	Select the customer in the Customer field.
·	Click on the Credit Note (F5) radio button to switch to credit mode.
·	Click the Save F12 button to open the processing view.
·	Click on the Append (F8) button and select an RA from the drop down box.



A pop up window will be displayed showing the lines of the RA. Deselect any lines you will not be crediting. You can also double click on the Qty column to overwrite a quantity.
·	Click the OK button and the RA will append to the browser.
In the diagram above, the operator has already appended the three lines of RA #2 and is in the process of appending RA # 3.
·	Finalise you credit not in the usual way.


.topic 1170

From the main menu, select Purchasing/Transfers/Transfers Out. The Transfers Out window will open.

Processing transfers out
Select the branch to which you wish to transfer stock in the Transfer to combo box.
Enter a reference number in the PO number field, if you wish, or leave as zero.
Select the method by which you wish to send the transfer - either EDI (via e-Comms) or by Disk.
Scan the barcode (or type in the ISBN)
Enter the Quantity to be transferred.
Click Save Line (F12).
To edit a line you have entered, double click on the line in the browser to open it in the edit line.
When all lines have been entered click Process Transfer (F12).
If sending via disk, a dialogue box will open. In the Data Path field select the path of the folder to save the transfer file to - this is a standard Windows selection field. Click on the button at the end of the field and browse to the transfer file folder (ie. on USB thumb drive), to select it.

Transfer History
To display completed tranfers, click on the Show History (F11) button. The browser will display all completed transfers out.

Filtering the transfers
Radio button  To all locations will display all transfers.
Radio button To selected location will allow you display only transfers to the selected branch. This will enble the Select order no. combo box where you can select a single transfer by transfer number.

Click on the End History (F11) button to end displaying history.


		

.topic 1160

Transfers inwards can arrive from two sources - either via e-Comms or via  a disk or network (ie. a USB flash drive).

Via e-Comms
When you run e-Comms, transfers are automatically loaded into the transfer inwards window where they can be processed as described in Step 3: Processing Transfers below:

Via disk
For transfers received via disk or network, you need to use the Import Transfers from Disk window. From this window transfers are  loaded into the transfer inwards window where they can be processed as described in Processing Transfers below:

From the main menu select Purchasing/Transfers/Import Transfers from Disk.
The Import Transfers from Disk window will open.
If the transfer files are on a USB drive, place it into a USB port.
In the Data Path field select the path of the folder containing the transfer inwards files - this is a standard Windows selection field. Click on the button at the end of the field and browse to the transfer file folder to select it.  (The folder, not the files.)
Click the Process Transfers (F12) button to load the transfer files from the folder into the Transfer Inwards window.

Processing Transfers

Inwards transfers are processed in the Transfers In window.

Step 1:
From the main menu, select Purchasing/Transfers/Transfers In. This will open the Transfers Inwards window. Unprocessed inwards transfers are  displayed in the browser.

Filtering the transfers
Radio button  From all locations will display all unprocessed transfers.
Radio button From selected location will allow you display only transfers from the selected branch. This will enble the Select order no. combo box where you can select a single transfer by transfer number.

History
Click on the Show History (F11) button to display processed (and partially processed) transfers in the browsers. Click the same button to cancel history view.

Step 2: Code changes	
Note that this refers to transfers via disk only.
Where a product code has been changed at the sending store but not at the receiving store, the line in the browser will be highlighted yellow. If you wish to update the product code locally, slecet the line in the browser and open the context menu (right mouse click) and select Code Change.

Step 3: Processing transfers

If you wish, filter the transfers you wish to process as in step 1.
Click on the Begin Processing (F12) button. The window will change colour and only only outstanding quantities will be displayed in the browser.
Double click on the line in the browser to  open the Edit Received Quantities window.
Enter the quantity to be received in the Received field.
Click on one of the Save buttons. The received and not received quantites are now displayed in the browser.
To process the transfer, click on the Process Transfers (F12) button.
The transfers will then be saved to the Receiving List against the Transfer supplier, code !TRA. Open the Receiving window, click the List button and post the receipts as you would for good inwards.

Shortcut
To receive all transfers displayed in the browser:

Click on the Begin Processing (F12) button.
Open the context menu (right mouse click) and select Receive all stock. This will fill the Rec column in the browser with the total quantity.
Click on the Process Transfers (F12) button.



.topic 2350

This window is located on the main menu in Purchasing -> Transfers -> Import From Disk.
This section allows you to manually load in stock transfers and title synchronisation files.



There are two types of import, as mentioned above, select from the two options of;
Process transfer files: Stock transfer files created using the 'by disk' method from the senders end.(will be 2 files, update(transfer number).DBF and update(transfer number).FPT )
Title synchronisation: Sync files containing product details that have been exported from any store. (Will be 2 files, tsupdate(number).DBF and tsupdate(number).FPT)

Append New Titles Only
When doing a Title synchronisation, you have the added option to only add titles that you dont already have in your system, otherwise it will add new and update the existing based on your settings for update.

To setup what does get changed when it does an update, from the main menu open Utilities -> System Options -> Stores Tab (shown below)
The options are listed below under the On Title Synchronisation section. If the option is checked, it will not update that filed on Title Synchronisation.



Next (while still in the Title Synchronisation window) click on the icon   to select the folder where the files you wish to import are saved.

Note: It is assumed that you have either received the files on a thumbdrive or saved the attachments from an email that was sent through to your site and that these files have been saved in the folder you will be selecting.


Click on the folder where the file are saved, then Click the OK button.

Next click the Proceed button, which will then check for any files that are of the type selected (Stock Transfer files and Synchronisation files)


This will then process the files based on your settings and the type selected. It will then show a message when done indicating what has been done. This example shows what a Title Synchronisation file will look like. On the main window (behind this message) you will see on the right hand side of the window the number of files it has processed, e.g. 1 synchronisation files.

Click OK button when done (it is already complete, but to clear the message to finish)

.topic 2340

This section is useful for multiple site stores where you wish to send the new titles entered on your site in a file to be loaded in at the other site. This can prevent double keying of products.

From the main menu, select Purchasing->Transfers->Title Synchronisation.


By default, as shown in the image above, will export all new products that have been added, since this was last run. If this has not been run before, it will export most of the products in your Title Master. It will also put the files into a zip archive (which makes it smaller for sending).

Settings for export

You can make changes to exclude any of the following (By using it to pick only what you wish to Include);

Department
Status
Suppliers
Imprints

Click on the button, e.g. Status.

The window that displays will show on the left-hand side All of the option, in this case Status and on the right-hand side what you wish to use for inclusion. (what remains on the left will be excluded)

Simply double click on items on the left-hand side and they will move to the right-hand side.

Click Save button when done.


When it returns to the main window, it will look like this.

This can be done in combination with all 4 of the options.

Product Export Count
To know how many products will be exported, before doing so, simply click the Get Title Count button, this will then display a message indicating the number of titles that will be exported if you save now.

Next Click Create File button when happy to save.



Next a window will appear to select the path to save the files to. Click this icon   to select the location (for advanced users, you can simply type the full path name in this box instead). If you have run this before, the location it was saved last time will show in this window, if this is the case, its best you keep using the same location (else make a new folder shown in the image below).


Select the folder you wish to save in, then click OK button.



When happy with the path, click the OK button.



What it will then finally show is a message to indicate that the file has been created, its name ( in this case tsupdate32493.zip )and also the number of products, or records saved in this file.

Note: 	The files saved are named tsupdate(number).DBF and tsupdate(number).FPT. If you have the zip file option selected as mentioned above these two files will be saved within the zip file, which will be called tsupdate(number).zip.
	You will need to transfer the files manually for import at the other site, (email, CD or USB drive) if zipped.  You can have e-Comms transfer the files by leaving the Zip file option unselected & saving to the Transfer -> Toho folder in your database folder.  You will still need to use the Import from disk window to import the synchronisation file.  It will be located in the Transfer -> Fromho folder in your database folder at that location.

.topic 1190
You can group individual products together and sell them as kits (pack). Each kit will have its own unique product code that can be scanned at point of sale or in the Invoice Entry window.
You can choose to have the sales recorded against either the components of the kit or against the kit itself. The latter is the default. You can set kits sales to be recorded against the components in the Sales tab of System Options.
All kits have a binding of KI and a main supplier of ^KIT.

Kits are managed in the Kits window. From the main menu select Purchasing, then Kits.
The upper browser of the Kits window displays all the kits listed in the title master.
The lower browser shows the components of the selected kit in the upper browser.



 Creating a kit:
In the Kits window click on the New Kit (F4) button to open the New Master Record window.
Enter the details into each field. Fields marked with an asterisk are mandatory.
Note: that the binding and main supplier fields have been completed for you.
Click the OK button when done.
You will then be invited to add components to the kit. You can either add them now or do it later
Adding components to a kit:
You can add components to the currently selected kit in the Add item to kit box.
Scan the barcode of the component into the ISBN field (or use one of the standard shortcuts or click on the F2 title product search button).
After selecting the product a dialogue window will be displayed asking for the quantity of this component in each kit.
Enter this quantity and click OK and the components is added.
Note that if you have already assembled stock against a kit you will not be able to add further components to the kit.

Setting the kit price
You can edit the kit sell price in three places:
In the New Master Record window when creating the kit.
In the title master as you would with any other product.
In the Kits window. Here the current sell price is displayed for the current selected kit. Also displayed is the total cost and sell price of all the components. You can use these to assist you in setting a price. To edit the sell price click on the Set Kit Price (F5) button and enter the new sell price.
Assembling kits
You can assemble components into kits in the Kits window.
Doing this will reduce the on hand level of the components and proportionally increase the on hand quantity of the kit stock.
The "Able to make" field displays the number of kits you are able to assemble.
Enter up to this number in the Assemble field and then click on the Assemble (F6) button.
Disassembling kits
The Assembled field displays the number of kits that are currently assembled.
Enter up to this number in the Disassemble field and then click on the Disassemble (F7) button.
Editing a kit
To change the quantity of a component in a kit, select the component in the lower browser.
Right click on it to open the context (right mouse) menu and select Edit Item.
A dialog box will open where you can change the quantity.
Note:  that you can only do this when no stock is assembled for the kit.
Deleting a component from a kit
To remove a component from a kit, select the component in the lower browser.
Right click on it to open the context menu and select Delete Item.
Note:  that you can only do this when no stock is assembled for the kit.

Tells the EFTPOS communication software, (supplied by your EFTPOS vendor), to initiate a login with the paired pinpad.

Display EFTPOS utilities



EFTPOS settlements



Power Failure recovery

This is used when there has been a communication error between the PC & the EFTPOS pinpad.  It will resend the data for the transaction to the pinpad to complete the transaction.  If you have already manually run the transaction through the pinpad we recommend that you contact Barcode Solutions, and we will assist with clearing the stored data.  (This is done by opening the registry editor & clearing the data held in the Barcode Solutions \ LastEFTTrans entry.)
