If there is a deposit outstanding on the special order, you will be given the opportunity to refund it. If you do not wish to, ie customer defaulted, you can set the refund to zreo by overtyping the amount displayed

.topic 1270

You can create a draft special order for a title whose ISBN you have not yet been able to identify. This allows you to create a special order at the POS and then later, when you have more time, look up the ISBN.

Creating the draft special order
Open the special order create window and enter your customer in the usual way.
Click on the Unknown (F8) button (or enter an asterisk in the barcode field and then press the Tab or Enter key). A pop up window will be displayed where you can enter an approximation of the title and the author. The title field is a required field.



The program will insert an asterisk for the barcode and set the supplier to the miscellaneous supplier (code 'MISC'). You can change this to the actual supplier if you know it. To change it back to MISC, click the Unknown (F9) button.
You can mix known and unknown titles on a special order.
To reopen the Title/Author pop up window of a line, double click on the line in the browser.
After entering all the lines of the special order, save it in the usual way.

Processing draft special orders.
·	Open the draft special order window from the main menu: Sales/Specials/Replace.
·	The window will display all outstanding draft special orders in the browser.
·	Double click on the line you wish to process.
·	The line will be copied onto the Suspected Title and Suspected Author fields and the cursor will move to the Enter Replacement ISBN field.
·	Type in the replacement ISBN or click the F2 button to open the title master to search for the title. (If you have just added the ISBN to the database, or just looked it up, you can instead just click on the Recall button).



·	Once you have found the title, click the Replace button. It now becomes a normal special order.
·	If you already have stock of the new title, the Allocate from Stock window will open allowing you to allocate stock against this order.


.topic 1280

From the main menu, click on Sales, then point to Specials and then click on Print.

Customer reports

These produce Special Order Status Reports for customers.
These are letters addressed to customers listing the outstanding titles on their special orders.

All
The letter lists all special orders for a selected customer.
			 	
Single
Select a single special order.
Click on the    in the special order box and select the order number required  from the list displayed.
		
Category	
Report to all customers with outstanding special orders in a selected category.

Other Reports

Status  	
Creates a report in customer order, reporting outstanding special orders for titles with a selected print status (eg NYP).
	
All	
Produces a summary of all special orders.
Parameters:
	· Order  numbers greater than.
	· Report unsupplied items only option.
	· Sort by either customer or by special order number.

Not Found
Lists all special orders where the ISBN is unknown.

Overdue	
Reports on overdue special orders.
Parameters:
	· Start and finish dates.
	Click here for assistance with changing dates.
	· Standing orders.
	Select whether to include or exclude standing orders or whether to report on them exclusively.			
	· Supplier.
	To select multiple suppliers, hold the control key down while clicking on the required suppliers.
	Click the Select All button to report on all suppliers.


.topic 1290

Periodically you should remove old, completed special orders from the database:
·	Open the Purge Special Orders window from the main menu: Sales / Specials / Purge.
·	In the displayed window, select the date from which to purge filled special orders.
·	Click the OK button. You will be asked to confirm before the program will perform the deletions.
·	Only special orders which have been finalised will be deleted.


.topic 203

Open the Layby Enquiry window from the main menu: Sales / Laybys / Layby Enquire.

Displaying Laybys
You can search for laybys in three ways:

Layby number
Select the layby number.
A list of all products on that layby number will be displayed.

Customer
Select a customer in the Customer field in the usual way (i.e. enter the customer code, use back slash or other short cuts or use the F3 or Alt F3 buttons).
A list of all titles on layby for the customer will be displayed.

ISBN
Scan or type in the barcode in the ISBN field. All laybys for that product will be displayed.
Note that in this mode, you can't delete or make a payment on a layby.

Handy shortcut -   Double clicking on a line will switch to Layby Number mode with the browser displaying just the details of that layby number.
	

		    	
Options
Payments
·	In the browser, select therequired layby.
·	Click on the Make Payment (F7) button.
·	The Tendering window will open displaying the total amount owing in the Layby Deposit field.



·	If the customer if not paying the full you can overtype this amount.
·	If the customer is not paying in cash, select a tender type.
·	Enter the tender amount in the Amt Tendered field.
·	Click on OK (or press the Enter key) to complete the transaction.

Deleting
·	In the browse, select the required layby.
·	Click on the Delete (F4) button.
·	If there are outstanding payments on the layby, the Tendering window will open displaying the payment total as the amount to be refunded.
·	If not giving a full (or any) refund you can overtype this amount with the actual refund.
·	If not refunding in cash, select a tender type.
·	Click on OK (or press the Enter key). The Tendering window will close.
·	A pop up window, Cancellation Comment will now be displayed showing a default comment of the refund amount, cancellation date and operator.  Overtype or add to the comment if you wish and click OK or press the Enter key.



·	The cancellation is now complete. If there is cash refund for the customer, the Change window will now be displayed

Payment History
·	In the browser, select the required layby.
·	Click on the Payment History (F5) button.
·	A window will open listing each payment.



Handy shortcut -   Except in ISBN search mode, double clicking on a the line will have the browser will also open the history window.

Docket Reprint
·	In the browser, select the required layby.
·	Click on the Print Docket (F6) button.

Layby Archive
·	This allows the viewing of completed laybys for viewing purposes only.
·	Click Show Archive (F9) button.
·	To return to the current laybys click Show Current (F9).


Purge Laybys

This will remove all Finalised laybys that are older than the specified data

.topic 1320
In the Order Entry window you can process customer orders, creating:
·	invoices,
·	picking slips,
·	approvals (or inspection copies),
·	consignments outwards,
·	customer quotations,
·	pro forma invoices.

Orders can be entered by:
·	manually typing them in,
·	importing from your web site, (e-Web module)
·	importing from EDI invoice file, (wholesale)
·	appended from approvals, special orders, quotes and pro forma invoices.

Also in the window, you can also process customer returns and other credits. These credits can be:
·	manually entered,
·	appended from invoices (allowing you to easily reverse an invoice)
·	appended from returns authorisations.  (wholesale)
(Not all users have access to all these functions. It will depend on how your system is configured).

ORDER ENTRY WINDOW

The Order Entry window is opened from the main menu: Sales / Invoices / Invoice, Credit, Approval Entry.
Below is an outline of how to process customer orders

HEADER DETAILS

Step 1: Select the customer	
Select a customer in the Customer field in the usual way – enter the customer code in to the customer field, click the F3 button to open the customer search window or click the Alt F3 button to recall the last customer.
·	The customer's name and default details will be displayed.
·	Details on their account balance are displayed - current, 30, 60 and 90 days.
·	For the distributor module, a summary of the customer's current orders and backorders is displayed.
·	For tax exempt customers, 'Tax Exempt' will be displayed in a red field.
·	For customers on stop, 'Stop' will be displayed in a red field and you will be unable to proceed further.
·	If the customer has exceeded their credit limit, 'Limit Exceeded' will be displayed in a red field. You will still be able to create an invoice.
·	Customers who are not account customers will  will only be able to have Approvals and Quotations created for them unless the Mailorder flag is set in the Customer Master / Detail tab. When searching for a customer, account customers (debtors) are highlighted in aqua in the Customer drop down box.

Step 2: Transaction header details
Now enter the header details for the transaction:
Enter the customer order number (optional). Order numbers can also be entered line by line.
You can change the:
·	customer contact:  the combo box lists all of that customer's contacts,
·	sales rep: the second sales rep field is for use where you have separate reps for different product types (e.g. books and music),
·	sales terms
·	delivery address: You can use the arrow buttons to select alternate delivery addresses or you can add a new address,
·	header discount off the transaction total. (You can optionally enter a discount off the total value of the transaction. This can also be entered (or changed) in the footer window. If a discount is entered here then the running total displayed as you are entering the order lines will reflect the discount. If the discount is dependent on the value of the invoice, you would enter it in the footer window).
	
Step 3: Transaction type	
In the Transaction Type radio button group select the transaction type you require:
·	Standard invoice is used to process customers' standard orders. It is used to produce an invoice, picking slip or forward order.
·	Credit note is used for processing customer returns and correcting invoice errors.
·	Approval is for sending out goods on approval to customers.
·	Consignment outwards is available only with the Consignment module. It is used for sending goods on consignment to retailer customers.
·	Pro forma invoice will create a pro forma invoice. It will not reduce your stock levels.
·	Quotation will create a customer quotation. It will not reduce your stock levels.

Step 4: Transaction mode	
In the Transaction mode radio buttons group select the required option:

Invoices, approvals and consignment orders

Retail configuration
·	Stock adjusting: the transaction will reduce stock and create a standard invoice, approval advice or consignment advice.
·	Charge only:  (invoices only) the transaction will not reduce your stock levels but will create an invoice. It can be used to correct undercharges on an invoice.

Credit notes
·	Return to stock: the transaction reduce stock and create a standard invoice.

Distributor/publisher configuration
·	Picking slip will produce a picking slip for the warehouse (or back office) to process. The transaction will reduce your stock.
·	Already sent will create a standard invoice, approval advice or consignment advice and reduce the stock level.
·	Charge only: (invoices only) the invoice will not reduce your stock levels but will create an invoice. It can be used to correct undercharges on an invoice.
·	Forward order will create a picking slip to be processed at a future date. It will reduce your stock levels.

Credit notes
·	Return to stock: the transaction reduce stock and create a standard invoice.
·	No stock movement: the transaction will not reduce your stock levels. This option can be used to correct overcharges on an invoice or for crediting goods lost in transit.

Step 5: Saving/displaying the header
When you have finished with the header, click on the Save button (F12).
At any time you can return to the header by clicking on the Show Header button (F11).

Important option
You can quickly and easily batch invoice all your special orders here. See the Batch processing section of the Special Order chapter for details.

ENTERING THE ORDER

Step 6: Processing a line
·	In the ISBN field: scan the barcode or type in the ISBN. Alternatively you can use either one the various shortcut title searches, the F2 title search button or the Alt F2 button to recall the last barcode used.
·	The Order No field: for the first line this will display the order number entered in the header. If you enter a new order number, subsequent lines will default to this new number.
·	In the Order Qty box, enter the quantity ordered.

Retail configuration
·	In the Supplied box, enter the quantity you can supply.
·	Where their insufficient stock to fill the order line, you will be given the option to put the item on special order when you save the line to the browser. See below in the 'Backorder' section of 'Options'.

Distributor/publisher configuration
·	In the Supply box, the quantity you can supply will be displayed.
·	Any backordered quantity will be displayed in the B/Order field.
·	You can apply a discount against a single line here. (To apply the same discount for the whole sale, do it in step 11).
·	You can also add a Comment against the title here.
·	The running total of the transaction will display in the Order value field.
·	Click Save (F12) to save the line to the browser. Alternatively click Clear Line (Esc) to delete it.

Step 7: Displaying the footer window
Repeat step 7 for each line of the invoice. Click Post (F12) to open the footer window.

OPTIONS
 Editing
To edit any line you have saved, double click on the line in  the browser. The line will be copied into the input line in the header.
Overtype any changes you want to make and click on the Save button.
To delete a saved line, right click on it in the browser and select Delete from the drop down menu.

Free text
To add a message line to an order, type an asterisk ( * )  into the barcode field.  The free text line will appear. Type in your message and then click on the Save button (F12).

Backorders
Backordering is enabled by checking the Auto Backorder checkbox in the Copies tab of System Options.
When this flag is set, a special order (backorder) will be automatically created during invoice posting if the Supplied quantity is less than the Order quantity.  There is a option F7 to display the Status of the title on such lines – to give advice on the printed invoice copy and update the title master status. If you wish to use this Status option you should ensure the Status "Backorder" column is ticked on all status that you wish to backorder on (Tables/Authority Tables/Status window by editing the status and ticking "all backordering' or alternative putting cursor over the backorder field in the list against each title and double clicking).


Faster input
For faster input where you are processing single quantities with no discounts or line comments and with the same order number, set the Singles (F6) checkbox to true. Then simply scan or type in the barcode of each product and it will be saved automatically to the browser.

Appending
When invoicing, you can append approvals, special orders, pro forma invoices and quotations.When entering a credit note, you can append an invoice to reverse it.
Wholesale (distributor/publisher configuration only).
Note that to append an approval you must select invoice type 'Charge Only', as the stock will already have been removed from your inventory.
When entering a credit note, you can append an invoice to reverse it or from a returns authorization.


How to append a transaction
·	Click on the Append button.
·	The Append From combo box will be displayed. This will display all the transaction numbers for this customer that are available to be appended into this transaction:
·	special orders where stock has been received,
·	quotations,
·	pro forma invoices,
·	approvals ( if 'Charge Only' has been selected)
·	invoices,
·	return authorisations.



·	Select the transaction you wish to append (e.g. Quote 367425 )
·	A pop up box will be displayed showing all the lines that can be appended.
·	To de-select a line double click on the Use column.
·	To reduce the quantity to be appended, double click in the Quantity column and overtype it. Note that for approvals, you will not be able to edit the quantity.



·	Click the OK button or press the Enter key and the selected lines will be copied into the browser. You can then edit any line by double clicking on it to copy it into the edit line.
·	Click Save (F12) to save your changes.

Appending another customer's transactions
The Append From combo box display displays only the approvals, specials etc for that customer. To append transactions from another customer, right click on the window to open the context menu and select one of the five Append options.

Results of appending
·	Special orders: when the invoice is posted, the system will update the special orders file to show that special order as invoiced.
·	Approvals: when the invoice is posted, the system will update the approval file to show that approval as invoiced.
·	Invoices: appending an invoice to a credit note. On posting, the program will automatically allocate the credit against the invoice in the debtors file. You can turn off this feature for individual customers by setting the 'no auto allocate' flag on Financial tab of the customer master.

Backorders, stock availabilityand due dates
Distributor/publisher configuration only
You can set a stock availability message against each status type in the Status Maintenance window (main menu: Tables/Authority tables/ Status).
The message will print as the backorder reason against each backorder line of your invoice. For example you may set 'temporarily out of stock' against the 'active' status.

In the Order entry window, this message displays in red on the edit line.


You can edit this field and set a due date for the delivery by clicking the F7 button. This opens the Availability pop-up window. Set the due date and a new availability message and click the OK button.




ENTERING THE FOOTER DETAILS

Step 8: Footer details
·	Apply a discount off the sale total.
·	Add a freight charge.
·	Add a surcharge.  (If wholesale)
·	You can add up to three lines of header Comments.

Step 9: Applying payments
To apply a payment click on the Payment button (F9). This will open the Tendering window.



·	The default transaction type will be that set on the Financial tab of System Options.
·	The Purchases, Total Sale, Owing and Amount Tendered fields will display the sale total including backorders.
·	If there are backorders, the total excluding backorders is displayed just above the Amount Tendered field. To accept payment for just the lines in stock, click on the Use button. (Use button will act as a toggle between including and excluding backorders). In the retail configuration, the backorder will take the form of a special order. Any payment for it will be stored as a deposit on the special order.
·	The Tendering window process is the same as that in the POS window. Refer to that section for details.
·	Note: for non-debtor customers the invoice must be paid in full to process. You will know that this is required by the Required message, as shown in the image below:



Step 10: Print options
·	Print Preview: choose this option when you are processing single quantities of goods with no discounts or line comments and with the same order number. Simply scan the barcode of each product.
·	Batch print: when the box is unchecked invoices are printed as they are posted. When ticked, invoices will be printed when you close the window at the end of the session. You can also start the batch printing by opening the context menu (right mouse click anywhere in the window) and selecting 'print batched transactions'.
·	Carton labels: if you wish to print carton labels, tick Print checkbox. For more than one label, overtype the label quantity in the 'No:' field. You can print more carton labels after you have posted by opening the context menu (right mouse click anywhere in the window) and selecting 'reprint invoice labels'.

Step 11: Posting
·	Click the Post button (F12) to post the transaction.
·	If the customer is not a debtor and full payment has not been entered, the Tendering window open allowing you to record the payment (See Step 10 above.)
·	When the invoice has posted a pop up window will display with the invoice number.
·	For users of marketing module upon posting it will display the marketing window. See the Point of Sale chapter for details of the Marketing window. Note: the marketing window will only display for invoices and credit notes.









.topic 2740
Web orders are picked up from your web site by Bookscan's e-Comms program. e-Comms stores the orders in the Bookscan database and  displays them in the Web Orders window. From the Web Orders window you can manage your web orders, processing them without re-keying.

SETTING DEFAULTS
Prior to downloading orders from the web site, you my choose to map the web site transaction types to Bookscan types. This only takes a few minutes.
Open the Web export window, open the context menu, (right click),  and select "Set Defaults". Then click on the "Web transactions" [F11] button to open the following window:

Enter each of the transaction types in the order they are displayed on your web site. The numbering and order are important.
Those transactions that are made through a gateway on your web site must have the gateway checkbox ticked.
Where relevant, transactions should be mapped to Bookscan tender types.

WEB ORDER PROCESSING
The Web Orders window is opened from the main menu: Sales/Invoices /Web Orders.


There are three views which can be selected in the Filter radio button group:
Awating stock - if the order is waiting for stock it will be displayed here.
Require payment - if a credit card payment is yet to be processed the order is displayed in this view.
Ready to invoice - orders where the stock has been received and the payment processed can be invoiced in this view. Users of the warehouse module would probably want to use the picking and packing window rather than this view.

Awaiting stock
Orders waiting for stock it will be displayed in the "Awaiting stock (F4)" view. This option is for viewing only and has no processing function. As backorders and special orders are released, orders will move out of this view.

Processing payments
Orders waiting for a manual credit card payment is to be processed are displayed in the "Require payment (F5)" view.
(If payment has been taken on the web site via a credit card gateway or via PayPal, the order will be displayed in the "Ready to invoice" view).

MOTO Payments
If your EFTPOS provider suppprts MOTO payments (where you can type the credit card details into the EFTPOS machine), select the "Tendering window (F8)" option in the Payment Method box. When you click the Take Payment (F12) button the Tendering window will open and you can process the credit card in the usuual way.

Other payment methods
If you are procesing the card payment my other means, select the "EFTPOS payment (F7)" option. The default tender type is be displayed in the combo box just above this radio button. You can change the tender type here if you wish.
Click the Take Payment (F12) button to mark the payment  as taken.

To have the invoice be created as you take the payment, tick the Invoice when tendering (F9) checkbox. Otherwise the order will be move to the "Ready to Invoice" view.
For users of the warehousing module, the order, after taking payment, be displayed in the Picking window.

Invoicing
Orders waiting is to be invoiced are displayed in the "Ready to invoice (F6)" view. To invoice orders, simply select them in the left browser and click the Invoice (F12) button.







.topic 1330
Web orders are picked up from your web site by Bookscan's e-Comms program. e-Comms stores the orders in the Bookscan database and Bookscan displays them in the Web Orders window. From the Web Orders window you can manage your web orders, processing them without re-keying, via the Order Entry window. For the distributor/publisher configuration, a picking slip will be created. For the retail configuration, it will be an invoice.

The Web Orders window is opened from the main menu: Sales/Invoices /Web Orders.



·	The upper browser displays all the outstanding web order header details – the customer's email address, the web order number and date, the order value and shipping method.
·	The lower browser shows the items ordered.
·	To view the customer details, select a line in the upper browser and click the Show Details (F7) button (or just double click on the line). The customer details will display, hiding the upper browser. Click the Show List (F7) button to go back to the previous view.



·	If the customer already in the customer master the Customer field will display the customer code and name. If it is empty you will need to add the customer. To add the customer to the customer master, just click on the Add button.
·	To invoice the order, click on the Invoice (F8) button. The Order Entry window will open with this order filled out.



Make any amendments that you wish to the order header information and then click on the Save (F12) button. The browser, with the order lines filled in, will now be displayed.



Make any amendments that you wish to the order lines and then click on the Finalise (F12) button and the Order Entry Footer window will open.



·	Add any other information such as comments, freight, invoice terms etc.
·	Click on the Payment (F9) button to open the Tendering window.
·	Process the payment as for a standard invoice. If there are back orders, you will have the choice of taking payment for the full sale or just for the items in stock. In the latter instance you will need to process another payment when invoicing the backorder. In the retail configuration, the backorder will take the form of a special order. Any payment for it will be stored as a deposit on the special order.
·	Click on the Post (F12) button to create the invoice (or for the distributor/publisher configuration, a picking slip).


.topic 2660
Customers can place orders with you electronically using the Pacstream service. See the EDI chapter for details about setting up Pacstream. Using EDI orders will save re-keying and reduce the number of order entry errors, saving you time and money.
Customer EDI purchase orders are received from Pacstream using Bookscan's e-Comms program. e-Comms stores the orders in the Bookscan database tables and Bookscan displays them in the Incoming EDI Purchase Orders window. From this window you can manage your orders, processing them without re-keying, via the Order Entry window.

PREPARING EDI ORDERS



·	The upper browser displays all the outstanding EDI order header details – the customer code, unique EDI number, and their order number and date. Other columns relate only to Coles Myer orders.
·	The lower browser shows the items ordered.
·	To view the order header details, select a line in the upper browser and click the Show Details (F7) button (or just double click on a line in either browser). The customer details will display, hiding the upper browser. Click the Resume (F7) button to go back to the previous view.



·	By default the selling price to be used in the invoice is that shown in the Order Price column and the New Price showing as zero will be ignored. If you enter a non-zero figure into the New Price field, that price will be used in the invoice.
·	Order lines where the order price is different to the order price are colored red.
·	You can change all the prices in at order using the radio buttons in the Pricing Calculations group. Select the calculation type and click the 'Apply to all items' button. The new price will be displayed in the browser.
·	You can edit an individual order line by double clicking it in the lower browser to load it to the edit line. Enter your changes and click the Save Line (F12) button.
·	To cancel an unprocessed order click on the Cancel PO (F9) button.

INVOICING EDI ORDERS
For the distributor/publisher configuration, a picking slip will be created. For the retail configuration, it will be an invoice.

To invoice the order, click on the Invoice PO (F8) button. The Order Entry window will open with this order filled out.



Make any amendments that you wish to the order header information and then click on the Save (F12) button. The browser, with the order lines filled in, will now be displayed.



Make any amendments that you wish to the order lines and then click on the Finalise (F12) button and the Order Entry Footer window will open.



·	Add any other information such as comments, freight, invoice terms, payments etc.
·	Click on the Post (F12) button to create the invoice (or for the distributor/publisher configuration, a picking slip).


.topic 1340
Invoice, EDI and picking slip enquiries

In the Invoice Enquiry window you can look up:
·	invoices,
·	credit notes,
·	EDI orders,
·	outstanding picking slips (distributor/publisher configuration) and
·	customer backorders (distributor/publisher configuration).

Open the window from the main menu: Sales/Invoices/Enquiry.



Selecting the enquiry type
The default enquiry type is invoice and credit note. To change the enquiry type, click on the appropriate button – EDI Orders (F7), Pick Slips (F8) and Backorders (F9).
Notes
·	Picking slips and backorders are only relevant for distributor/publisher configurations.
·	Back orders and EDI Orders are only available during customer searches. Select the customer first, and then click on the EDI Orders (F7) or Backorders (F9) button
·	Credit notes display with negative figure in the Qty column of the browser and a red text field saying "credit". See the diagram below.



You can display transactions by transaction number or by customer code.
Displaying a single transaction
(See the top diagram)
To display at transaction in the browser, select it from the Trans No combo box. The easiest and fastest way to do that is type in the transaction number and press the Enter key. Alternatively use the mouse to select it from the drop down list.

Displaying a customer's transactions.
Select a customer in the Customer field in the usual way: entering the customer code or pressing the F2 button to open the customer search window.



In customer mode, you can opt to display the customer's outstanding EDI orders, picking slips and backorders by clicking the corresponding button.

Searching for a transaction by customer order number
Click on the Order No (F11) button to open the Order No Search pop up window. Enter the order number onto the search field and press the OK button. The program will search the invoice and picking slip database tables and if it finds the transaction, display it in the browser.

Options

Changing the backorder quantity (wholesale module only)
·	Select the customer in the Customer field.
·	Click on the Backorders (F9) button.
·	Double click on the line you want to change.
·	A dialogue window will open displaying the existing backorder quantity.
·	Overtype the new quantity and click OK the button.

Deleting backorders
·	In backorder view,highlight the lines you want to delete.
·	Click on the Delete Item (F5) button.

Deleting a picking slip
Single transaction  view
·	Set the transaction type to picking slip by clicking on the Pick Slips (F8) button.
·	Select a picking slip from the Trans No combo box.
·	Click on the Delete Order (F4) button.

Customer view
·	Select the customer in the Customer field.
·	Highlight a line from the order you want to delete.
·	Click on the Delete Order (F4) button.
·	Note that you can only delete one picking slip at a time.

Deleting lines from a picking slip
·	In picking slip view,highlight the lines you want to delete.
·	Click on the Delete Item (F5) button.

Amending picking slips
·	You can make the following amendments to picking slips:
o	forward order date,
o	header discount,
o	freight.
·	Type your changes in the appropriate fields and then click on the Save (F12) button.

.topic 1390

You can mange and enquire on approvals in the Approval Enquiry window. Open this window from the main menu: Sales/Approval/Enquiry, returns, sales.

Displaying approvals
You can lookup Approvals in three ways:

Enquiry by Approval number
In the Approval No field, type in the approval number and press the Enter key or click on the down arrow and select from the drop down list. All the lines on the approval will be displayed in the browser.
	
Enquiry by customer
Select a customer in the Customer field in the usual way - i.e. enter the customer code, use the F3 button to open the customer search window or the Alt F3 button to recall the last customer. All approval lines for the customer will be displayed in the browser.

Enquiry by ISBN
Type the ISBN into ISBN field. Alternatively use one the title shortcuts, click the F2 button to open the product search window or use the Alt F3 button to recall the last ISBN you used in the program. All the approvals for that ISBN will be displayed in the browser.



The browser will display three quantity fields:
Qty		The quantity ordered.
Ret'd		The number of this item returned by the customer.
Inv'd		The quantity of this approval line that has been invoiced.

Actions
Deleting an approval
In the browser, highlight a line of the order you wish to delete and click on the Delete Order (F4) button.

Deleting a line from an approval
Highlight a line in the browser, click on the Delete Line (F5) button.

Approval returns
Click on the Returns (F6) button. The window will change to an orange colour and the browser will display only items which can be returned. See approval returns below for details.

Invoice an approval
Where stock is still outstanding against an approval you can invoice it by clicking on the Invoice Appro (F8) button. The browser will display in invoicing mode and the window will turn green. See invoicing approvals below for details.

Loading a loan file from an external scanner
This is a specialist function and can be ignored by most users
This option is for users using the approval module for handling items on loan and using hand held scanners to process the loans and returns. A brief summary follows but you should contact Barcode Solutions for further details on setting up and using this option.
Creating export files for the scanners
Open the context menu and select in turn, 'Create title export file' and then 'Create customer export file'. This will create csv files of your customers and products to load into your scanners.
Importing loan files from your scanners
Export your csv loan file from your scanner. Click on the Load File button in the Approval Enquiry window. A pop up window will display where you can select your csv file. Click the OK button to load the file into the approvals table.

Printing
To view the approval reports printing options, click on the Printer Icon on the Bookscan toolbar. A pop up box will be displayed showing the available approval reports. These are:
All approvals
Prints a report of all outstanding approvals.
Option: Include approvals that have been filled.

Approvals summary
Prints a summary of outstanding approvals.
Options: Sort by customer or by date.

Approvals over 14 days old
Prints a summary of approvals over 14 days old.
Options: Change aging date.
Include approvals that have been filled

Approval by customer
Prints a summary report of approvals for selected customers.
Options: Print in title order
Include approvals that have been filled.

Approval reprint
Reprints a single approval.
You will need to select the approval from the drop down list.

Approval returns

Approval returns are done in the Approvals Enquiry/Returns window. A step by step guide follows:

Step 1:	Enter "returns mode"
·	To process returns, click on the Returns (F6) button ( or press the F6 key).
·	The window will change to an orange colour to indicate that it is in "returns mode".
·	If you have already selected an approval number or a customer, then only items eligible for return will be displayed in the browser.

Step 2:	Select the customer or approval number
By Approval Number
Select the Approval No. from the drop down box. All items on that approval that are eligible to be returned will be displayed in the browser.

By Customer
Select the customer by entering their key in the Customer field or by using the F3 button. All approval items for that customer that are eligible to be returned will be displayed in the browser. This option is particularly useful if the customer has taken many items at different times and returns them all in one go.



Browser Column heading definitions
Qty		Quantity originally despatched
Outstand	Number eligible to be returned (i.e. not invoiced or already returned)
Ret Qty	Quantity of returns of that you have entered in this session.


Step 3:	Enter the items to be returned
There are two methods for processing the returns
Scanning
If you have the returned items in front of you, you can scan them in.
·	In the Barcode field, scan or type in the barcode.
·	A dialogue box will open asking for the Returns Quantity (defaulting to the quantity that is eligible to be returned).
·	Change the quantity if you wish and click on the OK button (or press the Enter key).
·	Repeat this step for each line to be returned.
Note: Items entered this way are processed against the oldest approval number first.

Double clicking
If multiple items have been sent to a customer on different approval numbers, you may wish to use the double clicking method to ensure that returns are allocated against the correct approval number.
·	Double click on a line in the browser.
·	A dialogue box will open asking for the Returns Quantity (defaulting to the quantity that is eligible to be returned).
·	Change the quantity if you wish and click on the OK button (or press the Enter key).
·	Repeat this step for each line to be returned.

Step 4:	Options	
·	To print a receipt for these returns, check the 'Print returns list' box.
·	To check for special orders, check the Check special orders box. This will cause a Special Orders Slip to print if anyone is waiting for this item.
·	To print a returns docket n the docket printer, tick the Print Docket checkbox.

Step 5: Process returns
·	Once all the returns have been entered for the selected customer or approval number, you can finalise the returns by clicking on the Process Returns (F6) button.
·	If you change your mind, click on the Cancel Returns button (or press the Escape key) to cancel the returns process.

Step 6:	End returns mode
·	To go back to enquiries, on the Cancel Returns button (or press the Escape key).


Invoicing approvals

You can invoice your customers for products sent to them on approval in two different areas of Bookscan:
Order Entry window
In the Order entry window, select the 'Standard Invoice' transaction type. Then after saving the header information, click on the append button to load the approval (or part of it) into the browser. Finalise the transaction. An invoice will be created and the approval table amended to show the approval as invoiced

Approval Enquiry window
You can invoice approvals in the Approval Enquiry window. Open this window from the main menu: Sales/Approval/Enquiry, returns, sales.

Step 1: Set to invoice mode
After selecting an approval or customer, click on the Invoice Appro (F8) button.
·	The browser will display in approval invoicing mode and the window will turn green. Note that only approval lines that have stock still outstanding will be displayed.
·	If you see a blank browser then either the approval has been invoiced or returned.



Step 2: Load the edit line
Double click on a line in the browser that you want to invoice.
·	The edit line will appear showing the ISBN, quantity to be invoiced and the pricing.
·	The quantity to be invoiced will default to the quantity outstanding except when you are editing a line, when it will show the quantity you entered previously.



Step 3: Editing
·	Amend the quantity, pricing and discount fields as required.
·	Click on the Save Line (F12) button to save the line.
·	Alternatively click the Clear Edit Line button (or press the Escape key) to close the edit line without saving.
·	In the browser the quantity to be invoiced will display in the Sales column.
·	Repeat steps 2 and 3 for each line you wish to invoice.

Handy hint
Auto Scroll (F11)
Tick this checkbox to have the edit line display the next line when you click the Save Line (F12) button. Using this option can speed up data entry.

Step 4: Create invoice
To create the invoice, click on the Post Invoice (F8) button. Alternatively click on the Cancel Invoicing button (or press the Escape key) to cancel the process and return to enquiry mode.
The effects of posting are:
·	An invoice will be created.
·	Both the stock on hand and approval quantities in the title master will be reduced for each line.
·	The quantity invoiced in the approvals database will be increased for each line.

.topic 1440

Ordering
To create a purchase order for consignment stock, click on the  consignment  check box (or press F4) for each consignment line.
The purchase order for consignment lines will show This order line for consignment stock.



Receiving
When receiving stock, those lines that were ordered as consignments will have the Consignment box checked when you scan them in. You can turn any line into consignment stock by just clicking on the check box . Otherwise consignment receipts are processed in the same way as ordinary receipts.
When consignment receipts are posted, the On Hand and the Consignment figures in the title master are increased.

Sales
Bookscan automatically keeps  track of your consignment sales. If stock of a product is a mix of consignment and purchased stock, the system always assumes that you sell your purchased stock first. When that is all sold, sales are recorded as consignment sales.

Reconciliation
Periodically (usually monthly), you will need to advise your suppliers of your consignment sales. You do this in the Consignment Reconciliation window.
From the main menu select Sales / Consignments In to open this window.



Step 1 Update sales
In the Update to field select the date up to which you wish to process.   Then click on the Update sales button.
The system will search the sales history  and update the consignment sales quantity (Sales)  and average sales amount (Sales $) columns for each product in the browser. In the above example, there were 39 sales of "Rainmaker" at an average price of $17.64. The cost price, recorded during receiving, was $8.82. The Extended cost price was $343.98.

Step 2 Make amendments, shrinkage, correct returns, buy the stock
Double clicking on a line in the browser will copy it into the edit line. Here you can:
·	Amend the cost price if required.
·	If the cost price is to be calculated as a percentage of the average sell price, type that percentage into the Discount field and press the Tab key and the cost price will be recalculated.
·	If you are have decided to pay for the stock before selling it, enter the quantity to be sold in the Buy field.
·	If there has been any stock shrinkage and you are now going to pay for these items, enter the quantity in the Shrinkage field.
·	If you have returned the stock to the supplier, but have not processed them as consignment returns, enter the quantity returned in the Returned field. This will reduce the quantity on consignment but leave the on hand figure untouched.
Note: This procedure is only used to make corrections where stock was returned as non-consignment stock. If you want to process consignment returns, do so in the Draft returns window (main menu - Purchasing / Returns / Add / change )
·	Click on Save line (F12) to save the changes.

Step 3 Select invoice or sales advice option
In the Print area, select either Sales Advice to produce a Consignment Sales Advice report  or Invoice to create an invoice and update your creditors files.

Step 4 Process
Clicking the Process button produces your Consignment Invoice / Sales Advice reports. It will also update the consignment browser and the consignment field title master.

Right Mouse Options
Right click on the Consignment Reconciliation window to display these menu options:

Title master
Display the title master for the current product selected in the browser.

Delete consignment
Delete the currently selected consignment line.

Mark as complete
For the currently selected consignment line, sets the outstanding quantity as returned.

Show processing history
Opens a window showing a summary the consignment sale advices for the currently selected consignment line.
You can select a consignment advice here and reprint it.

Reprint consignment advice
Opens a window where you can select a consignment advice and reprint it.

Print summary
Prints a hard copy of the current browser. If the display is filtered by supplier, it will print a consignment listing for just that supplier. If no filter is set, the report lists consignments for all suppliers.

Invoicing consignments out
You can invoice consignments out in the Approval Enquiry window. Open this window from the main menu: Sales/ Consignments/ Consignments Out.

Step 1: Set to invoice mode
After selecting a consignment or customer, click on the Invoice Sales (F8) button.
·	The browser will display in consignment invoicing mode and the window will turn green. Note that only consignment lines that have stock still outstanding will be displayed.
·	If you see a blank browser, then either the consignment has been invoiced or returned in full.

Step 2: Select input method

Handy hint
Auto Scroll (F11)
Tick this checkbox to have the edit line display the next line when you click the Save Line (F12) button. Using this option can speed up data entry.

The program provides two methods for entering your customer's sales data:
Sales quantity method
Use this method if your customer has reported his actual sales quantities.
·	Click on the Sales radio button to set this mode of processing.
·	Double click on a line in the browser that you want to invoice.
·	The edit line will appear showing the ISBN, sales quantity field and the pricing.



·	The sales quantity will default to zero except when you are editing a line, when it will show the quantity you entered previously.
·	Type in the sales quantity.
·	Amend the pricing and discount fields as required.
·	Click on the Save Line (F12) button to save the line.
·	Alternatively click the Clear Edit Line button (or press the Escape key) to close the edit line without saving.
·	In the browser the quantity to be invoiced will display in the Sales column.
·	Repeat steps 2 and 3 for each line you wish to invoice.

Stock count method
Use this method if your customer has reported the amount of stock he has left.
·	Double click on a line in the browser that you want to invoice.
·	The edit line will appear showing the ISBN, stock count field and the pricing.



·	The stock count field will default to the quantity outstanding except when you are editing a line, when it will show the quantity you entered previously.
·	Type in the stock count.
·	Amend the pricing and discount fields as required.
·	Click on the Save Line (F12) button to save the line.
·	Alternatively click the Clear Edit Line button (or press the Escape key) to close the edit line without saving.
·	In the browser the stock count will display in the stock count column and the (calculated) sales quantity will display in the Sales column.
·	Repeat steps 2 and 3 for each line you wish to invoice.

Step 3: Create invoice
To create the invoice, click on the Post Invoice (F8) button. Alternatively click on the Cancel Invoicing button (or press the Escape key) to cancel the process and return to enquiry mode.
The effects of posting are:
·	An invoice will be created.
·	Both the stock on hand and consignment quantities in the title master will be reduced for each line.
·	The quantity invoiced in the consignments out database will be increased for each line.



.topic 1334
  Important: 	Close all windows within Bookscan
			on all terminals before proceeding.

Each day you need to close sales for the day to prepare your banking. If you do your banking more than once a day, you will need to run this process each time.

End of day overview
The end of day process is controlled in the Sales / Daily window and consists of several steps:

Printing the X report
As sales are made during the day the transactions are saved to a file called sales.dbf. The X report or the daily sales Graphic report will get its data from this file.

Closing sales
Closing sales copies all the data from the sales file into a file called psales.dbf and empties the sales.dbf file, ready for the next day's transactions.
Important: to ensure all the day's transactions are completed, all terminals must be set back to the main menu before closing sales. Normal business can resume immediately after the closing is complete – this should be a very short time.

Printing the daily sales and banking reports
The All sales (sales by item) and Cashbook reports use the psales.dbf file for their data.

Posting sales
Posting sales copies the data in the psales.dbf file into the sales history files, specifically saleline.dbf (financial data) and salehist.dbf (item sales). It also updates various summary files including period (usually weekly), monthly and annual sales files. Once these updates are done psales.dbf is emptied.

CLOSING AND POSTING SALES
Open the Daily Sales window from the main menu: click on Sales/Daily.
This window will guide you through the end of day process, remembering where you are up to in the process and ensure that you complete all the compulsory steps.
As you complete each step, the system will display a tick in the checkbox shown against each process.

Step by step guide to end of day processing



Step 1:	Print X reports
·	An X report is a summary of the day's takings by transaction type for all registers. The report gets its data from the daily sales table - sales.dbf.
·	You can choose to print the report in summary format or in detail; use the Summary Format check box below the X Report button to set this flag.
·	On the Sales tab of System Options, there are two more options you can set up:
·	Split register takings: setting this flag will report the sales takings for each register.
·	Split store takings: if your store is divided into sections, say General and Children's, you can set the register names in each area to reflect this by adding a prefix, e.g. GReg1, Greg2 and CReg3.  With the split store takings flag set, the takings will be subtotaled for each area.
·	At the bottom left of the Daily Sales window there is a checkbox to print this report on the docket printer. Otherwise the report prints to your standard printer.

You can also run a graphic sales report here.

Step 2:	Close sales
·	You will need exclusive access to the sales file to run this short routine. So ensure that all terminals have no Bookscan windows open.
·	Click on the Close sales button.
·	As soon as sales are closed you may begin trading again.
·	The date of the last close is shown below the button.

Step 3:	Print sales and cash reports
Note that these reports can only be printed after closing sales and before posting!
Click on the appropriate buttons to print the All Sales and Cash Book reports.
All sales report
This report is a list of all the day's sales by item. The data is taken from the psales.dbf table.
You can order the report by either:
·	Supplier
·	Sales Department
Other options are:
·	Summary or details format
·	Total on ISBN: when this flag is set the report will show total sales for each product. When not set, each sale it listed.
·	Consignment sales: when set, the report will also include consignment sales.
·	Consolidation (department order only): this special set up is where you have prefixed your department codes by a single letter to split them into groups. In this case, the report is sorted by these groups. E.g. you have departments A100,A101, A102, B100, B101 etc. The report will show all 'A' departments first then 'B's etc, subtotaling on each grouping.
	
Cash book report
This report is a list of all the day's sales transactions by transaction type. The data is taken from the psales.dbf table.
Options here are:
·	Summary or details format
·	A Banking List: with this flag set, the report will also print a banking list showing the cheques taken that day.
·	Split stores: splits the report up in register groups, similar to the X report (see above).
·	Split register takings: setting this flag will report the sales takings for each register.

Step 4:	Post sales
After reconciling your takings, click on the Post Sales button to conclude the process. The psales.dbf file will be copied into the sales history files and used to update the various summary files including the period, monthly and annual sales files. Once these updates are done psales.dbf is emptied.



.topic 1460
Vouchers

Vouchers are set up in the Title Master with the Type set to Untracked Voucher, Voucher, Tracked, or Docket Voucher.
For Docket vouchers, the starting number is initially set in the System Options - Counters tab.  Tracked Vouchers use the number that is on your pre-printed voucher, and this is enterred into Bookscan during the sale process.


Tracked vouchers
A tracked voucher is a gift voucher whose number and outstanding value is recorded within the Bookscan program. You can view the amount outstanding and the history of the vouchers use in the Voucher Enquiry window.

You need to set up voucher tender types in the Transaction types.

In the Tendering window, when you select a tender transaction of type 'tracked voucher', the window replace the Amount Tendered field with two fields: Voucher Amount and Voucher Number.



Enter the voucher number in the second field. The Voucher Amount field will then display the amount outstanding on the voucher. Click the OK button or press the Enter key, and if the voucher is sufficient to cover the sale, the transaction will be finalised. If the voucher still has an outstanding amount after the sale, this amount will be displayed in a pop-up window. Write this new balance on the voucher.
If the voucher is insufficient to cover the sale total, click the OK button will cause the amount to be saved to the Transaction browser, the Owing and Amount Tendered fields reduced and the tender type reset to the default.



Select another tender type to complete the transaction. See 'multiple tender types' below.

Docket Vouchers

These are tendered using the same process as tracked vouchers above, however when only partially redeemed, Bookscan will print a new copy of the voucher with the same number, but an updated remaining value, when the sale is completed.

Rewards traked vouchers

You must have set up a rewards tracked voucher separate to the normal tracked vouchers.  When you select this tender type, the drop down list will display ony the reward tracked vouchers for the customer selected in the cash sales screen.



Selling tracked vouchers
Tracked voucher are set up in the title master in the usual way but with the 'product type' set as 'tracked voucher'. You may have your vouchers printed with a barcoded product number on them or you may choose to set up your vouchers with an easy to remember product code such as 'VOU'. See the Title Master section for further details. To sell your vouchers through the POS, enter the product code in the Barcode field. The program will display the Voucher Entry window.



Enter the number of vouchers the customer is buying.
In the next field enter the first voucher in the sequence. The program will then display the final number in the sequence. If any of the voucher numbers in the sequence have already been sold, you will be prompted.
If you have a broken sequence of numbers, break them up into contiguous sequences or enter each voucher individually.
Click the OK (F12) button to save the line to the browser.
Then add any other purchases and finalise the sale as usual.


Gift cards and untracked vouchers
You may find it useful to set gift cards and untracked voucher transactions to be of  type "require name". Where this setting is made, you will be required to enter a customer name (or other detail such as a voucher number) in a text field on the Tendering window. (Refer to the Set Up chapter for details in setting up the various transaction types).



So in the Tendering window, select your transaction type.  The window will display a text field Surname below the Amount Tendered field. You can overtype the amount tendered if required then press the Enter or the Tab key to move to the Surname field. You can type up to 20 characters. Click the OK button or press the Enter key to finalise the transaction. If the amount tendered is greater than the sale amount, the difference is displayed as change in the Change window. If the amount tendered is insufficient to cover the sale, it becomes a  part payment, and you will need to select another tender transaction to complete the sale - see 'multiple tender types' below.

Enquire on Vouchers
From the main menu, click on Sales, then point  to Vouchers and then click on Enquire.
A window will open displaying the list of vouchers on the system.
Check the Show outstanding only box to display only those vouchers not finalised.



Columns displayed:
Voucher number
Date issued
Customer Key (if any)
Customer name (if any)
Purchase price
Amount outstanding
If it is a voucher generated by a rewards program

Click on the      icon at the top of Bookscan to print a listing of the vouchers.

You can also double click in the Outstanding field to adjust the amount to a new, (lower only), value.  The amount cannot be adjusted upwards.

Print voucher reports
From the main menu, click on Sales, then point to Vouchers and click on Print.
You have the option to select all vouchers or finalised vouchers only.
Click OK to print.

Purge vouchers
From the main menu, click on Sales, then point to Vouchers and click on Purge.
The system will offer to purge all finalised vouchers older than a year. To change the purge date, select a new date from the drop down box.
You have the option to purge all vouchers or finalised vouchers only.
Click OK to purge.

.topic 1500

Your system will already have been configured for you by Barcode Solutions during system installation.
The following information is for those making subsequent changes to their system.

Selecting the account type default
Below the debtors check box is the Open Item check box.
Here you can select the default account type that will be displayed when adding a customer to the customer master.

Select from the two types of debtors accounts supported by Bookscan. They are:
	· Open item accounting (Check the box to select)
	· Balance brought forward

Open item accounting
This retains all transactions on the account until they are paid. At the next aging, they are then moved to the history file. Payments are applied against specific Invoices.
Unpaid or part paid items remain on the account until cleared.

Balance brought forward
These accounts show only the account balance for anything older than current month transactions.
The complete history can be obtained only from previous statements.

Barcode Solutions strongly recommend the use of Open Item accounting as this retains the details of each transaction until cleared.
Should you be asked some months later to explain how an outstanding balance is made up, this is very simple with Open Item Accounting.  With BBF Accounting this information is not readily available.

Setting up debtors for multiple stores
This will have been configured by Barcode Solutions when they installed the system.
If all the debtors are to be handled at a head office, check the following boxes on the Stores window of System Options:
	· Centralised debtors
	· Installation has a head office
If this store is also the head office, check the box:
	· Installation is a head office
For branch stores leave this box unchecked.
Click on the link to go to System Options for details.

	   Return to accounts receivable summary

.topic 2860
Statements can be emailed to debtors during End Of Month.  Those who do not have an email will be saved into a single PDF that can be printed for mailing.

1. Before setting up in Bookscan, you need to have the PDFexport printer device installed on your computer.  To do this, download the installer from http://support.e-web-site.com/downloads/ftp/Barcode_PDF_Writer.zip
Open the zip file with File Explorer and extract the Barcode PDF Writer folder to your Bookscan Downloads folder.  Go into the folder & double click the Setup.exe to run the installer.

Follow the prompts & leave the default settings.  If asked, do not set it as your default printer.

2. Set up email program.

2a. Mozilla Thunderbird (as at v78.6.1): Go to Tools – Options, Scroll down & click Config Editor.
In the search bar enter mapi.blind-send.warn, double click on it to edit & set it to false.

2b. MS Outlook: You will probably need to apply some settings in the registry to allow Bookscan to send the emails.  You can download & set them by running the registry file from: http://support.e-web-site.com/downloads/utilities/Outlook_v12-16.reg

3. In the Customer Master, there are now options to apply a secondary email address for finance and the option for No email statements.  If a Finance email is saved on the address tab, it will be used in place of the regular email.



If the client does not wish to receive statements by email, you can tick the No email statements on the Details tab.



4. To set up to email statements, go through the End Of Month process as normal until you reach the Run Statements section.  Before running them, tick the I Would Like To Email Statements checkbox & click on the Email Settings button.



In the next window, enter the text you want to appear as the email header, & in the body.



Clicking on the Help For Email Setting button will open a window with the codes to have the emails customised for the clients with their names.



eg. If in the example above, I have used 'Dear &(salute) &(surname),'.  This will start the body of the email to, (for example Mr John Smith), as 'Dear Mr Smith,', based on the data entered in the salutation & surname fields of the client in the Customer Master.

Once this has been entered, click the Test button to verify that the PDF printer & email are set up & working.  Then click Save (F12).

You can now click Run Statements.  This will generate the emails & send them one by one through your email program as an attachment.  The emails will go to the clients from the local PC email account, with the normal footer for that account.

Statements that are not emailed will be saved as a PDF in the [database folder]\debeom folder, and can be printed for mailing in bulk if required.  After sending emails out, you can see them in your Sent folder, & you should check your Inbox for any rejections or bounce messages.  These will come with a reason for the rejection that can be used to correct the issue.

.topic 1530

Posting invoices
If you have selected Auto Post GRNs in System Options, invoices are automatically posted to accounts payable during receiving.
If Auto Post GRNs is not selected all invoices and credit notes have to be entered manually as described below.

Manual Invoices
Note that if Auto post GRNs in system options has been selected, invoices are automatically posted here during receiving.
In that case the manual invoice entry allows you to enter an invoice raised externally.

Step 1:
From the main menu, click on Creditors and then click on Transactions.

Step 2:
In the Creditors Transactions window, select a supplier by either:
·	typing the supplier key in the Supplier box.
·	clicking the    button and scrolling through drop-down list, or
·	clicking the     button and using the Find window.

Step 3:
Right click anywhere in the window and select Invoice (or Credit) from the drop down menu.

Step 4:
Enter the customer's Reference number , the Transaction amount and a Description of the transaction.
Click OK.


Credit notes and journals.
Manual credit notes and journals are handled in exactly the same way as manual invoices except that,  instead of Invoice, you select Credit, Debit Journal or Credit Journal  from the drop-down menu.
· Journal Credits can be applied against any outstanding debit.
   If no debit is selected, these entries appear on the account as unapplied credits.
· Journal Debits can be applied against Credits, Journal Credits, or any unapplied payments.

Your system will already have been configured for you by Barcode Solutions during system installation.

Selecting the account type default
On the Modules tab in System Options (from the main menu - select Utilities/ System Options.)
Below the creditors check box is the Balance BF Credit check box.
Here you can select the default account type that will be displayed when adding a customer to in the customer master.

Select from the two types of accounts supported by Bookscan.
They are:
	· Open item accounting (Check the box to select)
	· Balance brought forward

Open item accounting retains all transactions on the account until they are paid. At the next ageing, they are then moved to the history file. Payments are applied against specific Invoices. Unpaid or part paid items remain on the account until cleared.

Balance brought forward accounts show only the account balance for anything older than the current month's transactions. The complete history can be obtained only from previous statements.

Barcode Solutions strongly recommend the use of Open Item accounting as this retains the details of each transaction until cleared.
Should you be asked some months later to explain how an outstanding balance is made up, this is very simple with Open Item Accounting.  With BBF Accounting this information is not readily available.

Auto posting GRNs
If you check this box, invoices and credit notes are automatically posted to the supplier account during receiving. (Of course you will still be able to add invoices and credit notes manually as well). This is the usual way of setting up creditors.
If you leave this box unchecked you will have to add all invoices and credit notes manually.

.topic 1550

End of Month
At month end there are three processes to be done on creditors, after your cheque run:
	· Print the remittances.
	· Age the accounts
	· Purge old orders.

Remittance Advices
From the main menu, click on Creditors and then point to End of Month and then click on Remittances.
You can choose to print remittances for:
	· All Creditors
	· A single creditors (select a creditor in the drop down box).
	· All creditors starting from the one selected.
	· Queued creditors - Use this option if used the Creditors/Cheques/Individual option to record your payments to creditors. Remttance advices for all the creditors you selected will be printed.

When  printing remittance advices for all creditors, check the Print for creditors with current transactions but zero balances
All payments show as current if there is an invoice in the 30 day period and a payment in the current period which clears the account, these would be missed if you don't check this box.

Aging
Before aging the accounts, ensure all activity on the network has ceased.
Then do an accounts back up.
Then age the accounts:
From the main menu, click on Creditors and then point to End of Month and then click on Aging. Click the OK button and it's done.

Purging Old Records
From the main menu, click on Creditors and then point to End of Month and then click on Purge.
The default date is twelve months before the current date.
However, depending on the size of your accounts, choose whatever date suits you.


Year End
The only thing you have to do with creditors at year end is to zero the YTD purchases figures.
From the main menu, click on Creditors and then point to End of Month and then click on Year end.
Change the date if necessary, and then click OK.

	   Return to accounts payable summary Remittance

.topic 2500
Please note: MYOB export is a licensed functionality.  Contact Barcode Solutions to purchase a license.
If you do not have a license you can use, for evaluation purposes, the window up to the point where the MYOB files are updated.

Introduction: exporting Bookscan accounts to MYOB

You can export all transactions created in Bookscan to MYOB standard edition V17 onwards. It only takes about 10 minutes to set up Bookscan for MYOB export. The steps are outlines below. Once set up, you can export daily weekly, monthly or whenever you choose. Exporting should only take a few minutes.

During the export the following types of transactions are totaled and journals are created for import into MYOB:
·	POS sales
·	Laybys
·	Layby deposits, deposit refunds and payments
·	Special order deposits and refunds
·	Special order deposit redemptions
·	Debtor payments and refunds
·	Cost of goods sold
·	Invoices and credit notes (if you are not exporting them individually).
·	Purchases and credit claims (if you are not exporting them individually).

If you are not using the Bookscan creditors option, you can export your supplier invoices and credit claims individually rather than as totaled journals.  In this case you will also need to export your supplier cards into MYOB.
Similarly if you wish to handle debtor payments in MYOB rather than in Bookscan, you can export your customer invoices and credits individually rather than as totaled journals. In this case you will need to export your customer cards into MYOB.
You can set one or both of these options on the Financials tab of System Options.

Important note
For the export of purchases you will need to have the correct invoice totals and GST for supplier invoices. These can be entered in several places:
·	First page of the Receiving window
·	The final window when posting received items into stock.
·	The Good Inwards History window: this is a new window for version 5.00.00. To open it, select Purchasing/Incoming/Receiving history from the main menu. Double click on a line to edit the invoice details.
·	The Creditors window: Select "change invoice details" from the context menu.

Export methods
There are two methods of exporting into MYOB:

1. Exporting directly to MYOB
Exporting directly to MYOB is faster and safer than using manual files. The cost of this service is including in the Bookscan accounts export license - there is no extra charge.

2. Exporting via csv files
To set up this option just click on the Create CSV import file radio button and then select the path for the csv files in the file path field.

Five steps to set up exporting to MYOB:
Step 1: Set up the MYOB activation code.
	
Step 2: Set up the MYOB ODBC drivers.	
	
Step 3: Set up Bookscan.

Step 4: Map your chart of accounts.

Step 5: Prepare for first time use

These steps are detailed below in the Setting Up section.

Summary of procedures for exporting from Bookscan to MYOB:
·	Exporting out of Bookscan and into MYOB
·	Exporting purchases separately
·	Exporting invoices separately
Detailed instructions on exporting are set out below in the Exporting to MYOB section.

Procedures for exporting to MYOB
	Setting up MYOB Export
	Exporting out of Bookscan and into MYOB
	Exporting purchases separately
	Exporting invoices separately


.topic 2510
Step 1: Connecting Bookscan to MYOB
To connect Bookscan to MYOB you must first update your MYOB license. Please follow the steps below:
·	Call Barcode Solutions for a MYOB activation number.
·	Call 1300 555 151 and select the option "to connect an add-on solution to MYOB". You will be required to quote this Add-on Solution activation number and your MYOB serial number.
·	Open the company file and login using the MYOB Administrator ID and Password. In the case of a multi-user system, you should use the 'Single User' option.



·	Once logged in, from the 'Setup' menu select 'Company Information'.


·	Choose the 'License' button at the bottom left of the Company Information screen.



·	Follow the onscreen instructions to update the license information held within the company file.


Once these steps have been completed, the file will be activated for your Add-on Solution and Bookscan Bookscan will be able to integrate with your MYOB company data file.

Step 2: Installing MYOB ODBC
You will need to set up MYOB ODBC. This is quite simple to do:

Install the software
·	In Windows, click Start, then All programs.
·	From the menu, select MYOB Accounting Plus v??/MYOB Tools/MYOB ODBC/ Install MYOB ODBC Direct V?.
·	Follow the instructions in the installation program.

Setting up the ODBC drivers
·	In windows, click Control Panel.
·	In Control Panel, double click Administrative Tools. Then double click Data Sources (ODBC). The Data Source ODBC Administrator window will open.


·	Click the Add button. Select MYOB_ODBC_AU7 and click the Finish button. Note that the name of the ODBC driver will vary with your location and version number.

·	In the next window that opens, enter a unique data source name say, 'MYOB7' and a Description say, 'MYOB Bookscan'.
·	In the Company file path field browse to your MYOB program (e.g. 'C:\myob16\MyShop.myo' ).
·	Click the OK Button

Step 3: Setting up Bookscan
Open the Accounts Export window from the main menu: Accounts/Accounts export.

In the set up tab:

Exporting directly to MYOB
·	Click on the direct to MYOB radio button.
·	In the 'MYOB .myo path' field, select the path of your MYOB account.
Example: C:\MYOB17\MyShop.myo
·	In the 'MYOB .exe path' field, select the path of your MYOB program.
Example: C:\MYOB17\MYOB.exe
·	In the MYOB ODBC DSN field, enter the name (DSN) you have given your MYOB ODBC driver.
Example: MYOB7
·	In the Network protocol field, select the network type.
Example: No Network

Exporting via csv files
·	Click on the Create csv export file radio button.
·	In the File path field, select the folder where you want your csv files placed.
Example: C:\MYOB17\e-BilityExport




Step 4: Setting up the chart of accounts
You will need to map your Bookscan data to your chart of accounts in MYOB.
A default chart of accounts (CoA) is set up in Bookscan when you first open the MYOB Export window. The chart is based on the default CoA that is created by MYOB for a bookshop. You can edit the account numbers to match the account number you have set up in MYOB.

Open the MYOB Export window from the main menu: Accounts/Export to accounts.
The CoA is displayed on the Setup tab.

The browser at the top right displays two cash asset accounts:
	1-1110 Cheque account
	1-1130 Cash drawer
You can add extra asset accounts by clicking on the Add Asset Account button.

Other accounts are listed the browser at the lower right. These accounts have a one-to-one mapping to the various transaction types so, while you can change the account numbers and descriptions, you can't add new accounts here. You should edit the account numbers to match those you have set up in MYOB. Just double click on the number you to change and overtype them.

Notes:
·	For each transaction type you have set up in Bookscan (cash, cheques, EFTPOS, vouchers etc), there is a separate Income – sales account set up on the Bookscan CoA. If you are using the standard MYOB CoA set up, you will need to add these accounts to your MYOB COA. If you do not want to split the Income – sales accounts change all these account numbers to 4-1100 (or your Income – Sales own account number).
·	Even if you are not using an area of Bookscan such as laybys, just leave the CoA entry in the browser. The export will ignore it.
·	At any time you can re-load the default chart of accounts by pressing the Load Default COA button.

Example
In the diagram above, EFTPOS sales have a default income account of 4-1115. This account will need to be added to your MYOB CoA. It has a default corresponding cash account of 1-1130 which is for the cash drawer. This figure should be changed to 1-1110 which is the cheque account.

Step 5: Preparing for first time use
You will need to mark old records as not to be exported. To do this:
·	Click on the 'Prepare for first use' button.
·	Select the date and press the OK button.
Records dated prior to the date selected will be marked as exported.


.topic 2530

You can export your Bookscan data to MYOB daily weekly, monthly or whenever you choose. Exporting should only take a few minutes.

When exporting from Bookscan to MYOB, the program will total all your various transactions created since the last export and create journals for them in MYOB. Fir invoices and purchases, you can opt to export the transactions individually if you wish.

Exporting accounts
·	Exporting takes place in the Journals tab of the MYOB Export window.
·	Open the tab and you will see the top right group box 'Load from sales'. This displays the date of the oldest transaction ready to be exported ("Start Date") and the date of the latest transaction ready to be exported ("End Date"). You can change the dates of the export to what you require (say, to the last month's transactions).
·	Click on the Load button to load the transactions in to the browser. All transactions between the dates specified will be totaled and the journals to be created will be displayed in the browser.

·	Select the date you want for the journals (say, the last day of last month).
·	Press the Export Journals button and the export is complete.

Effects of the export:
·	Journals will be created in MYOB for each line in the Journals browser.
·	The MYOB chart of accounts will be updated.
·	The transactions in Bookscan will be flagged as exported.


Using the manual csv file method
·	The process is the same as for the direct export except that in the last step, pressing the Export Journals button will cause the program to create a single csv text file called JournalsYYYYMMDD.txt in the path you selected in the Set up tab. The file will comprise all the journals to be loaded into MYOB
·	Open MYOB. In the MYOB main menu, select File\Import date\General journal entries. This will open the Import File window.
·	In the "Import file format" combo box, select "Comma-separated".
