·	In the "First record is" combo box, select "Data record".
·	Click the Continue button.
·	In the next window that opens, navigate to the file path you select earlier and select the JournalsYYYYMMDD.txt file. Then click the Open button.
·	In the Import Data window that now appears click on the Match All button. Then click the Import button and you are done. You may now wish to move the text file to a new folder, and re-name it, to prevent it being accidentally imported a second time.


.topic 2540
If you are not using the Bookscan creditors option, you can export your supplier invoices and credit claims individually rather than as totaled journals. You can set this option on the Financials tab of System Options. In System Options you can also select whether to export your credit claims as 'orders, or 'bills'. Check your MYOB documentation to see which suits your MYOB set up.

Supplier invoices, credit claims & suppliers
If you have selected "Export purchases individually" in System options, an extra tab, Purchases & Suppliers, is displayed in the MYOB export windows.

Setting up for first time use
You will need to export your supplier cards to MYOB first. First, flag any suppliers you do not want to export by clicking the 'Exclude from account export' checkbox on the Detail tab of supplier tab.
Next load all the unflagged suppliers into the browser: right click the browser and select Load all cards into browser. Then click the Export Suppliers button. This will create the export file SuppCardsYYYYMMDD.txt in your MYOB export file path.
Importing in MYOB is almost the same as outlined above in "Importing into MYOB":
·	Open MYOB. In the MYOB main menu, select File\Import date\Cards\Supplier cards. This will open the Import File window.
·	In the "Import file format" combo box, select "Comma-separated".
·	In the "First record is" combo box, select "Data record".
·	Click the Continue button.
·	In the next window that opens, navigate to the file path you select earlier and select the SuppCardsYYYYMMDD.txt file. Then click the Open button.
·	In the Import Data window that now appears click on the Match All button. Then click the Import button and you are done. You may now wish to move the text file to a new folder, and re-name it, to prevent it being accidentally imported a second time.
When new suppliers are added to Bookscan they will appear in the Supplier Export browser. You should export these to MYOB before exporting the purchases and credit claims.


Exporting purchases and credit claims
In the Purchases & Suppliers tab, the top right group box is Purchases export. This displays the date of the oldest transaction ready to be exported ('Start Date') and the date of the latest transaction ready to be exported ('End Date'). You can change the dates of the export to what you require (say, to the last month's transactions).
Note: MYOB will not import data dated before its lock date. You can enter the lock date here to ensure transactions dated prior to that date are re-dated so MYOB will import them.

Press the Load Purchases button. This will load the Purchases/Claims browser. Transactions whose supplier is not marked for '"no account export' will show a tick in the Use column. Only these records will be exported to MYOB. (The other records will be marked as 'not for export' after you click the Send to MYOB button). You can toggle the Use column value by double clicking it.

Importing into MYOB
Using the direct method
Press the Send to MYOB button.

Using the manual csv file method
Press the Send to MYOB button to create the PurchasesYYYYMMDD.txt file.
Importing in MYOB is almost the same as importing journals into MYOB:
·	Open MYOB. In the MYOB main menu, select File\Import date\Purchases\Service Purchases. This will open the Import File window.
·	In the Import file format combo box, select Comma-separated.
·	In the First record is combo box, select Data record.
·	Click the Continue button.
·	In the next window that opens, navigate to the file path you select earlier and select the PurchasesYYYYMMDD.txt file. Then click the Open button.
·	In the Import Data window that now appears click on the Match All button. Then click the Import button and you are done. You may now wish to move the text file to a new folder, and re-name it, to prevent it being accidentally imported a second time.


.topic 2550
If you wish to handle debtor payments in MYOB rather than in Bookscan, you can export your customer invoices and credits individually rather than as totaled journals.  You can set this option on the Financials tab of System Options.

Customer invoices & customers
If you have selected 'Export invoices individually' in System options, an extra tab, Invoices & Customers, is displayed in the MYOB export windows.

Setting up for first time use
You will need to export your customer cards to MYOB first. First, flag any customers you do not want to export by clicking the "No account export" checkbox on the Detail tab of the customer master.
Next load all the unflagged customers into the browser: right click the browser and select Load all cards into browser. Then click the Export Customers button. This will create the export file CustCardsYYYYMMDD.txt in your MYOB export file path.
Importing in MYOB is almost the same as outlined above in 'Importing into MYOB':
·	Open MYOB. In the MYOB main menu, select File\Import date\Cards\Customer cards. This will open the Import File window.
·	In the 'Import file format' combo box, select 'Comma-separated'.
·	In the 'First record is' combo box, select 'Data record'.
·	·Click the Continue button.
·	In the next window that opens, navigate to the file path you select earlier and select the CustCardsYYYYMMDD.txt file. Then click the Open button.
·	In the Import Data window that now appears click on the Match All button. Then click the Import button and you are done. You may now wish to move the text file to a new folder, and re-name it, to prevent it being accidentally imported a second time.
When new customers are added to Bookscan they will appear in the Customer Export browser. You should export these to MYOB before exporting the invoices and credits.



Exporting customer invoices and credits
In the Invoices & Customers tab, the top right group box "Invoices export". This displays the date of the oldest transaction ready to be exported ('Start Date') and the date of the latest transaction ready to be exported ('End Date'). You can change the dates of the export to what you require (say, to the last month's transactions).
If you have a multi store set up, you may wish to click the checkbox 'Add store code to invoice number'.

Press the Load Invoices button. This will load the Customer Invoices browser. Transactions whose customer is not marked for 'no account export' will show a tick in the Use column. Only these records will be exported to MYOB. (The other records will be marked as "not for export" after you click the Send to MYOB button). You can toggle the Use column value by double clicking it.

Importing into MYOB
Using the direct method
Press the Send to MYOB button.

Using the manual csv file method
Press the Send to MYOB button to create two files:  the InvoicesYYYYMMDD.txt file and the InvoicesCOGsYYYYMMDD.txt file (cost of goods sold).

Both the text files need to be imported into MYOB. The process is almost the same as importing journals into MYOB:
·	Open MYOB. In the MYOB main menu, select File\Import date\Sales\Service Sales. This will open the Import File window.
·	In the Import file format combo box, select 'Comma-separated'".
·	In the First record is combo box, select 'Data record'.
·	Click the Continue button.
·	In the next window that opens, navigate to the file path you select earlier and select the InvoicesYYYYMMDD.txt file. Then click the Open button.
·	In the Import Data window that now appears click on the Match All button. Then click the Import button. You may now wish to move the text file to a new folder, and re-name it, to prevent it being accidentally imported a second time.
·	For the InvoicesCOGsYYYYMMDD.txt file follow the same procedure for journal imports outlined in Importing Journals into MYOB above, except that you select the InvoicesCOGsYYYYMMDD.txt file.


.topic 1560

Bookscan allows you to create categories and sub-categories of your products for display on your web site.
The program also gives you a variety of ways to speed up adding products to your web categories and with a click of a button you can upload them to your web site.
If you wish, you can set up Bookscan to automatically update these categories as you add new products. See the Web Category Mapping section below.

Note: Before starting you will need to set the type of web site you are using. In System options, go to the Modules tab and select the website type in Web Site combo box.


Uploading products to your web site - a summary
·	Set up the main web categories. These might be Art & Architecture, History, Humour, Children's, Cooking, Fiction, Reference, Sport, Crime, Management, Romance and Best Sellers.
·	Within each main category, set up sub-categories. So the History category may have sub-categories of Ancient, European, American, Australian etc.



·	Add products to each sub-category. Products can belong to multiple sub-categories.
Manual method
In the Web Export window, enter products against each subcategory.
(You can do this one by one or you can select groups of products by department, imprint, Bookscan category, or Onix subject).
Automated method
In the Web Category Mapping window, you can link web sub categories to individual departments, imprints, Bookscan categories, or Onix subjects.
When creating new product codes in Bookscan, the product will automatically be added to the mapped web subcategory.
After the mapping has been completed you can do your initial loading of products into your web sub categories with just a few button clicks.
·	If you want to show Best Sellers on your, create and fill this category using the Best Sellers button on the Web Export window.
·	To export your categories to the web site, click on the Update Web button.
·	Open your web site and, in turn, click on these links in to complete the procedure:
§	Import Bookscan categories (skip this step if only updating products).
§	Import Bookscan titles
§	Import Bookscan main category links (skip this step if only updating products).



Web Export window

The Web Export window is opened from the main menu: Modules/ Web Export/ Web Export.



The window contains three browsers:
·	The first displays all the web main categories,
·	The second displays all the sub categories for the currently selected main category and
·	The third displays all the products on the currently selected subcategory.
To see all the columns in the products browser, it is recommended that you maximise the window.
You can choose to view either all products or only products not yet exported to the web using the 'Not Exported Only' check box.

NOTE:  All main and sub caterory names must be unique and cannot contain the characters listed below:
/ \ ' " & ( ) ,

Setting up web main categories
Note that to make your web site easy to use, it may be best to use at most, ten to fifteen main categories on your web site.
·    Click on the Add Main Category (F3) button
·	Enter the category name e.g. History in the main category field.
·	If you are going to display an icon or picture with this category, select the patch of the picture file in the Picture Path field. (Not relevant for e-web users)
·	Click on the Save (F12) button.
·	Add all your main categories in this fashion.

Setting up web sub-categories
·	Select a main category in the browser.
Click on the Add Subcategory (F4) button.
·	Enter the subcategory name e.g. Ancient History in the subcategory field.
·	Click on the Save (F12) button.
·	Add all your sub categories in this fashion.

Maintaining categories
·	You can edit a category name by clicking on the category in the browser and using the right mouse to open the context menu. Select Edit Category from the menu.
·	To delete a category, select it in the browser a select 'Delete Category' from the context menu. Note that when deleting a main category all its sub-categories and their products will also be deleted. Similarly deleting a sub-category deletes its products.

Changing the order of your main categories
Manual sorting
·	Right click on the window to open the context menu.
·	You will see that one of the two setting Double click to move UP and Double click to move DOWN.
·	Change the setting if required.
·	Double click on a main category to change its position in the list.

Automatic sort
You can sort your main categories alphabetically by opening the context menu and selecting 'Set main categories to alpha order'.

Note
Sub-categories appear in alphabetical order; their positions cannot be changed.

Adding a best sellers list
You do not need to manually create a best sellers category; the system will do this automatically.
·	Click on the Best Sellers (F11) button. A window will be displayed with the best sellers parameters.
·	Select:
o	The sales date range.
o	Whether you want the list based on quantity or profitability of sales.
o	The number of products in the list.
·	Click the Save Browser (F12) button (or if you're not happy with the list, the Discard Changes button).
·	The Best Sellers main category and sub-category will be created and the products loaded into the subcategory.	

Adding products to your web categories
You can add titles to a sub-Category manually by clicking Barcode (F2) or by scanning them in.  You can also map Departments, Imprints, Subjests or Categories directly to the Web Categories you have created & populate them automatically.  To do this see the Web Category Mapping page.


Upload to the web site
You are now ready to upload your categories and products to the web site. Instructions for users of the new Barcode Solutions e-Web web site follow immediately. Instructions for users of the earlier Barcode solutions web site and for other systems follow after that.

EXPORTING

Select the website type in Utilities - System Options - Modules tab, under the Web site section, middle left.


Also note; you can control which titles are uploaded by their publisher Status code using the Web flag in the Tables - Authority Tables - Status window.

Exporting to nopCommerce



Exporting to Other Sites

.topic 2830
EXPORTING: WooCommerce

Note: Uploads cannot be sent by machines running Windows XP.

Select Microsoft - Auto-update / Manual update, as preferred.

Initial setup for exporting

1. Registering WooCommerce libraries.

For Windows Vista/7/8/8.1/10
Run the supplied batch file: WooLibrary register. Run as Admin.bat by right clicking on it & selecting Run as administrator.


Alternatively, in the Windows destop search bar, type "cmd".  Right click on Command Prompt and select Run as Administrator.
Copy & Paste one of the following lines into the command prompt window depending on your version of Windows:
(To check your version, press [Windows]+[E], then in the File Explorer window, open the C:\ drive.  If the Program Files (x86) folder is there, you have 64 bit Windows, otherwise you have 32 bit.)

For 64 bit Windows - C:\windows\microsoft.net\framework\v4.0.30319\regasm "C:\program files (x86)\Bookscan\woolibrary.dll"
For 32 bit Windows: - C:\windows\microsoft.net\framework\v4.0.30319\regasm "C:\program files\Bookscan\woolibrary.dll"

If you need to unregister the library, please use the appropriate following command:

For 64 bit Windows: - C:\Windows\Microsoft.net\framework\v4.0.30319\regasm /unregister "C:\program files (x86)\Bookscan\woolibrary.dll"
For 32 bit Windows: - C:\Windows\Microsoft.net\framework\v4.0.30319\regasm /unregister "C:\program files\Bookscan\woolibrary.dll"

3. Set Defaults

Open the Set Defaults window by right clicking in the Web Export window, then clicking Set Defaults.



Set the image path in the Small Image Path field to where your images are stored in the Bookscan database.
Set the Jacket  FTP Server to the supplied ftp server path and
Set the Small and Large image Directories to /wp-content/uploads/
As shown below.
(The Custom Fields & Description field do not matter as they are used for the older e-Web uploads.)



Click Save(F12) when done.
Click on the Export to Web (F9) button.
Leave this window and Bookscan open & go to your web browser.

Web Site Settings

Log into your website with the administrator login & go to Users - Add New and create a new administrator login for yourself.  (Please set the Role to Administrator before clicking Add New User.)



After creating this, go to WooCommerce - Settings. Then select the Advanced tab & once the page loads, REST API, then click on Add Key.



Now enter a name for the new key, (eg. Uploads), the email for your admin user, select Read/Write from the dropdown selection and click Generate API Key.



Enter the Email used to create the new Administrator in the field marked Web site user ID:
Enter the Web Site URL the same as when opening the site in your browser.
Highlight the Public key with your mouse, right click on the test & select Copy.
Alt-Tab back to Bookscan and right click in the field marked Web Publickey and select Paste.
Highlight the Private key with your mouse, right click on the test & select Copy.
Alt-Tab back to Bookscan and right click in the field marked Web Secretkey and select Paste.
Tick the Remember passwords this machine (F3).




The Jacket user ID and Jacket password will be in the email with your site details.

You can now close your web browser if you like.

Setup is now complete, you can create a manual export by clicking Export (F12).

e-Comms will automatically update your web site when titles are updated, received or sold whenever it runs.




.topic 2850
EXPORTING: OTHER SYSTEMS

Export your categories
You are now ready to export your categories to the web site.
Note: If you have the Not Exported Only check box ticked, the system will offer to update the website by adding the new titles. (The export button will display Update Web (F9)). If the box is unchecked, the web site will be over written. (The export button will display Overwrite Web (F9)).
·	You can choose to export just the currently selected subcategory or the currently selected main category or all categories. Select the appropriate radio   button above the export button.
·	Click on the Overwrite Web (F9) button (or, if you are updating, Update Web (F9) button).
·	The web export files will be created and the products marked as exported.
·	For Barcode Solutions provided web sites, Bookscan e-Comms will load your web export files to your web ftp site.

Upload to the web site
These instructions are only for uploading into a Barcode Solutions provided web site.
·	Open the back office of your web site.
·	If you are loading for the first time, or have changed your main categories or sub categories click on the link: Import Bookscan categories.
·	To upload your products click on the link: Import Bookscan titles
·	If you are loading for the first time, or have changed your main categories or sub categories click on the link: Import Bookscan main category links.

Export file options
You can set various options for your web export files. In the Web Export window, open the context menu and select 'Set defaults'. The will open the Web Page Defaults pop-up window.





The optional exports are some the title master fields you may or may not want to export.
The stock availability options allow you to export either the stock on hand figure, no stock figures or a stock level message.
Export file can be in four different formats. For Barcode Solutions provided web sites, use the csv option.

Tab delimited exports
Note that the Optional Exports settings are ignored for tab delimited files. For thse files the field order is:
ISBN
Catalogue number
Short comment
Title
Edition
Selling price
RRP
Cost price
Jacket file name
Blurb
Publisher
Series
Available stock
Author
Illustrator
Year
Publication date
Binding (full binding name from the binding file)
Status (full status name from the status file)
Imprint (full imprint name from the imprint file)
Pages
Weight ( with measurement type defaulting to grams e.g. 250g )
Height ( with measurement type defaulting to 'mm' e.g. 200mm )
Width ( with measurement type defaulting to 'mm' e.g. 175mm )
Long comment





.topic 2680
You can map some or all of your imprints, Bookscan categories, departments and BIC subjects to your web categories. You can then load all your products into your web categories using this mapping. As products are added to the title master over time, the program will use the mapping to also add the products to your web export list. This will allow you to keep your web site up to date.

The Web Category Mapping window is opened from the main menu: Modules/ Web Export/ Web Category Mapping.



Before doing the mapping you will need to have set up your web categories in the Web Export window. See above for instructions on ding this.

Mapping departments
Select the Departments radio button. The Bowser will fill with all your departments.
As you highlight a department in the browser, it will display in the Link Department combo box. If you select a department in the combo box it will be shown as selected in the browser.
To link a department:
·	Select it in the browser or combo box.
·	Select the main category in the Main Category combo box. This will load that main category's sub-categories in the Sub-category combo box.
·	Select the sub-category.
·	Click the Link button.

In the example above HB NONFICTION has been linked to General Reference and MAPS has been linked to Atlases.

Other mapping
You can map your imprints, Bookscan categories and BIC subjects in the same way. You don't have to link them all; just what seems relevant.

Loading you products
After you have completed your mapping, you will need to load all your products to the web categories.
Open the Web Export window.
Open the context menu.
Select 'Load all titles from mapped databases'.
The browser will load with all the products mapped to the appropriate web categories.
Click the Update (F9) button to update the web site.

New products
As you add products to the title master, they will be automatically added to the web lists. Periodically you will need to open the Web Export window and export them to the web site.


.topic 199
In the Mail Out window you can create a mailing list for selected customers. This last may be in the form of:
·	mailing labels,
·	merge files
·	simple listings of customers and their addresses or
·	emails to the selected customers.
These options can be selected in the Produce combo box.

Customer lists can either be created in the window or loaded from files created earlier, either here, in customer rewards or when printing customer reports such as Most Profitable Customers and Best Customers.

Sending emails
If Email is selected in the Produce combo box, the Print button will be re-captioned Email. Clicking this button will open the Bookscan email window.



The selected customers' email addresses will be automatically loaded. You can add further email addresses manually.
Type a subject and a message in the appropriate fields and attach any files you want to send. Click the Send button to send the emails.	

Opening a saved customer mail out list:
·	Click on the Load List button. (If this button is not visible then you already have a list loaded. If so, click either the Save List or Clear List button first).
·	A standard Windows dialogue box will open where you can navigate to the file to be opened.
·	Select the file and click OK.
·	The browser will fill with the customer list.
Note that only mailout files created in Bookscan can be opened here.  (Mailout files can be created in the Customer Rewards module.)



Creating a customer list:
The report can be created from either:
·	the customer file,
·	customer categories.
·	editing a saved mailing list
·	adding customers manually

Creating a list using from the customer file
·	Click on the All Customers radio button.
·	All customers will be loaded into the browser.
·	You can then refine the list using the various filters etc described below.

Using customer categories		
·	Click on the Customers by Category radio button. This will enable the category fields.
·	Select categories using the Add Category combo box. The categories will be displayed in the Categories list box.
·	For complex expressions select the logical operator (AND, OR or AND NOT) from the Default Link combo box.
·	Then use the right mouse to add any brackets to the expression.

An example: To select customers from the Rewards category who are either also in the History or Gardening categories:
·	Select Rewards category.
·	Set the link to AND.
·	Select the History category.
·	Set the link to OR.
·	Select the Gardening category.
·	Right click on the History line in the list box and select opening bracket.
·	Right click on the Gardening line in the list box and select the close bracket.	 	
·	The browser will re-fill as you select each category or when a viable expression has been entered.
·	For complex expressions you can click on the Test Expression button to check for logical errors.  	

Using an existing mail out list
·	Open a saved mail out list as described above in Opening a saved customer mail out list.
·	Refine the list using the various filters etc described below.
·	Click on the Save List button and enter a name for the new file.

Adding customers one by one
·	Clear any existing file from the browser by clicking the Clear List button.
·	In the Add customer area, select customers in the usual way entering the customer code, shortcut or using the F2 button.
·	Click on the Save List button to save to file.

Filtering the customer list
Last sale parameters
You can use these options to select only those customers
·	ignoring when their last sale was ( All radio button ),
·	who have made sales since a selected date,
·	who have had no sales since a selected date
Select the appropriate radio button in the Last Sale Parameters box and then select the date.
Click on the Apply Sales Filter button to apply the filter.

Filters
You can also filter on:
·	customers  whose postcodes start with the same number,
·	debtors only,
·	mail-order customers only,
·	customers with email addresses,
·	customers without email addresses.

Manually selecting
Double click on the Use checkbox against a customer in the browser to de-select them. When you save the list, only customers with Use selected will be saved.
Alternatively you can use the contact menu to do this. The context menu will also allow you select/de-select all above and all below and selected record.

Sorting
You can sort the browser and the report by:
·	customer key ( default sort ),
·	surname
·	full customer name
·	post code.
To perform the sort, click on the required column heading in the browser.


.topic 2230
This module is an aid  to users who are putting together catalogues or promotional material. It allows the user to create lists of products and then export the list (and a selection of product fields) as a comma-delimited file (csv file) for importing into other programs such as Excel or Word. The window provides plenty of shortcuts for assembling  your lists and you can also save the list as a dbf file fro later use. You can add an unlimited amount of text against each title and automatically import text from either the title abstract field or from a blurb imported from the publisher.

Using the Catalogue window

Open the catalogue window from the main menu - select Modules/Catalogues.

Building a list of products
There is a variety of ways to create a list of products.

Import from a department, category, subject or imprint
Select a department category, subject or imprint in the appropriate combo box on the left side of the window.
Click on the Add to Browser button.
The products will be added to the browser. The program will check for items already in the browser, so you don't have to worry about duplications.
If you change your mind (before you add the products) you can clear the combo box fields by clicking the Clear Combos checkbox.

Adding product one by one
Add products by entering the barcode (or using one the standard shortcuts) in the barcode field.
You can use the F2 button to search for a product and the Recall (Alt F2) button to load the last used product.
Again, the program will ensure you don't duplicate a product in your list.

BestSellers
You can calculate what your best-selling products are and add them to yuor list.
Click on the Best Sellers button. This will open the Create Best Seller List window.
Select the sales date range you require.
Click on one of the radio buttons to select either best sellers by quantity or your most profitable products.
Enter the number of records you want to import.
Click the OK button.
The best sellers will be added to your list.
Again, the program will ensure you don't duplicate a product in your list.

Product data

When you add products to your list the following fields are copied from yuor master file:

Product code (ISBN)		Title			Author
Binding				Selling price		Abstract*
Jacket path			Imprint			Dimension (eg 250 x 150 x 25mm)
No of pages			Release date		Publisher
Subject

If you already have a list, you can update it for any changes in the title master (except for the abstract and product code fields). Just click on the Update from Master button to perform the update.

*In your catalogue file there is a text file where you can store any text about the product that you wish. When adding products to the browser you can choose to automatically fill this text field from either the abstract field on the Catalogue tab on the title master or from the Blurb/Reviews field on the Publisher tab or from neither of these.The Abstract combo box you can set one of the following:
		None (don't load any abstracts)
		Use master abstact
		Use publisher review
		Publisher review if no master abstract
		Master abstract if no publisher review.
The combo box will be set to whatever you used last time (on that computer).

Editing the abstract field
Double click on the Abstract column of the browser.
The Catalogue Abstracts window will open. This will display the title master abstract and the blurb/reviews field from the publisher tab in the top text fields. The lower text field displays the catalogue file abstract.
You can append or overwrite the catalogue file abstract by clicking on the Append or Overwriet buttons.
You can also just type in (or cut and paste) any text you want.
Click on the Save button to save youy cahnges to the Catalogue file. To discard yuor changes click the cancel button.
Note that any changes you type in the upper text fields are not saved back to the title master.

Saving and loading files

Saving a catalogue list
Once you have assembled a list of products you can save it for later use.
Just click on the Save List button and save the file as you would for any Windows file.
The file will be saved with an extension of .dbf.

Clearing the Browser
To clear the browser, click on the Clear Browser button. warning - any unsaved data will be lost.

Loading a List
To load a list click on the Load List button and navigate to the dbf file you wish to open (not the csv file!).
You may need clear the browser first before the Load List button appears.

Export field selection
You can select which of the fields (listed above) that you wish to export to the csv file. The default for new users is all fields;  teh program will then remember which fields you chose last time (on that computer).
Click on the Field Selection button. This will open the Field Selection dialogue window.
Use the arrow buttons or double click on a field name in the one of the list boxes to select ot deselect it.
Click Save to save your changes or Exit to discard your changes.

Creating the CSV file
To create a comma-delimited text file, click on the Export button and save the file as you would for any Windows file.
The file will be saved with an extension of .csv.							

.topic 194
Customer rewards, or loyalty, programs are a popular way of encouraging repeat sales. Bookscan allows you to define a rewards program. It will track your sales for each customer and allow you to reward your customers based on these sales.

Rewards programs are set up and managed in the Customer Rewards window which is opened from the main menu: Modules/ Rewards. This window contains five tabs:

Select rewards program
This is a simple browser allowing you to select which rewards program you wish to work on. If you are using only the one rewards program you won't need to refer to this tab. Otherwise just click on the required program and then select the tab you wish to work in.

Define reward programs tab
This tab allows you to set up or amend a rewards program.

Program specials tab
This tab allows you to enter different items to give special prices on these items for members of a reward program.

Payment/contract tab
This will be your main working area in rewards. Here you create the list of customers to be rewards, produce your mail out list and customer letters and where you produce the rewards.

Old records
In this tab you can look at your rewards history and purge old records.
The browser can be filtered to display only customers rewarded on a selected date by selecting that date in the Date Rewarded field.

Customer Rewards: Define Programs

Rewards programs are defined in the Define reward programs tab of the Customer Rewards window.



There are buttons to add, save, delete and copy a reward program. Multiple reward programs are allowed.

Adding a program
Click on the Add Program button and complete the various fields:.

·	Program name: Give the reward program a name

·	Base criterion -> Base criterion for how the rewards program will calculate rewards. There are two options:
o	$Amount Net (Rewards will be based on the dollar amount of sales)
o	Purchase Quantity (Rewards will be based on the quantity of items sold)

·	Reward Percentage: This is the percentage of total sales that will be used to calculate the reward amount.

·	Round Reward To: As the final reward amount is calculated from a percentage, it may not be a whole dollar amount, so you can specify a dollar amount here that the final reward amount is rounded to.

·	Program Start: The start date of this reward program.

·	Program End: The last date of this reward program. If you want the program to go on indefinitely, then leave this blank.

·	Maximum rewarded: The maximum amount of sales that this program will reward.

·	Minimum rewarded: The minimum amount of sales that this program will reward. If using Instant Rewards this is the threshold at which "points", (the customer's previous purchase value), becomes redeemable as a reward in subsequent purchases.

·	Disqualify Stops: Set this flag to prevent customers who have been put on stop from being rewarded.

·	Include GST in reward: You can choose to base rewards amounts on sales either including or excluding GST.

·	Vouchers to be issued for rewards: If this is checked then vouchers will be printed as part of rewards processing.

·	Voucher report form: Set the path to the report form used to print the rewards vouchers. The voucher form can be modified to suit your company.

·	Membership type: The type of membership required for this rewards program. There are three types:
1.	Not required,
2.	Renew on program date,
3.	Renew each <n> months,
Select not required if you don't need membership to reward programs.
If you select option 2 or 3 then the "membership setup" button will be enabled.
In this case press the "membership setup" button, to enter the renewal date and cost of the membership fee as shown below.
	

·	Category selection: You can use Bookscan categories to define which customers are eligible for, or members of a reward program. The browser on the right of the window displays a list of all categories. Select those categories which define eligibility for the program. By default all categories will be selected. If you don't wish to use categories to define customer eligibility, tick the Ignore categories checkbox.

·	You can set selected customers to be always included (or always excluded) from a reward program. Click on the Always Include or Always Exclude button to set these.

·	Scan freebie: If you are giving away a product as part of the customer reward, enter the product code in this field.

·	There is also a check box. "When a sale qualifies under multiple programs pay highest"
·	As you can have multiple reward programs if a customer qualifies for multiple programs, then this check box will ensure that they get the highest reward amount. If this is unchecked then they will get the lowest amount.

Rewarding customers
Rewards are managed in the Payment tab of the Customer rewards window.

Step 1: Create a customer list
Select the start and end date of your rewards period and then click on the Prepare Rewards List button. This will do all the reward calculations based on the criterion that were entered into the Define reward programs tab.
Once this has finished, a list of customers who have qualified for rewards and how much they will be rewarded will be displayed in the browser

Step 2 Edit the rewards list
If you wish you and now edit the customer list by clicking on the appropriate buttons:

Remove Cust From List
Highlight a customer in the rewards list then click on this button to remove them so they will not be rewarded.

Mark Rewarded
This will mark all customers sales in the rewards list generated as rewarded. Used when you are starting a new reward program and you want to clear out the existing sales for customers.

Customer Detail
Highlight a customer in the rewards list then click on this button to show their detailed customer record.

Step 3 Print list
Here you can print a listing of the customers in the browser and/or create a mail out list of the customers listed.
If you are creating a mail out file you will need to name the file (and select the file path) in the field below the Mailout file checkbox. This is a standard Windows selection field. Click on the button at the end of the field and browse to the folder where you wish to store the file. Then either select a file there to overwrite or enter a new file name.

Step 3 Finalise
When you are happy with the generated rewards list, click the Produce Rewards button to produce the rewards reports and vouchers to reward your customers with.
When finalising is completed the browser will clear. You will now be able to review these records in the Old Records tab.




You can also view the rewards earned by an individual customer from the customer master window.



At the bottom left of the window is a button labeled "Memberships/Rewards".
This is also where memberships are updated for each customer. Once you have selected the desired customer click on the button to bring up the following window.



This will bring up a list of rewards programs that have been defined. Highlight the one you are interested in. You can then click on the "Update Membership" button to update their membership if required.

To view the sales for a particular customer, open the customer master window and search for the customer. Then open or go to the "Purchases Tab". This shows a list of items sold to this customer.
To view only the unrewarded sales check the "Unrewarded sales only" check box and you will only see the sales that have not been rewarded yet.
You can click the "Total" button at any time to total these individual sales and give a total amount.

Finally at the top of this tab is the label "Unrewarded portion of rewarded sales". When you reward a customer at a certain dollar amount, if the customers sales go over this amount then the difference will be carried over into this field and added to their next reward.

If "Unrewardsd sales only" is ticked, you can also see the totals of the customers rewardable sales, GST on those sales, quantity of items towards the rewards and unrewarded items.






.topic 1570

The Bookscan Academic module is used to assist in calculating order quantities for textbooks used in school, college and university courses.
It allows you to keep track of the following data:
Lecturers and the courses they teach
Textbooks and other materials used on the courses
Student numbers for  these courses.

Summary of the Academic module procedures	
Setting up a course
Attaching lecturers to a course
Maintaining student numbers and rolling over figures at year/semester end.
Course texts -adding and editing
Placing orders
Academic reports

In the following screen-shot an existing course has been selected, and its details are showing in the various fields.


Course Details
 An existing course can have been selected either from the drop-down list of the Select Course combo-box or by using the Find Course By Lecturer button.
The course details are displayed in the various fields.

Course History
In the Rolls box the history of student numbers for the course is displayed
Next to the faculty Phone number field is a combo-box by which you may choose to view either five or ten slices of history (these slices are nominally referred to as years but the figures can be rolled with whatever frequency is desired).
When the course is being set up, you may enter as much historical information as you prefer.  From that point forward, each time the course information is rolled the existing data will move one column to the right.

Lecturers
The list of lecturers attached to this course is displayed and edited  by clicking on the Lecturers (F5) button.

Course Texts
The textbooks set  for this course are displayed in the browser.
Current set texts are displayed on  a white background, recommended titles on yellow and deleted titles on cyan.
To edit any of these lines double click on the line in the browser. The browser will then be hidden and the titles detail fields displayed.
To save your changes and return to the browser view, click on the Save Text (F9) button.
You can also scroll through all the texts using the Previous (F11) and Next (F12) buttons. When you use these buttons any changes you make to a text will be saved.
To discard any changes, click on the Cancel button (or  press the Escape key )

Buttons
Add Course (F3)	Add a new course
Delete Course 	Delete a course
Edit Course (F4) 	Edit a course's details

Lecturers (F5)	To display or  attach lecturers to the course.
Edit Rolls (F2)	Edit the course's student numbers and history.
Title Details (F2)	To display the master record of the selected title.  The Courses tab of the master record can be used to show all courses on which the title appears.
Marketing (F7)	An on-screen report of sales by marketing type for the currently selected title.

Add Text (F9)	To add text(s) to the course.
Delete Text	To delete a text from the course.
Order Text (F11)	To transfer current order values to the Draft Purchase Orders or to zero the current order values.  Items added to the Draft are left on hold and show a source of "Te".
History (F8)	An on-screen report of the currently selected title's sales history.

Right mouse options
Edit Rolls 	Edit the course's student numbers and history.
Add Course 	Add a new course.
Change Course Code 	Change the course code of the currently selected course.
Delete Course 	Delete a course.
Reports 	Print one of the 5 available Academic reports.
Period Sales Display	Display a floating window of the currently selected text's sales history.

End of Year Rollover	Roll over the student numbers and history at year end.

Show all items 	Have the browser display all texts for a course.
Hide dropped items 	Have the browser display only current texts.
Show only dropped 	Have the browser display only dropped texts.


.topic 1580

Bookscan can be configured for either a retail environment or for a publisher or distributor. The main feature of the publisher/distributor configuration is the warehousing module:

Customer orders
Customer orders are entered into the Order Entry window to create picking slips. These can be printed in the warehouse via the picking window. When the stock is picked it is scan packed at the packing station using the Packing window. Customer labels are printed and then the invoice is created and despatched with the goods.
You can have the program automatically link multiple picking slips for each customer allowing you to process all the orders for customer and so saving you freight.
The Order Entry window also monitors credit limits, and controls small orders, either adding small order surcharges or holding small orders until further orders are received.
See the Order Entry chapter for details.

Backorders
Where there is insufficient stock to fill a customer order line, the product is backordered (as opposed to creating special orders in the retail system). As the backorder is created, so too is a draft purchase order for the product. As stock is received to cover backorders, backorders can be released in the Backorder release window.
Pricing backorders
You can arrange to have the backorders automatically re-priced to reflect the latest prices. This is a very handy feature when you want to take orders on a new title but don't yet have a price for it. It is also useful where you are bringing in products from overseas and, because of fluctuating exchange rates, you don't want to set a price until the stock arrives. See the Re-pricing backorders section below.

Picking Slip Control  The program has a picking slip control window allowing managers to monitor warehouse activity.

Packing
For the packing stations, the program has a dispatch window where you scan in each product as you pack it. This ensures the accuracy of your shipments. It also prints the carton labels, allows recording of the shipping details and charges, creates the invoice and, for EDI orders, electronically transmits the ASN (advanced shipping notes).
The program caters for Coles Myer central warehouse electronic invoicing and labeling.

Setting up carriers
Set up each of your carriers (road freight, air freight, post, etc). This will enable you to record the shipment method and the consignment note against each invoice. You may also wish to set up quasi-carriers such as "Pick up", "Delivered by rep"  and so on.

Customers with multiple branches (e.g Coles Myer)
Electronic orders from customers with multiple stores or DCS.






.topic 1590

The Picking Slip Control window is used by the store persons to print out picking slips. It is also used my managers to monitor the warehouse work that is outstanding.

The window is opened from the main menu: Modules/ Warehousing/ Picking.
The window header will display:
·	a summary of all unprinted picking slips and
·	a summary of picking slips that are being picked but have not yet been packed and invoiced.
The browser will default to listing all unprinted picking slips.

Printing picking slips
Select the picking slips to be printed and press the Print button (F12).
You can select picking slips in several ways:
·	A single line - click on it
·	Multiple lines - hold the Control key down and click on the lines required.
·	A group of lines - click on the first line while holding down the Shift key. Click on the last line of the group. All intervening lines will be selected.
·	First five lines - click on the Next 5 Lines button (F5). Clicking on the button again will add the next five to the selection.
·	Clearing selections - click on the Clear Selections button (F6) or just click on another line in the browser.

Held picking slips
Picking slips below the minimum order level are 'held" so that they can't be printed. Held picking slips display a tick in the Held column of the browser. You can manually set a picking slip as held by double clicking on it.

Reprinting
Click on the Show Printed button.  The browser will display all printed but uninvoiced picking slips. Select the picking slips and click the Print button (F12). Click on the Finish Reprinting button when finished.

Forward orders
Forward order picking slips are not displayed in the browser until the forward order date. Then they are treated as standard picking slips.
To display forward orders that are not yet due, click on the Forward Orders button (F8). The summary fields in the header will now show the totals only for forward orders.  The browser will display only outstanding forward orders.  The picking slips can be printed in the usual way. Click the Hide Fwd Orders button when finished.

Stopped Accounts
Picking slips for customers whose accounts are on stop are displayed in red in the browser. These picking slips will not print until the customer is taken off stop.


.topic 1600
Note: the steps below detail the procedures for packing standard orders. For orders for multiple stores, shipped to distribution centres (DCs), such as Coles Myer, the procedures are slightly different. See the Coles Myer section below details.

At each packing station, stores persons use the Packing window as they pack customer orders. As the barcode of order items are scanned, the program checks the product code and the quantity against the order. Packing labels are printed as each carton is filled. When finalising the order, the program will alert the packer to any items not yet packed. The packer has the option of add the missing items or, if they represent warehouse shortages, finalising the order. In this latter case, the program will backorder the missing items and the shortage flag on those products. As the order is finalised the invoice is created and printed for dispatch with the goods.

Detailed instructions
The Packing window is opened from the main menu: Modules/ Warehousing/ Packing.
·	In the Scan picking slip combo down box, scan the picking slip's barcode.
o	To consolidate a customer's orders into one shipment, just scan any subsequent picking slips for this customer.
o	The scanned slips numbers will be displayed below the drop down box.
o	The order lines for the picking slips will be displayed in the browser.
Note: You will not be able to process picking slips for customers who are on stop.
·	Press the F5 key to start packing (or click the Start button).
·	Scan the barcode of each product as you pack it into the carton.
·	The Quantity is automatically set to 1.
·	For multiple quantities you can overtype the actual quantity.
·	Press F12 to save the line (or click the Save Line button).



·	After you fill a carton press the F8 key (or click the Carton Full button). The Shipping Details window will open.
o	For the first carton, select the Carrier and enter the Consignment Note.  These will be displayed when this window is subsequently opened.
o	If a Freight charge is to be levied on the order, enter it here.
o	You may also record the weight and size of the carton if required for this carrier.
o	Click Save when done. A carton label to be printed and the carton number incremented.
·	When all items for the customer have been packed, click the Post button. If the order has been set for Invoice with goods, the invoice will now print. Otherwise the ASN will be created and electronically despatched to the customer.

Features
Unpacked items
If you try to post the order without it all being packed, the system will display a message 'Warning Not all lines have been packed. Backorder unpacked lines?'.
·	If have been unable to locate the stock and wish to send the rest of the order click on the Yes button.
o	The invoice will be created only for the lines packed.
o	All items not despatched will be place on backorder and the master file flagged as having a stock shortage.
·	If you click on the No button, the browser will display all the lines not yet packed. To resume packing press the F5 key (or click the All Lines button).

Unpacking from or adding to a carton
To edit what you have packed in a carton, click on the Edit Carton (F6) button. The contents of the current carton will be displayed in the browser.
·	To select a different carton use the arrow keys beside the carton number.
·	To edit an item double click on the line in the browser to copy it into the edit line.
·	Type in the corrected quantity
·	Press F12 to save the line (or click the Save Line button).
·	To resume packing press the F5 key (or click the Resume button).

Singles
If you just want to scan each item as you pack it without being able to type in multiple quantities, click on the Singles checkbox (or press the F9 key). This can speed up entering the data as there is then no need to press the F12 key after scanning each item.

Pallets
To palletise an order, click on the Palletise button.
The pallet number will be set to 1 and four pallet labels will be printed, to be placed on each face of the pallet after it is shrink-wrapped.
As each subsequent pallet is filled, click on the Next Pallet button.


.topic 176
In the Order Entry window, if there is insufficient stock to fulfill an order line, a backorder is created. As stock subsequently becomes available, you will need to release these backorders, creating picking slips for the orders.
The Backorder Release window is accessed via the main menu - Modules/ Warehousing/ Backorder Release.
Note that it is best to maximise window when you are working on it to display all the columns of the browser.
The window displays a browser listing all backorders in the system



Backorders are released against the oldest order first until all available stock is exhausted. However you can prevent individual backorder lines being released (setting them as Held), release backorders only for a selected customer or product code and restrict the maximum quantity to be released to any one order.

Backorders Browser
The browser displays the customer backorders in the system.
There are three methods of filtering the browser display:
·	All backorders
·	By customer: display only backorders for the customer selected in the Customer field.
·	By product: display only backorders for the product selected in the Product field.

Sorting the browser
Where all backorders are displayed, you can change the sort order by clicking on the appropriate column header.
·	Pick number			Sort chronologically
·	Date				Sort chronologically
·	Customer key or customer	Sort in customer key order
·	Product code or Title		Sort by product code

Releasing backorders
Backorders are released by clicking on the Release (F12) button. Backorders will only be released for those lines displayed in the browser.
So if you have displayed backorders only for a particular customer, then only that customer's backorders will be released.
Similarly if you are showing only backorders for a certain product then clicking on the Release (F12) button will release backorders only for that product.
If All Backorders is selected then a full backorder release will be done.

Holding backorders
To prevent a backorder from releasing, you can set it to held by double clicking its checkbox in the Held column. To remove the flag double click on the check box again.
To hold all displayed lines, right click to display the context menu and select Hold All.
To remove the held flag on all lines in the browser, click on the Remove Held button.
To remove the held flag on all lines, not just those in the browser, check the Remove all Held checkbox and then click on the Remove Held button.
An example: you want to release a single line for a customer.
	Move the cursor to the Customer field and enter the customer key.
	Right click on the browser and select Hold All.
	Double click on the Held checkbox of the line you want to release
	Click on the Release (F12) button

Stopped Accounts
You can prevent stock being allocated to order for accounts which are on stop by checking the No allocation to customers on stop check box.

Linking
If the Consolidate Pick Slips option in System Options has been selected, released backorders will be automatically linked to any unprinted picking slips for that customer.
If the total value of these orders is below the minimum order value, the released order will be flagged as held.
If the released order brings the total value of the customer's current orders to above the minimum, then all the held flags will be automatically removed and the customer orders will be available for despatch.

Backorder reports
There are three backorder reports that can be run from this window and from Bookscan Reports. These are:
·	All Backorders (sorted by date, customer or product code).
·	For a single customer.
·	For a single product code.
To run the reports, select either the customer or the product to display them in the browser and then click on the Print (F11) button.
For all backorders, select the All Backorders option. If required change the sort order by clicking on the appropriate column header (as outlined above). Then click on the Print (F11) button.

Handling backorders where there is insufficient stock
Where there is insufficient stock to cover all backorders for a certain product, you can limit the maximum quantity to be allocated to each order.
Select the product in the product field and then enter the maximum quantity you want allocated any order in the Max/Order field. Then click on the Release (F12) button.

Stock availability
You can set a stock availability message against each status type in the Status Maintenance window (main menu: Tables/Authority tables/ Status).
The message will print as the backorder reason against each backorder line of your invoice. For example you may set 'temporarily out of stock' against the 'active' status.
In the Backorder release window, this message displays in the Out of stock reason column of the browser. Next to it is a column showing the due date from your supplier.
You can edit these fields by double clicking on them. This opens the Availability pop-up window.





.topic 182

You can arrange to have the backorders automatically re-priced to reflect the latest prices. This is a very handy feature when you want to take orders on a new title but don't yet have a price for it. It is also useful where you are bringing in products from overseas and, because of fluctuating exchange rates, you don't want to set a price until the stock arrives

Summary
·	A title's re-price flag is set to true in the title master.
·	Backorders are taken against the product with the customer's discount set.
·	When the items come into stock, the price is set in the title master.
·	Then when the backorder is released, the pricing on the orders will be calculated based on the new price and the discount set when the order was taken..

Detailed steps
Title Master window
In the Extra Fields tab of the Title Master window, tick the Re-price field .
If you wish, you can also set the Auto lift re-price flag. This will cause the Re-price flag to be automatically removed as soon as stock for it is received. If the product is a new release you would set the Auto lift flag to true. If the product is regularly brought in from overseas and re-priced each time as the exchange varies, set the flag to false.
(You can set the default setting of this flag in the Titles tab in System Options).

Order Entry window
When entering backorders for products flagged as "re-price", a red message will be displayed in the edit line Re-price on release.



Just save the line as you normally would and it's all done - the backorders will be flagged to be re-priced during backorder release.
However there are a few options:
·	If  you can partially supply a line (e.g. the order is for ten and you have three in stock), you will be given an option when saving the line, of whether you want the backorders re-priced or sent out at the existing price.
·	If you re-set the Sell price then the pricing and discounts used will be those you type in - in other words the re-pricing is cancelled.
·	If you leave the Sell and Price fields as they are and just amend the Discount field, then that discount will be taken off the re-priced amount. (For example, if you enter a discount of 10% and the product is later re-priced at $100, then the customer will be charged $90).
·	If you amend the Price field and leave the Sell and Discount fields as they are then, the product will be charged at that price, although the RRP will be later updated.

Receiving window
When stock is received, the price will be set or updated in the title master.
If the Auto lift re-price flag has been set, the Re-price flag will be removed against the product.

Backorder Release window
During backorder release, the prices will be re-calculated to exactly the same values as if you were now entering the order in invoice entry.

Warning
Information on re-pricing is stored in the picking slip tables. Setting a titles re-price flag after the backorder has been created will not cause it to be re-priced. The flag must be set at the time the order is first entered.


.topic 2720
The bin locations option forms part of the warehousing module. It allows you to map your warehouse locations using different bin types (say standard, oversize, fast sellers etc) and automatically assigns an appropriate picking location to each product as it is received into stock. After receiving stock, you can print a 'putting away' report listing the received stock against it locations. Note that the location mapping is one to one: one location per product.

Setting up
System Options
In System Options, select the Options tab and tick the "Use bin locations" checkbox.

Location Types
Open the Bin Location Types window from the main menu: Tables/Authority Tables/Warehouse Bin Locations/Bin Types.

The default bin location (which has an empty bin code field) will have been automatically created for you.
Add any other bin types you require.
If some bin types are related to the shape of the product, you should map the bin type against the binding. In the example below, the binding  'Outsize' has been mapped against the 'OS' bin type code.



Now when automatically allocating a bin for products with this binding, it will be an 'Oversize' bin.

Warehouse bin location mapping
Warehouse locations should be in the format row, shelf and column slot: 99XX-9. For example 04AG-3 represents the bin in row 4, shelf AG and column slot 3.
Now you need to set up your warehouse bin locations.
Excel method
The easiest way to do this is to set them up in Excel in two columns: bin number and bin type. This way you can use Excel's auto-numbering and other features.



The above example shows locations standard (or default) locations 77QQ-1 and  77QQ-4 .and. oversize locations 77QQ-2 and  77QQ-3.
To load the file open the Warehouse Mapping window from the main menu: Tables/Authority Tables/Warehouse Bin Locations/Bin Locations. Click on the Load CSV File button and select the csv file. The BinLocs table will fill with the bin locations and display in the browser on the left.


Alternate method
You can create locations in the Bin Locations window.



Enter the row, shelf and slot ranges and select the required bin type. Press the Create Location button. The BinLocs table will fill with the range of bin locations and display in the browser on the left.

Editing locations
The bin locations browser can display in three modes: all, allocated and unallocated.
Unallocated view
When unallocated locations are displayed you can:
Delete locations - select the locations in the browser and click the Delete Locations button.
Change the bin types- select the locations in the browser, select the new bin type in the left-side combo box and click the Change Bin Type button.

Allocated view
When unallocated locations are displayed, the right side browser is enabled and displays the selected location's product. You can remove the product from the location by clicking the Remove ISBN button.

Allocating stock to a location

Master file
You can manually add, change or remove a location in the Details tab of  the Title Master window.



The Locations field is read only. To edit it, press the F3 key to open the Bin Allocation window.
Editing a location
For title that have already been allocated a bin number, the Bin allocation window will display the following:
The current location.
The bin type of that location.
A browser listing the available locations for the selected bin type. Changing the bin type in the Bin Type combo box will change the display in the browser.
The 'Move to' field will display the first available alternate location if you wish to move locations.
As you select a different bin in the browser, it will display in the 'Move to' field.
To change the location to that field, click the Save (F12) button.
To remove the product from the current location, select the Remove from location (F5) button and click the Save F12 button.

Allocating a location
If a title has not been allocated a bin location:
The current location will display 'No location set'.
The Bin Type field will display the bin type mapped against that product's binding. It it is not mapped the default bin type will be displayed.
A browser lists the available locations for the selected bin type. Changing the bin type in the Bin Type combo box will change the display in the browser.
The 'Move to' button will be re-captioned to 'Set Location' and the text field will display the next available location for the selected bin type.
As you select a different bin in the browser, it will display in the 'Set Location' field.
To allocate the product to the selected location, click the Save (F12) button.

Receiving
In the Receiving window, bin locations will be automatically allocated against unallocated products. You can also re-allocate a bin location here.



When a product code is entered in the edit line, the program check if a bin had been allocated to that product. If it has, it displays the location in the 'Loc' field; otherwise it allocates the next available location mapped against that product's binding and displays it in the 'Loc' field.

You can change this location by pressing the F11 key and opening the Bin Allocation window. As in the Title Master you can allocate a different location by selecting it in the Available Locations browser.

In the example above, the product's binding has been mapped against the default bin type. To allocate it to a 'fast movers' bin instead, select 'Fast movers' in the bin type combo box. The Available Locations browser will change to display 'fast movers' locations only and the Set Location field will display the first available bin. Click the Save Changes (F12) button to allocate the product to the new location.

In the main, stores people can ignore the Locations field and let just let the computer do the allocating.

Receiving EDI Orders
In the Receiving EDI Orders window, bin locations will be automatically allocated against unallocated products. If you wish you could change the alloaction after saving the stock into the receiving window or change it in the Title Master.

Putting away lists
After receiving stock, and before posting it, you should print putting away lists. This will list all the received stock from a selected supplier, in bin location orders:


The report can be printed from the main menu: Purchasing/Receiving/Receiving Reports/Putting Away Report. You can also print it when printing the GRN report.










.topic 1610

 The pre-pack module assists booksellers in partnering schools to deliver required books (and other items such as stationery and school levies  -  collectively known as booklists) to the students.

Select Modules\Pre-pack, from Bookscan's main menu, and you will open a submenu of six items.  They represent the major components of developing, selling, ordering and being paid for book-lists.  They will be disabled if the module is not installed.  To install Pre-pack, select System Options from the Utilities menu.  (The option is grayed out if any other Bookscan windows are opened.)  Click the tab with the caption Modules and check the Pre-pack control.  Checking it will enable some other controls which help to tailor the way that Pre-pack operates.

The pre-pack submenu gives you access to these components:

	A:	Developing book-lists.
	B:	Logging the students' requests.
	C:	Packing and invoicing
	D:	Ordering
	E:	Pre-pack Reports
	F: 	Year Rollover

		Entering a book-list

.topic 1620


Select System Options from the Utilities menu.  (The option is grayed out if any other Bookscan windows are opened.)

Click the tab with the caption Modules and mark the Pre-pack check-box.  This will enable the following controls:

Generate Item IDs automatically:  When a Title is included in a book-list for the first time, it can be given an unique ID Number (some sellers call it a Stock Number) to provide a means of identifying it (more rapidly than having to data-enter the ISBN).  Once this has happened, any ISBN-input will find that title if the ID-Number entered is prefixed by "<" (no quotes).  Bookscan will generate sequential numbers if this option is checked, otherwise you will be prompted to enter your own each time one is needed.

Treat Students as Debtors:  If you process thousands of students and don't want them to clutter your debtor reports, leave this unchecked!  In the  current version this setting only affects student identification in the customer table.

Reward Pre-pack Sales:  By default, the customer-loyalty processes ignore sales made through pre-pack.

Discount for 2nd-hand in good condition:  Defaults to 33.33%.

Discount for 2nd-hand in poor condition:   Defaults to 66.66%.

.topic 1630

Every item (aside from comments) which goes into a book-list must have a corresponding entry in the master table (i.e.: effectively, it must have an ISBN).  You may wish to enter all of these before you start.

You may also wish to enter school details in advance (which can be done by selecting Tables\Authority Tables\Schools from the main menu) but they can be added "on the fly" from the book-list building window.  Similarly, you may prefer to enter the Subjects and their Key Learning Areas in advance.  The main-menu selection for this is just above the one for Schools.

With the preliminaries out of the way, begin entering a book-list by identifying the school (either from the combo-box or by clicking the button to its right, marked with ellipsis, by which new schools can be added).

Then enter the class' year-number.  As you tab off that field, Bookscan will point out that the class does not yet exist (unless it does  -  in which case you will see the list's items in the bottom, "browser" pane) and will offer to create one.  With that done you can start clicking the big, "Add Item To List" button.



The (above-mentioned)Item window can be reopened at any time by double-clicking on the row for the item that you wish to edit excepting that, if you double-click certain columns, there are special results.  The columns with their own results are:
	Seq:	 	Adjust the item's position in the list.
	C:	 	Edit a comment attached to the current line.
	Subject: 	Change the item's subject.
	Order:    	Edit the pending-order quantity.

Most commands that you will need, to assemble the lists, are found on the right mouse-button's pop-up menu.  Its two submenu's (one for Items and one for Book-lists) have most of the goodies.  Under Items you can delete any line no longer needed.  And you can add a line which is just a comment.  This sort of comment is not restricted to the "Comments" column, when the book-list is printed.  It comes out much as it appears on-screen.  Note that comment-lines should be given the same Subject as their adjacent item in order to help with the logical grouping of lines by the reports.

Under Book-lists (on the mouse-menu) you can Delete a whole book-list, Copy one (to a new School/Year), jump to the Ordering window or Re-sequence the current list.  Re-sequencing is important once the book-list has been completed and the items sorted into their final order.  Never re-sequence a list after you have started taking orders for it!

After entering a draft list, run the Worksheets report.  This provides a listing of what you understand the school's requirements to be and features plenty of white space (and prompts) for their further comments.  The report can be sorted by Key Learning Area, for convenient distribution to course masters.  The reports can be selected by clicking the printer icon on the tool-bar.

The Report Form input (above the "browse" pane) allows advanced users to produce varied output for different schools (or even different years within a school).  To do this, you must make a copy of the default report-form (the file named in the edit control), under a different name, and save that new name using the edit-control.  Each booklist takes on, as its default, the form assigned to its school.  Any form can be changed using the standard reporting tools provided by Barcode Solutions.

Once the work-sheets are returned, and the final changes put in place, re-sequence the list and you are ready to print it, for distribution to students.

	Logging the students' requests

.topic 1640

Pre-pack ordering can be approached in a number of ways.  The order-arrangement window can be opened from either the Book-list or the Students Maintenance windows.  It can also be opened from the Unders/Overs report-preparation window or from Bookscan's main menu.

Once you are viewing the book-list item of interest, double-clicking in the Next Order column lets you enter the number to be ordered.

The Ordered column displays how many unit-orders have been finalised for this item so far (this year).  The Avail column shows how many units are on-hand and the last column recalls the previous year's order.

The selection controls, at the top of the window, can vary each others' effect.  For example, if we clicked the AXIOMS OF GEOMETRY FOR CALARE PUBLIC SCHOOL Year 4 radio-button and then checked the View/order across all book-lists check-box, the list would show that title wherever it was used by any book-list.  If we then looked at the higher radio-button, its caption would read All items for all booklists.  If you pressed that button, all items on all book-lists would be listed.  You could limit this selection by item-type as well, using the Item-type combo-box (to view only books or just stationery).  Single-clicking (selecting) an item from the list changes the lower radio-button prompt to pertain to that title  -  so these controls combine to give access to any item on any list and to group related orders smartly.

Displaying the group (or single item) that you need to order, click the Place Order button.  Bookscan asks for confirmation of the order before proceeding.




For single titles across multiple book-lists, you can employ the Order On All Lists button to avoid entering Next Order quantities line by line.  This can be well-suited to stationery orders, which are not as likely to require separate store locations (identified, when printing the GRNs, by whatever goes into Store Notes).
For single titles you can also change the supplier you wish to order from.

Users must be aware that if there is stock on hand, those quantities will be placed on hold for pre-pack and the order-quantity reduced by the same amount.  (To have the stock that are being held for pre-pack subtracted from Bookscan's calculations of stock availability you must set the appropriate formula under Utilities\System Options, on the Titles tab.)

The Report Received button gives a summary of ordering and receiving for the selection.  For a more comprehensive view into the ordering equation, open the Overs And Unders reporting window.

	Reporting Overs And Unders

.topic 1650

This window gives detailed insight into the ordering status of everything on your book-lists.  If the window opens showing no items, click the List button.

Any combination of the listed items can be selected and placed into the draft purchase orders (using the Order Selection button).

If a selected line is showing quantities in the DRAFT PrePack column then the Edit Qty in draft button will be enabled.

The list can be filtered using the radio button group, and also by the combo-box (which can limit the view to Overs-only, Unders-only or only items which have a pre-pack order already drafted).


The figure in the OnHand column will vary in accord with the in-house method of calculating available stock (see Utilities\System Options, on the Titles tab).

The Print button gives a hard-copy of the information and closes the window.

	Other Pre-Pack Reports

.topic 1660

You can close the (pre-pack) year once you have collected all monies owed by students.  The only alternative is to delete unpaid-for lines from the record of the student's order.

Selecting this option from Bookscan's main menu, you can proceed either to close all schools or to close just one.  It clears out student orders and rolls statistical data into history.

.topic 1670

This integrity check is a part of Bookscan which enables for the re-calculating and validating of different figures within the system to ensure data accuracy. There is also the option to change the 'case' of text of titles names and authors in the title master.

 Ensure that all terminals are back to the main menu.

To open the integrity check on the main menu click Utilities->Database Utilities->Integrity Check.



The options, as listed in the image above with their explanation are:

· Stock fields - The fields this includes are: Backorders, Special Orders, On approval, Conignment out, Total on Order, Consignment In, Laybys.
· Update Bookscan records
· Customer/Supplier codes - This option checks for duplicate customer/supplier codes and if there are any the process temporarily stops and it displays a window where you can delete either/both or change the code of either/both.
· Delivery addresses
· Change case of master - This option allows you to change 'case' of Titles and/or Authors so there is a consistency within the title   master.

The other options relating to changing of Title and/Or Author letter case format.
These are the fields from the Title Master and require the Change case of master option to be selected.
		The two settiings are Upper Case (all letters in captials) and Proper Case (first letter of each word is a captial letter).
	Both Author and Titles can be updated at the same time.

Step 1
It is advised that you run a re-index before running the Integrity Check.
