For information on how to run a re-index, click the link.

Step 2
Select the desired options by clicking on the boxes, as Stock fields has been done in the above image.
Then click the OK button.

Step 3
Click Yes button (Having run a re-index in step 1)
If Step 1 not completed click Cancel and return to step 1.

Step 4
While running it will prompt you with the results of each option selected.
Click OK button.
If it prompts to confirm the checking of records, click Yes button.
Note: Running the integrity check can take some time depending on database size.

Step 5
When finished it will notify you that it has been completed and click OK button.

When completed it will display DONE next to the option in the list and update in blue font the date the check was last run for items.

.topic 1680

Summary of stocktake procedures
Below is a summary of the steps taken to conduct a full stocktake. You can also do stocktakes by department.

Step 1: Data error report
Check the accuracy of your prices and margins:
·	Print a Data Error report and use it to correct erroneous data in the title master.
Details....


Step 2: Prepare the stock
Prepare for an accurate stock count:
·	Check outstanding approval and consignment stock.
·	Quarantine finalised returns.
·	Quarantine unprocessed goods inwards stock
·	Ensure all stock is barcoded.
Details..

Step 3: Portable scanners
·	Prepare the portable scanners.
Details...

Step 4: Prepare the database
·	Cease all stock movement.
·	Re-index the database.
·	Print a Stock Valuation report.
·	Run a Database Integrity Check.		    			
·	Set the Bookscan to stocktake mode.
 Details....

Step 5: Count the stock
·	Counting at a terminal.    Details....
·	Counting with portable barcode scanners.  Details....
·	Downloading counts from portable scanners Details....		

Step 6: Check the accuracy of your count
·	Use the Unders and Overs reports and the Error report to identify if and where to re-count.
·	Other stocktake reports.		
Details....

Step 7: Finalise the stocktake
·	Back up the database.
·	Update the stock figures.
 Details....
	
		

.topic 1690

Cease all stock movement
It is imperative that stock not be moved during a stocktake. It leads to double counting and counting stock that has not been posted to the database. Before proceeding further ensure that all selling, moving, receiving and despatching of stock ceases.

Re-index the database
To ensure that your database is in proper condition for the stocktake, re-index it:
·	From the main menu, select Modules/ Re-index Tables.
·	In the Re-index Tables window that opens, click on the Select All button and then the Re-index button.
·	Click Close when done.

Stock valuation report
This one page report will provide a snapshot of your pre-stocktake stock holding. It gives total figures and is useful in reporting the stocktake results.
·	Open Bookscan Report.
·	Select the report Title/Valuation/All.

Database integrity check
This routine will cross-check the accuracy of certain files used in conjunction with stocktaking. In particular, it checks the approval information stored against each title.
·	From the main menu, select Modules/ Stocktake/ Integrity.
·	In the Integrity Check window that opens, ensure that the Stock Fields option is checked.
·	Click on the Start button.

Setting the system to stocktake mode
This is the last step to be taken just before the counting starts. It does two things:
·	Zeroes the Last Stocktake field on every title record.
As you count your stock, the count is added to this field.
The stocktake count remains stored here until this step is run again at the next stocktake. This is why you can print stocktake reports (except Overs and Unders) at any time until the next stocktake.
·	Sets an internal flag that indicates a stocktake is now in progress. This flag allows you access the stocktake counting screens.
From the main menu, select Modules / Stocktake. This opens the Stocktake window on the Prepare tab. Select Full Stocktake in the combo box and click on the Prepare button.




             Summary    Next step

.topic 1700

Check outstanding approvals
Stock out on approval or consignment is counted as part of your inventory and so should be reported as accurately as possible. The system will add all live approvals and consignments to your stocktake counts when finalising the stocktake. To ensure your records are accurate, print a summary report of outstanding approvals and consignments and check that your data is up to date.

From the main menu, select Sales / Approvals / Print.

For a detailed report,
·	Click on All,
·	Ensure the Include approvals that have been filled box is unchecked.
·	Click OK.

For a shorter report,
·	Click on Summary
·	Chose the Sort by Date option from the drop down list.
·	Click OK.

Review the report and delete, return or invoice approvals which are not current.
Note that deleting an approval returns the item to stock.

If you are using the consignment out module, repeat all the above for consignment stock.

Quarantine finalised returns to supplier
·	If you have damaged or faulty items waiting to be returned, or any approved returns, finalise them and despatch them to the supplier. This saves having to count them.
·	Cut off processing returns inwards at least a day ahead of the stocktake.
·	Separate finalised and unfinalised returns.
·	Finalised returns must not be included in the stocktake. If you cannot despatch them now, quarantine them so that they are not counted.
·	Returns to supplier which have not been finalised must be counted.

Quarantine unreceived stock
·	Cut off processing goods inwards at least a day ahead of the stocktake.
·	Goods received after this time must be quarantined and not counted in the stocktake.
·	You should also clear any parked receipts in the Receiving window.
·	Ensure all stock received before the cut off time is processed, put away and posted.
·	In the 'list' view of the Receiving window, check for goods that have not been posted into stock. Before the stocktake can commence, this list must be emptied. Investigate each line and either post them or delete them.

Check that all stock is barcoded
To ensure that the stock counting proceeds smoothly and that it is accurate, it is imperative that all stock is barcoded. Counting stock without barcodes is very slow and very prone to error.
Well in advance of the stocktake, check your stock. Print and attach barcode labels for any items that require them.

           Summary    Next step

.topic 1710

Metrologic Scanner Setup

Metrologic scanners can be hired from Barcode Solutions. Contact the help desk to book them.

Scanner Pre-setup Checks
Before using Metrologic scanners, you will need to ensure that you have a computer that uses Bookscan that has a USB port. This is the port/cable type that is required when using this scanner type. Without this the scanners cradle/docking  bay will not be able to talk to your system.
Ideally this should be a PC that will not be switched off during the stocktake; the server is usually the best option.

Hardware Checklist
When you receive the scanner firstly you will need to ensure that you have the following;
·	Scanner Cradle/Docking Bay
·	Data Cable (that connects to a COM/Serial port on the pc)
·	Power Cable(will have a box connected on part of the cable)
·	Scanner(s) (this will be at least one, but could be any number of scanners depending on what was hired)
·	Installation CD-ROM Disc

Hardware Setup
1.	Only one PC is required to install the scanner cradle and software for stocktaking.
2.	Turn off the PC that the cradle is going to be connected to.
3.	Connect the USB cable into the back of the cradle (the part that connects is a long rectangular shape, roughly 15mm x 3mm).
4.	Then plug the other end into the USB port of the computer.
5.	Connect the power cable into the back of the cradle. (Small,  round  plug, slightly thinner than a pen with a silver tip/end).
6.	Plug the other end into the power point.
7.	Turn on the PC (allow it to start and log in to Windows as normal). Insert the CD for Optimus SP5500.
8.	Place a scanner into the cradle (buttons facing forwards)
9.	Cradles are used for communication (sending files or receiving files between PC and Metrologic) and charging you Metrologic batteries.
10.Install cradle device driver follow the steps:
Connect Metrologic OptimusS Cradle (Model: MI5500-614) with USB cable and power cable.
Make sure the Power light is on (Red color).
Plug in USB cable into PC and you will get the screen as displayed below:


Click radio button as 'Yes, this time only'. Click 'Next'.
Click button 'Install from a list or specific location  (Advanced)'. Click 'Next'
Check the box 'Search removable media  (floppy, CD-ROM...)'. Click 'Next' button.The  system  will  then  search  for  the  CIPHERLAB  308  USB-to-RS232  Interface converter.
After Interface Converter is found, the screen will be shown as below:


Just click 'Continue Anyway'.
After finish searching USB device driver, a complete install screen will be shown.
Right click mouse on Computer icon on Desktop. Select 'Properties'->'Hardware' tab->'Device  Manager'->Ports(COM  &  LPT).  Find  out  the  com  port  for Optimus USB  to UART Bridge controller (COM9). In this example, the com port is 9. It can be other than 9.

	11. Test each of the Scanners to ensure they are working. Do this as follows;:
Turn on the power of the PDT scanner.
Hold orange button on the middle of the scanner (this will activate the scanner laser). Do not scan anything; just ensure that the light comes on.
Press '.' to select the utilities option.
Key '1' for Browse Data, press Enter.
'No Data' message is displayed for a while.
Press 'ESC' to enter 'Stock Take Menu'.
Turn off the power of the PDT scanner.
Place the scanner back in the cradle and try the next.

Software Setup
1.	Once you have finished the previous section, and the cradle has been connected and the scanners appear to be working, we can now set up the software.
2.	Create a new folder in the Bookscan data directory on your local PC called 'StockTake'.E.g. C:\e-BilityData\Stocktake.
3.	Insert the CD-ROM Disc that was enclosed with the Scanner.
4.	View the contents of the CD through My Computer.
5.	There should be three things on the CD:
Help documents,
StkUtil.exe,
Setup.exe and installation files.
6.	Copy StkUtil.exe to C:\StockTake.
(Do this by clicking mouse to highlight this file, then right clicking on one of the file and select 'Copy' on the CD. Then in the target location, using the right mouse, select 'Paste').
7.	Use the mouse to highlight C:\e-BilityData\StockTake\StkUtil.exe and right click the mouse. Click [Send To] an then click [Desktop (create shortcut)]. An icon shortcut called StkUtil.exe - Shortcut will be created. Rename it to StockTake.
8.	You can run the program by double clicking on Stocktake icon. When this is done a window as shown below is opned. This window is used to upload your scanner stock counts to a csv file in your Stocktake folder.




           Summary    Next step


.topic 1711
Counting stock can be done using portable barcode scanners or at a PC running Bookscan.

Counting the stock at a terminal
Stock counting is done in the Count Stock tab opened from the main menu: Modules/ Stocktake/ Stocktake.




Select the Default Department from the drop down box (say STK Stocktake). Products without departments are allocated to this department. After the stocktake you can print a listing of these titles and correct them.

Click on the [F5] toggle button to switch between  SINGLES, entering one copy and QUANTITIES, entering multiple copies.
·	For single copy scans, every time a barcode is scanned the count is incremented by one. No need to key a quantity - scan the barcode and it's counted.
·	For entering multiple copies, scan the barcode. The barcode will be displayed and the cursor will move to the Quantity text box. Overtype the quantity and press the Enter key.

Correcting Errors
If you have entered an incorrect quantity, used a wrong ISBN or counted something twice, you can reverse the entry by entering a negative quantity:
·	Ensure that you are in multiple copy mode.
·	Scan the barcode of the item you wish to correct.
·	Type in a negative Quantity (eg -6) and press the Enter key.
 Note that the minus sign will not appear until after you type the first digit.

Stocktake Entry browser
The browser shows the titles already counted this session. The quantity fields are:
·	Progress	Cumulative total counted so far for this title.
·	On Hand	Quantity showing as currently in stock.
·	Qty		Number added to the count at that time.

Note: You can enter and exit the system as often as you like during a stocktake. The stocktake begins when you do a Prepare and ends when you've done a Finalise.  The count is stored in the Title Master not in the stocktake window.
However when re-opening the Stocktake Entry window, previous processing will not be displayed. So ensure you know what you last counted before losing the window.  The window can be closed, Bookscan & all PCs shut down.  When they are turned on again, the stocktake will continure from where you left off.

         Summary    Next step

.topic 1712
Note: Documentation will be supplied on disc with the scanners, please refer to that for the most relevent instructions for the scanners supplied.  There are different scanners available and they will come with drivers and instructions specific  to them.

When using portable scanners to count stock, you should try to do one section or gondola at a time. After counting a section, upload the file from the scanner and load it in to Bookscan. After uploading the scanner is automatically refreshed and is ready for counting the next section. This way, if an error is made or there is equipment failure, it will be easier to correct.


Counting stock using Metrologic scanners
1. Retrieve a scanner from the cradle. When you pick it up, the screen should turn on. If it doesn't, press the ESC button. The Stocktake menu of the scanner will display.
2. We need to ensure that the scanner's memory is empty before using it for the first time. To do this, press '.'  to enter 'Utilities Menu'. Then press '1' to enter 'Browse Data Menu'.  If  'No Data' is displayed, the scanner memory is empty.
3. Go back on the main menu and press 'ESC' to start the stocktake for this scanner.
4. Begin scanning products. To scan, squeeze the large buttons on the side of the scanner (the laser beam will come on).
Note:  If  there  is  a  quantity  to  be  scanned,  pressing  '-'  to  get  in 'Manual  Mode',  then  scan  the  barcode,  and  then on  the  scanner keypad key the quantity of the total item include the existing one. For example, your current quantity is 10, if you want to add another 4, you should  enter  14.  Press  Enter  key.  This  will  show  a  quantity  on  the screen against the products ISBN. The mode will then be changed back to 'Automatic Mode', which is increased by  1 for every scan. You can continue scanning the next item.
5.	Continue scanning all items.

Uploading you counts from Metrologic scanners
1.	When  yopu have finished scanning,  press '.' key on the scanner keypad. This will enter 'Utilities Menu' of the scanner. Pressing 3 to select  'Upload Data', and then press 'ENTER'. You will see a message 'UConnecting.'.
2.	Place the scanner back in the cradle.
3.	Return to the PC that the cradle is plugged into.
4.	Click on the Stocktake icon on your desktop to open the StkUtil program. The following window will now appear:


5. Set the 'Load Mode' to 'Upload data'.
6. Click 'Start' button.
7. A pop up window will open where you can enter a filename of your own choosing, for example Lookup08060301.CSV into your selected folder (e.g. C:\e-BilityData\Stopcktake).
8.	The data will start transmitting from the scanner to the cradle. The scanner will beep once when it has finished sending. On the screen of the scanner, it will indicate the number it items uploaded.
9. At this point you will know that the data has been received correctly. The data is automatically erased after a successful upload and the scanner is now ready for further counting.

Loading your stock counts into Bookscan

1.	From the main menu, select Modules/Stocktake/Stocktake. This opens the Stocktake Entry window.
2.	Select the Default Department from the drop down box (say STK Stocktake). Products without departments are allocated to this department. After the stocktake you can print a listing of these titles and correct them.
3.	Set File Fields to '2'
4.	Click Import from File button.
5.	In the pop up window that now displays, select the file saved in the folder C:\e-BilityData\Stocktake. The file name should be in the format:
6.	'Stocktake_todaysdate' or 'Stocktake_bayNumber'
Note: Please ensure you do not import a file for the second time; otherwise it will double up your figures for these titles.
7.	Click the Open button.
8.	A pop up window will display 'Import Complete' and display the total number of titles appended and the number of titles not found in the master file.
9.	A listing of these titles will be displayed when you click OK button. This list can be printed.
Note:  To print the report at a later time, right click in the Stocktake Entry window and select 'Reprint Exception report' from the drop down menu. Then select the DBF file in the window (which will have the same name as the file that was imported with unknown titles).
10.The titles that were appended should now be added to the active Stocktake window for you to view.
11.You are now ready to upload the next file.


         Summary      Next step

.topic 1720

Step 1 Data error report
The data error report is used for checking prices and margins. Correct pricing is necessary if you are to report accurate stock valuations. The report will list all titles with pricing and quantities outside the parameters you set; it will flush out errors such as scanning a barcode into a price field or setting a zero cost price.

Run this report well in advance of the stocktake, particularly if you are not in the habit of doing regular valuation checks. There may be many corrections you have to make.

From the main menu, select Reports to open Bookscan Reports where you select the report: Title/ Valuation/ Data Errors.

Set the parameters for your report:
·	Cost price less than
What is the lowest realistic cost price for most of your stock? If you wouldn't normally have anything less than $2.00, set the parameter at say $1.00. This will find anything with a zero cost price. Alternatively you may wish to set it to one cent to pick up zero cost prices.
·	Sell price greater than
If you wouldn't normally sell anything more expensive than say $120.00, but have a few items worth around $230, set this parameter at say $150. You can ignore the (correctly priced) high-priced items on the list and catch those wrongly priced.
·	On hand greater than
Use a quantity set just above your normal largest quantity. If you normally don't have more than 20 of something, set the parameter at 25. You might have 800 bookmarks but you can ignore them on the printout but catch a quantity set at say 97897.
·	Margin less than and Margin greater than
Again set the parameters wide of your normal margins. If you think you don't make less than 25% on anything and no more than 50%, then set these at say 15% and 70%.

Click the OK button to run the report. Review the report and correct any errors in the title master.


	   Summary    Next step

.topic 1730

Once you are satisfied with your count you are ready to complete the stocktake.

Backup your system
First, back up the database. This is essential to guard against catastrophic equipment failure.
From the main menu, click on Utilities/Database Utilities/Backup Data Files.
·	This will open the backup window.
·	The backup will be saved to the data directory. The previous backup will be overwritten. To save to another drive select the drive from the combo box.
·	Click on the Backup button.
·	All your data files and your reports will be zipped up and saved as the file ebbackup.zip. This may take some time depending on the size of your database.
·	Click on Exit when done.

Finalise the stocktake
Open the Stocktake Finalising window from the main menu: Modules/ Stocktake/ Finalise.
In the window that opens, click the OK button.
The system will open the master file and overwrite the stock on hand figures with the stock count figures.
The stocktake is now completed. You can now resume your normal activities.

               Return to the Summary

.topic 2460

The ticketing module allows you to sell and print tickets for movies, shows, events and conference rooms through the Bookscan POS. You can have multiple price points (say adult, child, concession or A, A reserve, stalls), set the number of seats for the room or theatre, take reservations and sell movie vouchers of a set number of tickets. In Ticketing Maintenance you can set up schedules of shows or movies, etc in different venues and with different price points.

Say you are showing a movie for two weeks in your theatre #2 with 200 seats, one show a day and an extra matinee on Sundays, but you are closed Monday and Tuesday. The ticketing module has wizards to help you quickly set up this schedule.

Ticketing is licensed functionality. Contact Barcode Solutions to purchase a license.

Setting up
First you will need to set up some default settings. Open the Ticketing Defaults window from the main menu: Modules/ Tickets/ Ticket Defaults:


Here you can set up your locations (theatres, conference rooms etc), default prices, show ratings and default session times, and set the days you are usually open.

Locations
Here you can set up your theatres or conference rooms, and you can allocate the number of seats for each venue.
Click the Add Location button:



Enter a Room/venue name (e.g. Theatre 2). Enter the seating/capacity (e.g. 200).
Click the Save button.
To edit or delete a room, double click on it in the browser.

Ticket prices
You can set your default prices here, though you set different prices for each show as you set up your schedule if you wish.
To add a default price, click the Add Prices button.
Enter the Price description (e.g. Adult) and the price (e.g. 25.00).
Click the Save button.
Don't set up price points for vouchers and complimentary tickets. These are built into the program.
To edit or delete a price point, double click on it in the browser

Show ratings
You can set show ratings against movie (say, G, PG, M, MA,R and X). A ratings can be set against each show and will print on the ticket.
To add a rating, click on the Add rating button.
Enter the Rating code (e.g. PG) and a description (e.g. Parental Guidance).
Click Save button
To edit or delete a rating, double click on it in the browser

Session times
You can set your default session times here. Later you can allocate different session to different shows as you set them up.
To add a session, click on the Add Session button.
Enter a session name (e.g. Matinee) and select a session time (e.g. 13:00:00).
If session times don't really apply to your situation, or the times vary considerably, just create a single session. When creating a show, you can use this session and just change the time).

Days Open
Say you are showing a movie for two weeks, one show a day and an extra matinee on Sundays, but you are closed Monday and Tuesday. When you are creating the schedule, the program will create a session for each show against which you can sell tickets. So the program needs to know which days you are open.



To set the days you are opened or closed, double click on the day in one browser to move it to the other.

Default quantity
This is a default ticket quantity for each purchase made through the POS. Set it to either 1 or 2.

Ticket message
You can have a message print on your tickets. These can be set individually for each show or movie and are usually used to promote a future show or movie. You can set the default message here.

When you have set up your defaults, click Exit button; any changes you have made will be saved when exiting.


Ticketing Hardware Setup

The ticketing module is set up to use the Star TSP847c Thermal TSP 800 series printers. Contact Barcode Solutions about using other printers for your tickets.

To setup the printer for ticket printer, install the printer in MS Windows as a normal. Set the printer name, say 'Ticket'. You can install the Star TSP847c printer's drivers that come with the printer, but it can also be installed as a generic text windows printer.

Now set the printer up in Bookscan. Open System Options and select the System Tab. Click the Other Print Settings button.  Enter the printer name ('Ticket' in the above example), into the Ticket Printer field. Click the OK button to save.


.topic 2670
Overview
For each movie, show or event (let's call them all 'shows' from now on), you will need to set up a product in the master file. Sales for every session of this show are recorded against this product code. So your ticket sales will be included in your standard Bookscan sales. As well you will be able to print reports showing how well your shows have succeeded.
For each show will you need to set a venue, set up a series of sessions and set the price points. This is done in the Ticket Maintenance windows where there are wizards to speed this up.
Then you are ready to start selling your tickets through the POS.

Departments and suppliers
Before creating tickets for the first time, you may wish to create an Bookscan department for shows. In the examples that follow, we will use a department called Tickets with a code TIX. You should also create a binding type (e.g. binding code 'MO', name 'Movies') for your show.

Bookscan requires that you set a supplier against each product. You will need to set up in the supplier master either a dummy supplier for your shows or, for movies, set up your distributor as a supplier.

Creating a show in the master file
For each show, you will need to set up a product in the master file. Open the Title Master window and click on the New F11 button as you normally would to set up any product.



You will need to set the following fields:
·	Product code
·	Title. You may also want to enter the director or principal star as the author
·	Product type as 'Ticket'.
·	Binding
·	Status
·	Department (as 'TIX' in our example).
·	Supplier

Set these flags to true:
·	Firm sale (so the program doesn't generate returns to supplier).
·	No discount
·	No special orders
·	Generic (turns off standard stock controlling – the ticketing module will control the number of tickets you can sell for each session).

Setting up your show sessions
Setting up your schedule of shows is done in the Ticketed Shows window which is opened from the main menu: Modules/ Tickets/ Ticket Maintenance. The window will show all your current shows in the browser on the left. Current shows are those for which you are now selling tickets. For the show selected in the browser, the schedule, prices and its other settings are displayed.



To add a show to the schedule, click on the Add Show (F12) button. The Title Master and Title Find windows will open. Find the show you want and press the Select button in the Title Master window to add it to the Ticketed Shows browser

For each show, you can add or edit a message to display in the tickets, set an audience rating and set the 'no printing' flag if you don't want tickets to print as they are sold at the POS.

To display both current and non-current shows, untick the 'Show only current' checkbox. The browser will display all shows and will add a third column: 'Cur'. Current shows will show a tick in the column. To toggle the currency of the show, double click the Cur column. Non-current shows will to be available for sale in the POS.

You can change the display order of the shows by using the Up (F3) and Down (F4) buttons. Moving your next-to-go show to the top of the list will cause it to be selected as the default in the POS.

Price Types
As you add each show to the browser, it will be allocated the default price points.

To add a new price point, click the Add Price (F11) button.

To edit a price point, double click on it. The Ticket Price window will open:



Overtype any field you want and click the Save (F12) button.
The Short Text and Text fields are what will print on the stub and long side of the ticket respectively.

You can change the display order of the price points by using the Up (F5) and Down (F6) buttons. Moving your most frequently sold price point to the top will cause it to be selected as the default in the POS.

Creating a Show Schedule
To create individual sessions for the ticket, click on the Build Schedule (F8) button. This will open the Ticketed Shows Schedule Creation window:



In this window you will create a session for performance of your show.
·	Set the first and last dates of your schedule.
·	Set the days there will be performances:
o	Every day: set this check if there are performances every day in you date range.
o	Single day: set this if there are just performances on one day. (This is provided as a short cut).
o	Set the recurring days: you may have performances say, every Wednesday to Friday over your date range.
o	Extra dates: click the Add Date (F7) button to add odd extra dates not within the date range or not on a selected recurring day.
o	Excluded date: click the Add Date (F8) button to exclude a date in the range. For example, you may have performances every Thursday of the year except Christmas Day.
·	Next add a session or sessions to the Default Sessions browser. Click the Add Session (F7) button to open the Ticket Session window:


o	Select a session from the Session Name combo box and a location from the Location combo.
o	You can alter the start time and the number of available seats.
o	Click Save (F12) to add the session. In our example above we will have two sessions a day
·	Now click the Build Schedule (F12) button to add each session to the Schedule browser. Repeat these steps for as many sessions and dates as required to build your schedule. If you get it wrong and want to start again, tick the Overwrite check box before clicking the Build Schedule (F12) button.

Example
An example may be useful here:
Say you want to schedule the play Wind in the Willows through January. There will be matinees on Wednesdays and Fridays in Theatre #1 and evening performances on Thursdays in Theatre #2.
·	Select 'Wind in the Willows' in the browser of the Ticketed Shows window
·	Click on the Build Schedule (F8) button to open the Ticketed Shows Schedule Creation window.
·	Set the Date range:  the First Day field to '1/01/2009' and the Last Day field '31/1/ 2009'.
·	In the Recurring Days field select Wednesday and Friday.
·	Click on the Add Session (F7) button. Select the session 'Matinee' and the location 'Theatre #1'. Save the session.



·	Click the Build Schedule (F12) button. The browser will load with matinee sessions in Theatre #1 for each Wednesday and Friday in January. The Default Sessions browser will clear.



·	Now change the Recurring days to Thursday and set the default session to 'Evening' in Theatre #2.
·	Click the Build Schedule (F12) button. The program will add to the schedule evening sessions in Theatre #2 for each Thursday in January.
·	Close the Schedule Creation window and you are finished.








.topic 2490

When the ticket module is enabled, a Ticket button will be displayed in the POS window.



Click on the Ticket button to open the Ticket sales window:


The browser on the left shows all the current shows.
The sessions and ticket prices are shown for show selected in the browser.
Note that the price points 'comps' and 'vouchers' are added by the program.

To sell some tickets:
Select the show.
Select the session,
Overtype the ticket quantities as required.
Click the Save (F12) button.
In the diagram above we are selling two adult and two child tickets to the matinee session of Wind in the Willows.

The sale line will be added to the browser of the POS.
Add any other sales (say from the candy bar).



Then just process it as a standard POS sale - click the Finalise (F12) button to open the Tendering window where you can accept your cash or process an EFTPOS payment. As the sale is completed a sales docket will print on the docket printer and the tickets on the ticket printer.

Options
Barcodes
Instead of using the Ticket button, you can enter the barcode of the show in the Barcode field. The Ticket Sales window will open with the show you scanned already selected. You may find it useful to print barcodes for each of your current shows and stick on a sheet of paper at each POS. Operator can then just can the required show instead of selecting it from the list.

Movie vouchers
You can set up movie vouchers in the Title Master window in a similar way to Bookscan tracked vouchers. The vouchers are good for a set number of shows. Customers can then use these to purchase tickets with the program keeping track of the number of shows left on the voucher.
In the Title master window, create your voucher product. Vouchers should have a barcode for the product and each voucher book should a voucher number unique to the product code. For example you have had printed a set of 1000 ten-ticket vouchers with a barcode of '46466565464' and numbered 1 to 1000. In the New Title window, set the:
·	Product Code field to '46466565464'.
·	Title to say, 'Movie voucher – 10 shows'
·	Product Type field to 'Ticket voucher'.
·	Ticket Voucher: Shows field (on the Extra fields tab) to 10.
·	Sell price.
You can set up as many movie voucher types as you like (e.g. 20-ticket voucher etc). To sell your books of vouchers, scan the barcode in the POS window. In the pop window enter the voucher number and proceed with the sale as usual.
When customers use their vouchers when purchasing tickets through the POS, select the 'Vouchers' price point. A pop up window will be displayed where you can enter the voucher number.

Reservations
If the customer has made a reservation for his tickets, process the sale as usual except that in the Ticket Sales window you should tick the Reserved Seats (F11) check box. When you click the Save (F12) button the Reservation window will open. Select the reservation and proceed as normal. This will cause the reservation to be cleared.



Note that the program will prevent you from selling more seats than are available in the selected theatre for that show. So it is important for popular shows to clear each reservation as the tickets are picked up.


.topic 2480

You can take customer reservations over the phone. Open the Ticket Reservations window from the main menu: Modules/ Tickets/ Reservations:



The Shows browser will display the current shows. The sessions for the currently selected show will display in the Sessions browser. The reservations for this session will appear the browser below.
Select the show and the session, and click the Add Reservation button to open the Edit Reservation window.
·	You can type in the customer's first and last names, their phone number and the number of seats reserved.
·	If the customer is already in your customer master, you can enter their customer key and the name and phone fields will be copied out of the customer master.
You will not be able to reserve (or sell) more seats than are available in the theatre for that show.


.topic 2060
 The marketing module is licensed functionality. Contact Barcode Solutions to purchase a license.

The marketing option allows you to:
·	record the location your customers are from (usually by postcode, state or other grouping), and
·	record your sales against your promotional materials (the 'source').
These are the standard customer breakdowns that people use but you can set any parameters you want.
At point of sale and in the order entry window, as an operator is finalising a sale, the Marketing window is displayed. Operators enter the Location or Source for the sale.
You can then print sales reports to monitor the effectiveness of your promotional campaigns and to see who you are selling to.



Setting up marketing

System options
In the Modules window of System Options you can set your marketing defaults:
You can choose to record sales by location or source or both. You can disable one or the other by using the respective Disable checkbox.
Once you have set up your locations and sources you can, you can set defaults in the Defaults combo boxes.
You can also choose where to force operators to make a selection and you can allow multiple selections per sale.

Setting up the sources
The sources are set up in the Marketing Source Codes window. This window is opened from the main menu: Tables/ Authority Tables/ Marketing/ Sources.



Add a source
·	Click on the Add button.
·	Type in the Code (maximum 6 characters)
·	Type in the Name (maximum 35 characters)
·	Click the OK button.
The source will be saved and displayed in the list.

Global change
You can absorb one source into another, deleting the original code.
·	Click on a source in the list.
·	Click the Global button.
·	Type the new source into the 'Into Source Code' field.
·	Click OK button.
·	The old source code will be deleted.

Setting up the locations
The sources are set up in the Marketing Source Codes window. This window is opened from the main menu: Tables/ Authority Tables/ Marketing/ Locations.



To set up the sources on the main menu click Tables, then move the mouse on Authority Tables, then Marketing, then click on Locations.

Add a location
·	Click on the Add button.
·	Type in the Code (maximum 6 characters)
·	Type in the Name (maximum 35 characters)
·	Click the OK button.
The location will be saved and displayed in the list.

Global change
You can absorb one location into another, deleting the original code.
·	Click on a location in the list.
·	Click the Global button.
·	Type the new location into the 'Into Llocation Code' field.
·	Click OK button.
·	The old location code will be deleted.

Using the Marketing module
Now in cash sales, laybys and invoicing, the operator will be able to select a Location and Source which will be recorded against the sale.
As the operator is finalising the sale, the Marketing pop-up window will be displayed:



What needs to be entered into the window will depend on you System Options settings. Assuming both locations and sources are enabled and there are no defaults:
·	Select a Location by either typing in some of the Code to find the one you wish and double click on it, type in the entire code.
·	Select a Source by either typing in some of the Code to find the one you wish and double click on it, type in the entire code.
·	Click Select (F12) button. The Marketing window will be closed and the sale proceed as usual.

Notes
·	If you have disabled using either Source or Location in the System options, you will not be able to enter that field in the above window.
·	If you have set a default for one or both options, you can simply click Select (F12) button to proceed. If you wish the defaults can be overtyping or overwritten by double clicking on a different option in the browser. Setting a default will mean fewer key strokes.
·	If you have set the 'multiple types per sale' flag in System Options, the marketing window will appear as each line of the sale is saved. This option is for specialized sites – it would not suit a standard retail store.

Reports
You can create reports to view performance by each of your marketing groups. Open the Marketing Report Parameters window in either Bookscan Reports or at Sales/Reports/Marketing.:



Parameters:
All items/ summary: display all sales or just total sales per marketing group
Date range: select the start and end date of your sales period.
Customer sales: setting this flag will report sales from the saleline.dbf file, otherwise the report is based on the sales history file.
Type: choose whether to report on your sales or your locations.


.topic 1740
 As Bookscan was originally written for the book and music industries, the inventory list is generally referred to as the title master. It is, however, quite adaptable for other industries.

Viewing products
Products are stored in the master table with extra publishing information stored in the publisher table. They can be viewed in the Title Master window which can be opened in many places throughout the program:
·	From the main menu: select Tables / Title Master.
·	By using the 'house' icon on the toolbar:

·	By using the F2 button in most windows in the program.



The Title Master window has 16 tabs displaying different information about your products:


Main tabs
·	Details - this tab contains most of the fields from the master file.
·	Extra fields - less important fields from the master file.
·	List - the list tab is a browser displaying all the titles in the master file.

Sales tabs
·	Specials - lists all current special orders for the selected title.
·	History - displays the monthly and weekly sales summaries.
·	All Sales - shows a detailed listing of sales of the selected title.
·	Approvals - shows all outstanding approvals for the selected title.
·	Consignment out - shows all customers to whom you have consigned the selected title.
·	Laybys - displays a list of customers who have laybys (retail configuration only).
·	Backorders - displays a list of customers who have the title on backorder (distributor/ publisher configuration only).
·	Promotions - shows a summary of all promotions that include the title.

Stock Control tabs
·	Stocks - shows a stock summary for each of your stores.
·	Movements - shows a detailed listing of the title's stock movements: goods inwards, returns to supplier etc.
·	Purchase Orders - shows a detailed listing of all purchase orders for the title.
·	Kits - displays the kits of which the title is a component. For kits themselves, it shows the kit's components.
·	Returns authorisations - displays a list of customers who have the outstanding return authorisations for the selected title (distributor/ publisher configuration only).

Specialist tabs
·	Courses - this view displays a summary of course adoptions for the title (Academic module only).

Displaying tabs
To open a tab (or close an open tab), use the right mouse to open the context menu and then select the required tab. Alternatively, use the shortcut keys listed at the bottom of the window. E.g. Alt F3 (i.e. hold the Alt button down and press the F3 function key) will open (or close) the Specials tab.



On each computer, you can save which tabs you want to open by default. With the window displaying only the tabs you want, and with the tab you want displayed first showing uppermost, use the right mouse to open the context menu and then select 'Save as default view'.

Important  note: in previous versions, to speed up the opening of the title master, users were advised to only have a few tabs open by default. This is no longer necessary. When preparing your default setting, open all tabs that you use, just de-selecting those tabs that are not relevant to you. Then save your default setting.
