	Search for Titles
	Delete a Title


Title Master Tabs/Screens:
The Title Master is split into different tabs or screens which display information about the title such as title, author and pricing information.
For more information on what are the tabs, what is contained on them and how to show different views click the link.




.topic 2700
Details tab
This tab contains most of the fields from the master file.
Buttons
·	On hand - click on this button to make a stock adjustment. A pop up window will appear where you have to type a password set in System Options, then another pop up window appears where you can make the adjustment.



·	Specials – opens and displays the Special Orders tab.
·	Laybys – opens and displays the Laybys tab.
·	Consignments out – opens and displays the Consignment out tab.
·	Approvals – opens and displays the Approvals tab.
·	Backorders – opens and displays the Backorders tab.
·	Holds – opens the Held stock pop up window.



·	Min stock – opens a pop up window where you can change the minimum stock figure. Note that the new figure defaults to zero.
·	Max stock – opens a pop up window where you can change the maximum stock figure. Note that the new figure defaults to zero.
·	Pricing matrix – opens the pricing matrix for the selected title
·	Abstract – the abstract is a comment field of any length. Click on the Abstract button to open the abstract in a larger, pop-up window either to edit it or view if there is more than will fit in the basic screen.
·	Hide/Show prices – hides/shows all but the Sell Price field. Use this feature where customers may be able to see the screen.
·	Web (F9) – click on this button to update the selected title from a either the Title Page, Nielsen Bookdata or Global BIP web sites, if you have saved the log in details in the System Options.



Category (F6) - clicking the F6 button will open a Categories pop-up window where you can add the title to or remove it from a category



BIC subject (F7) – clicking the F7 button will open a pop-up window allowing you to absorb all the titles with one BIC code into another



Flags
·	Firm Sale - check this box where the supplier will not accept returns.
·	No Discount – when this flag is set the POS and invoicing windows will not allow a discount or change of price on the title. If a header discount is placed on a sale, it will not apply to this product.
·	No Reward - stops customer rewards being given for the title.
·	No Sp Orders - prevents special orders being placed for the title.
·	Generic - the generic flag is used for products which are not stock controlled. E.g. if you are charging freight by setting up a title called "freight", you would flag it as generic. Another example would be a dump bin of an unknown number of products at various prices. In this case you would set the selling price in the master as zero and overwrite the price as you sell it through the POS.  Sets the Available quantity to 999999.
·	Tax exempt - sets the title as tax exempt.

Context menu options
·	Add to draft - this option allows you to create a draft order for the product. A pop up window will be displayed where you can enter the order quantity. Just type in this number, click the OK button and the draft order is created with the primary supplier.
·	Add to draft returns - this option allows you to create a draft return for the product.
·	Merge titles – opens a pop up window where you can merge the currently selected title with another title.
·	Marketing totals – opens a pop up window shows the sales of this product in each marketing group.

Extra fields tab
This tab contains less important fields from the master file.



The following fields may need an explanation:
Re-pricing
(Distributor/ publisher configuration only)
Where you don't know the price of a title and you wish to take backorders on it, set the Re-price flag to true. When the title is received into stock, you will set the new price. When backorders are released, the new price will be applied to the backorders (less the customer's discount).
The program will automatically remove the re-price flag during receiving unless you have also set the 'Auto lift re-pricing' flag to false. So if you are say, publishing anew title and you haven't yet set the price, tick both the 're-price' and 'auto lift re-pricing' flags to true. If you import a title and the price you set will vary with the exchange rate set the 're-price' flag to true and 'auto lift re-pricing' flags to false.

Shortage
(Distributor/ publisher configuration only)
During the picking/packing process, where insufficient stock is found to fill an order line, the program sets the product's shortage flag to true. This will cause further orders for the product to be backordered, giving you time to find the stock or reduce the on hand quantity.

Jacket path
The best way to store jackets jpg files for your titles is to name the jpg using the titles ISBN as the jpg file name e.g. 9780732271060.jpg. Store the file in a folder called 'Catalog' in your data directory e.g. C:\e-BilityDate\Catalog\9780732271060.jpg. In these instances there is no need to enter anything in the Jacket Path field and the jacket will display in this tab.
If you have stored the jacket image elsewhere or not used the ISBN as the jpg file name, you will need to enter the file path of your jacket image in the Jacket Path field.

List tab
This tab is a browser displaying all the titles in the master file.



Double click on a line in the browser to display the Details tab for that title.


.topic 1760
In all but a few instances, when you open the Title Master window, the Title Find window will open above it; so you can immediately search for the title you want. Otherwise, open the Title Find window by clicking the Find (F2) button on the Title Master window.

Select the radio button for search you wish to do.
·	Type in the search criterion in the Find field and then press the Enter key.
·	The Find window will close.
·	If only a single title matches your search, the master window will display that title.
·	For most searches, if more than one title is found, the List tab will be displayed with the products in the order of the search (e.g. after an author search, the products will be sorted by author). The selected product in the List browser will be the first product that meets your search criterion. Select the title you require either by selecting the line and pressing the Enter key or double-clicking the line with the mouse.
·	For embedded and category searches, if more than one title is found, a pop up browser window will be displayed from which you can choose the title you want.
·	For an ISBN search, if the title is not found, a pop up window will display, inviting you to search the Title Page, Nielsen BookData and Global BIP web sites to find the product.


Search methods
ISBN (F3)	
Scan or type the ISBN into the Find field. The title you require will be displayed.
If the List view is displayed, the titles will be listed in ISBN order, with the one you entered selected and centered on the screen.
If the title is not found, a pop up window will display, inviting you to search the Title Page, Nielsen BookData and Global BIP web sites to find the product.


Title (F4)
Type in the start of the title, into the Find field. For example, if you want to search for the title 'Bourne Supremacy', type 'bourne' into the Find field. The program will search for all title starting with 'bourne'.
Hints on searching by title
1.	Don't type 'a', an' or 'the' at the start of your search string.
2.	Ignore upper or lower case.
3.	Stop typing before you get to an apostrophe or other punctuation mark; it may or may not have been included during data entry. For example, if you're looking for "Russia's Youth and Its Culture", just type in 'russia' and press the Enter key.

Author (F5)	
Type the first few letters of the primary author into the Find field and press the Enter key.
If the List view is displayed, the titles will be listed in alphabetical order of author. The selected product in the List browser will be the first product that meets your search criterion.
Note that author name must be entered in the correct order: if the author appears in the title master as 'Brown, Dan' searching for 'brown' will work, but searching for 'Dan Brown' will not.

Catalogue (F6)
If you are using catalogue numbers (and this is usual for music stores), type the catalogue number of the product into the Find field. The title you require will be displayed.
If the List view is displayed, the titles will be listed in catalogue number order, with the one you entered selected and centered on the screen.

3 – 2 - 1 (F7)	
The 3 – 2 – 1 code is made up from the title field and serves as a mnemonic shortcut. The code is derived from the title: It takes the:
·	first three letters of the first word of the title,
·	first two letters of the second word and
·	first letter of the third.
So the title 'Luscher Color Test' has a 3 – 2 – 1 code of 'LUSCOT".

·	The following small words are ignored – a, an, the, of, and, to, as, on, in. For example: 'Jeeves and the Feudal Spirit' will have 3 – 2 – 1 code 'JEEFES'
·	All punctuation is ignored – only letters and numbers as used.
·	There are no spaces in the code. So 'P.G. Wodehouse: a Life' will have 3 – 2 – 1 code 'PGW'.

Type the 3 – 2 – 1 code into the Find field and press the Enter key and the requested title will be displayed. If the List view is displayed, the titles will be listed in 3 – 2 – 1 code order. The selected product in the List browser will be the first product that meets your search criterion.
Note that only part of the code need be typed; in one of the above example, searching for 'JEEF' will still find 'Jeeves and the Feudal Spirit'.

Supplier (Alt F7)	
Type the supplier code into the Find field and press the Enter key.
The titles in the List tab will be listed in of supplier code order, with the first title for the supplier selected and centered on the screen.

Imprint (F8)	
Type the imprint code into the Find field and press the Enter key.
The titles in the List tab will be listed in imprint order, with the first title with this imprint selected and centered on the screen.

Abstract (Alt F8)
In the Find field, type a search string and press the Enter key. The program will search the Abstract field for the string and will display the first title where it finds the string. Unless you can enter a search for a unique string, you may find the embedded search more useful as it will display all titles that meet the criteria

Category (F9)	
For this search the Find field is replaced by a combo box. Enter the category code in the field, or select it from the drop-down box, and press the Enter key. A pop up window will display, listing all titles in that category. Select a required title from the list.
You can also do a multi-category search. See the section below.

Web Search (F11)	
This is a powerful search enabling users to search the Title Page, Nielsen BookData and Global BIP web sites to find products. See the web search sections below.

Embedded (F2)
This is a powerful search that will look for a word or phrase in several fields of the title master. Type your search string into the Find field and press the Enter key. The program will search for any occurrence of your string in any of these fields: title, author, illustrator, abstract and comments.
If only one title meets your search criterion, that title will be displayed.
If more than one title is found, a pop up browser window will be displayed from which you can choose the title you want.

Multi-category Search (Alt + F9)
The multi-category search allows for the searching of categories using the logical operators: 'AND', 'OR' and 'AND NOT'. This allows you to narrow a search by excluding categories as well as including them.

To run the search:
·	Click the Multi Category (Alt F9) button.
·	Select the Add category drop-down field to select which category to add first category.
·	Using the Add Category drop-down list and clicking on an item, you can add it to the search which is displayed in the list box.
·	The default logical operator is 'or'; you can change it using the Logical combo box.
·	When you have entered your search, click the OK button.
·	As with the standard category search, a pop up browser window will be displayed from which you can choose the title you want.

In this example, the program will search for titles in either the Biography or Dictionaries categories that are not new releases from January.

Logical options:
·	^AND^ - 'A and B' - titles must be in both categories A and B.
·	^OR^ - 'A or B' - titles can be in either category, A or B.
·	^AND NOT^ - 'A and not B' - titles must be in category A and not in category B.

Testing the expression
If you have a complex expression, you can test that it is a valid search by clicking on Test Expression button.

Other Options
·	Delete Cat	Removes the selected category from the search.
·	Delete All	Clears all the current multi-category search criteria.
·	Sort Up	Swaps the category selected with the one above it.
·	Sort Down	Swaps the category selected with the one below it.




.topic 1750

There are several ways to load products into your title master tables:
·	Manually entering titles in the New Title window.
·	Creating second hand titles from existing titles in the Buyback window.
·	Using the Web Search feature in the Title master window to download individual titles from Title Page, or groups of titles from the Nielsen BookData and Global BIP web sites.
·	Importing titles in the Import window, from text or HTML files downloaded from the Title Page, Nielsen BookData or Global BIP web sites.
·	Importing titles, in the Import window, from Onix or Bisac files issued by publishers or distributors.

New Title window
You can open the New Title window from the Title Master window. Just click the New F11 button.


Notes on some of the fields
·	Mandatory fields are coloured red.
·	After you enter the ISBN into the New Code field, the program will check to see if the title is already in the database. If it is, it will offer to return to the Title Master window and display that title. If the title is not in the database, it will check the archive. If the title is in the archive, the program will offer to return it to the master file.
·	Clicking the Copy F3 button will allow you to copy all the details, except the ISBN, of an existing title into the New Title window's fields.
·	Clicking the Copy Last F2 button will allow you to copy all the details, except the ISBN, of the last title you used into the New Title window's fields.
·	Clicking the F6 button will open a Categories pop-up window where you can add the title to or remove it from a category. See the Details tab section above, for details.
The Next (Alt F2) button will load the next dummy product code (set on the Titles tab in System options) into the ISBN field. The program will then increment the next dummy product code.  If this code is an ISBN (i.e. 13 digits starting 978 or 979) then the last (check) digit will be calculated to make it a valid  ISBN.




.topic 2260
Deleting a title

Titles can be deleted through the Title Master individually, but with a restriction: all stock figures must be zero. This includes stock currently on hand, on order, and on consignment, minimum stock etc. (but does not include the last stocktake figure)
To delete titles:
·	Open the Title Master.
·	Search for the title you wish to delete in the search window.
·	When found, ensure the title is selected (either by having the title open in the 'Details' tab, or highlighted in the 'List' tab).
·	Click the Delete Icon (red with a white cross) on the toolbar at the top of the Bookscan window or press the F5 key.
·	You will be asked to confirm the deletion and then given an option to move the title to the archive.

Note: All quantities must be zero for the title to be deleted.









.topic 2300
Using Title Page with Bookscan
There is a service provided for Title Page subscribers that allow the use of the Title Page API, which enables a more automated way of retrieving data from their service without manually opening their website.

Title Page Limitations
There is a limited amount of information that this Title Page (API) service will search and retrieve:
·	Searching: You can only search the Title Page (API) using ISBN13. There is currently no Embedded, Title or Author type searching.
·	Data fields retrieved: Title, Author, Author Role, Supplier, Status, Price, Supplier Stock Level, Supplier Order Level, Publisher Ship Date.

Notes
·	As listed, this service does not retrieve all the data that would normally come in from an ONIX file downloaded from Title Page website. This is a limitation on Title Page's side. We are retrieving everything available.  If you wish to use Bookscan as a springboard to your website, with jacket images and the like, it would be best to use Nielsen Bookdata, ONIX files from suppliers or Title Page's website as your primary source of data, as they contains more data.
·	Title page's Supplier Order Level and Supplier Stock level fields do not get saved into Bookscan, but will be visible when using this Title Page API through Bookscan.
·	Title page's Supplier field, just as in the ONIX import window, does not automatically link with the suppliers contained in your own Supplier Master. You will need to do the linking up yourself (as will be explained below).

Setup
Before you can use the Title Page API features in Bookscan, you will first need to ensure your account with Title Page has this feature enabled. It is not enabled by default and you have to contact Title Page to enable it. We've no idea why!
Open the Titles tab of System Options and click on the Title Page Set Up button. Enter your Title Page Username and Password and save them.

Updating Existing Titles
This method of updating can be done anywhere throughout the program where you can view the Title Master. It is used to update price and availability
Open the Title Master and display the Details tab.

Click on the Web (F9) button.
The Web Search window will open.

·	Ensure that Title Page (F6) is selected and click the Login (F12) button. Bookscan will attempt to log in to the Title Page web site.
·	A normal course of it running will show these messages (in order);
§	Connecting to Title Page
§	Requesting data for: (ISBN)
§	Success!: data found for : (ISBN)
·	If you don't succeed in logging in, the window will show 'failed' and a detailed failure message will display.
·	If the log in succeeds, Bookscan will retrieve an update file for the title and the Title Page Update window will open:

·	The fields coloured blue display what is currently in your master file. The fields coloured black are from Title Page except the Set supplier field which displays your current primary supplier.
·	Clicking the Update (F12) button will cause the program to overwrite this fields that display a tick against them.
·	To stop a field from updating, click on the tick to remove it.

Title
If the checkbox beside the Title field is checked, the master file title field will be updated. Ticking the 'Upper case' checkbox will cause it to save in upper case.

Contributor:
If the checkbox beside the Author field is checked, the Author field in the Title Master will be updated. The Role field is the role the contributor plays with this product. The field next to it is the description of that role. A01 is (principal) author. If the incoming contributor role is not this, you may choose to not update the Author field.

Supplier
Title page's Supplier field can't be mapped to Bookscan's supplier master. You will need to do the linking up yourself.
The Supplier Name field shows the supplier as recorded by Title Page. The blue field below it shows the primary supplier in your master file.
The Set Supplier combo box will also default to the Bookscan supplier. It's here that you can manually set the Bookscan supplier to be the same as the Title Page supplier.

Status
The Availability field displays the ONIX status as recorded by Title Page. The blue field below it shows the status in your master file. If you have mapped your status fields to Onix fields (or if your statuses use the Onix standard), the Set Status combo box will default to the Title Page status. If you haven't done the mapping you will need to set the Status field manually.

Publisher shipping date
If the publisher/distributor is out of stock and have nominated an expected shipping date, it will be displayed in the Pub Ship Date field. This will update the corresponding field in the title master.

Pricing
The Sell Price and Retail Price fields display the Title Page (Australian) prices with the master file price in blue, beneath them. The master file prices will update when you click the Update (F12) button. If you wish, you can manually key a different Sell or Retail price in these fields, or un-tick the checkboxes to cause the prices not to update.
Note: Below the Retail field is the price type for the Title Page prices. Standardly, the prices are 'retail price including tax'. If it is something other than this, you will need to take it in consideration when updating.

Supplier Availability
This information is for information only; the data is not imported into Bookscan.
·	On hand – indicates whether the supplier has stock.
·	On order - indicates if the supplier (Yes/No) has stock on order.

Adding new titles using the Title Page API

Title Master
If an ISBN search in the Find Title window fails to find a product in the master file, the program will open the Web Search window from where you can search the TitlePage web site for the product.
·	In the Web Search ensure the Title Page radio button is selected and click the Log in (F12) button.
·	The Title Page login window will display. Bookscan will attempt to log in to the Title Page web site.
·	A normal course of it running will show these messages (in order);
§	Connecting to Title Page
§	Requesting data for: 9781841593067
§	Success!: data found for : 9781841593067
·	If you don't succeed in logging in, the window will show 'failed' and a detailed failure message will display.
·	If the log in succeeds, Bookscan will retrieve an update file for the title and the Title Page Update window will open:

Note that the window is slightly different from the update window shown earlier – the only optional import fields are the status and sell price.

Notes
·	The ISBN, Title, Author and Retail fields will always be imported to the master file.
·	Note that you can overtype these fields in the window if you wish.
·	Ticking the 'Upper case' checkbox will cause the title to save in upper case.
·	The Role field is the role the contributor plays with this product. The field next to it is the description of that role. A01, for example, is (principal) author.

Supplier
Title page's Supplier field can't be mapped to Bookscan's supplier master. You will need to do the linking up yourself. The Supplier Name field shows the supplier as recorded by Title Page. The Set Supplier combo box will be empty. It's here that you can manually set the Bookscan supplier to be the same as the Title Page supplier.

Status
·	The Availability field displays the ONIX status as recorded by Title Page.
·	If you have mapped your status fields to Onix fields (or if your statuses use the Onix standard), the Set Status combo box will default to the Title Page status.
·	If you haven't the field will be blank and you will have to set the Set Status field manually.
·	Unticking the checkbox to the right of the Set Status field, will prevent the master status from being updated.

Publisher shipping date
If the publisher/distributor is out of stock and have nominated an expected shipping date, it will be displayed in the Pub Ship Date field. This will update the corresponding field in the title master.

Pricing
The Sell Price and Retail Price fields display the Title Page (Australian) price. Unticking the checkbox to the right of the Sell Price field, will prevent the master sell price from being updated.
Note: Below the Retail field is the price type for the Title Page prices. Standardly, the prices are 'retail price including tax'. If it is something other than this, you will need to take it in consideration when updating.

Supplier Availability
This information is for information only; the data is not imported into Bookscan.
·	On hand – indicates whether the supplier has stock.
·	On order - indicates if the supplier (Yes/No) has stock on order.




.topic 2570
Adding new titles using the Nielsen BookData API

Bookscan contains a Bookfind-on-Line web search (from Nielsen BookData). This allows users to download on the fly, title details from the Bookdata's web site using a variety of search criteria: ISBN, title, author, publication dates etc.

Note: You will need a subscription to Nielsen BookData to use this service.

Setting up
System Options
To set upe-Bility to use this feature, open the Titles tab of System Options, click on the Set Up button for Nielsen Bookdata. Enter your Nielsen Bookdata login code and password.

Binding and Status mapping
Nielsen Bookdata now uses Onix codes for binding and statuses. If you haven't done so already, you will need to map your bindings and statuses to Onix. From the main, open the Bindings and Status tables: Tables/ Authority tables/ Bindings and Tables/ Authority tables/ Status.
Open the edit window on each line and select the related Onix mapping from the drop-down box. This will take about 5 minutes.

Supplier mapping
Bookdata have 6 digit supplier codes. It is an advantage to map the suppliers you use to your supplier master. This may be a large undertaking and you may want to map them as required.
The supplier mapping is done on the Supplier tab of the Supplier Master window:


Using Nielsen BookData's search engine
The search is activated:
·	Via the Title Find window for title master searches.
·	After an unsuccessful ISBN search in the Title Find window, the program will offer to do a web search to look for the title.
·	Via the Details tab of the Title master (click on the Web (F9) button).
·	Via the Receiving window – scan the barcode of a title not in the database and the program will offer to search the web site for the title.
·	Via EDI receiving – titles not in the master file display "Not in the master file" in the browser. Select one of these titles, open the context menu and select "Add ISBN" to search the web for the title.

When the search is activated, the Web Search window is displayed:

You are able to do simple searches by typing the search criterion into the relevant fields.
In the example above, the user has created a title search for 'wodehouse' and has added a further search criterion of a (local) price date range of 20.00 to 40.00. So the search will be all titles by Wodehouse priced from $20.00 and $40.00. The search has found 31 titles.

Creating your search criteria:
·	Enter the search data in the relevant fields (e.g. the title, "South Pacific").
·	Click the Search (F12) button to do your search. Bookscan will contact the BookData web site and begin loading a browser with the results of your search.
·	Title (and other text) searches are embedded. The example above will find any title with "South Pacific" anywhere in its title field.
·	Bookscan will prompt you if many titles meet your search criteria (if you get 100 hits, you may wish to backtrack and add extra criteria); otherwise it will just begin downloading. You may set your own prompt level in the window – the default is 20.

Creating complex searches:
See the section Search logic at the bottom of this page.

Search results

·	As the results are downloaded you will see them added to the results browser.
·	You can click the Cancel button, or press the Escape key, at any time to cancel the download. So you can watch for the title you require and after it appears in the browser, press the Escape key and the download will stop.
·	After downloading, if you have too many titles in the browser, you can click the Show Search (F11) button to edit the search and re-do the download.
·	In the browser, the second column, On File, shows whether you already have the title in your master file. Selecting such a title in the browser will cause the title master to display that title.
·	You can filter the results browser to just show items with local pricing by using the "Local Only (F7)" check box.
·	Set the "Keep Open (F6) checkbox to true if you want to download multiple titles, otherwise the search window will automatically close when you down load a title or group of titles.
·	Note that the search window is not modal to the Title master; so you can switch back and forth between the two windows.

Single title import
In the Results browser, highlight the title you wish to import and click the "Update Master (F12)" button. Or just double click on the title in the browser. The title will be added to the browser. You can then return to the title master window and edit the departments and other fields you want.
Alternatively you can set the program to display the Extra Fields window (see 'multiple title import' below), where you can set the Department and other fields before importing. This flag is set in the Web Search Defaults window (se below).
If the "Keep Open (F6) checkbox is ticked search window will remain open after you have added a title, allowing you add multiple titles. If it is unticked, the window will automatically close.

Multiple title import
You can import multiple titles to the master file by highlighting all the titles you wish to import and click the "Update Master (F12)" button.  The Extra Fields window will open where you can select the department, supplier and a few other details.


If you have mapped the Bookdata supplier code to your supplier, the Supplier field will display the supplier. If not you will need to select the supplier.
You can set the minimum stock add the titles to the draft..
If the Always Overwrite checkbox is ticked, then all the titles imported will have the supplier nominated in the Supplier field as their primary supplier. If it is unticked, only those import records with the same Bookdata supplier code will record this supplier; other titles will have their primary supplier left unchanged.

Setting web search defaults
The Web Search Defaults window can be opened from the Web Search window by clicking on the Set Defaults (F8) button. If you have already started your search and the search results are showing you can open the window using the context menu.

You can set the following:
·	Don't download blurbs.
Select long or short blubs.
Don't download jacket images.
·	Set the jacket size.
Set a maximum number of records to download on any search.
·	Set a default department (useful when updating by double clicking in the browser).
·	Set a MISC as the default supplier (useful when updating by double clicking in the browser).
·	Always show the fields selection ('Extra Fields') window for single title downloads.
·	If you have a TitlePage account, you can have the program go to the title page site to get the price and availability data.


SEARCH LOGIC

Wildcard Searches

Wildcard searching allows you to widen a search on a textual or name field by including a truncation symbol (* or ?) in your search term. The * symbol is used to match any number of characters and the ? symbol is used to match a single character.

Examples of truncation:

Searching for variants of words, for example BEACH* to find Beachball, Beachams, etc

Searching for words with different spellings, for example COL*R for colour and color.

Searching for titles with ISBN prefix, for example 07139*

Searching for a word where a single character may vary, for example WOM?N to find woman and women; fertili?er to find fertiliser and fertilizer.

Single Word searches

Finding single word titles can be difficult, especially if the title is also a common word.  Put the pipe symbol ( | ) round your search term and the single word will be found.

For example, |boy| will find the title "Boy" but not "101 Things for a Boy to Make".

You can also use it to find an exact match of more than one word, for example |The War of the Worlds| will return titles where the title is only The War of the Worlds and exclude titles with other words in the title.

Phrase Searches

Sometimes you'll only want results that include a phrase. In this case, simply put quotation marks around your search terms. 
For example a title search for "Nuclear fuel"  will include titles were these words adjacent to each other eg. "The International Nuclear Fuel Market" but not "Nuclear Energy and its Fuel Cycle".

To find the exact match of a phrase or word put the pipe symbol (|) around your search terms, for example
|The War of the Worlds| will return titles where the title is only The War of the Worlds and exclude titles with any other words in the title.

AND OR NOT Searches

Operators are used to link search terms or fields. Depending on the operators used and the contents of the database, the number of search hits will be broadened or narrowed.  AND OR NOT must be entered in CAPITALS.

The AND operator is used to narrow a search by including specific terms. For example, ABANDONED AND ABACUS would include all records where the search field contains both the word ABANDONED and the word ABACUS.

The OR operator is used to broaden a search by including any terms. For example, ABACUS OR ABANDONED would include all records where the search field contains either the word ABACUS or the word ABANDONED or both words.

The NOT operator is used to narrow a search by excluding specific terms. For example, ABANDONED NOT ABACUS would include all records where the search field contains the word ABANDONED but not the word ABACUS.

You cannot enter NOT as the first term in any search field.

If you enter two or more terms in a search field just separated by a space, eg FOOD BOOK, the system will treat them as though they are joined with AND operators, so will look for records which have both words in that field.  
 
Search Term Nesting

Using brackets you may nest search terms to ensure that they are evaluated in the order you require. For example the search GREEN OR RED AND BLACK will first locate the records with GREEN or RED and then select those that contain BLACK. Whereas the search GREEN OR (RED AND BLACK) locate the records that contain both RED and BLACK and also include any records containing GREEN.

You may have more than one set of brackets in a search. For example (GREEN AND BLUE) OR (RED AND BLACK) You may nest sets of brackets. For example (GREEN AND (BLUE OR RED OR BLACK)) NOT YELLOW.

Range Searches

For text strings the curly brackets are used, for example to find all Authors with names between these alphabetic ranges enter in the Author/Contributor field: {Adams TO Addler}


.topic 2640
Adding new titles using the Bowker Book in Print

Bookscan contains two Bowker Books in Print web search:
Bowker Books in print
This is the standard search engine Bowker subscribers will use. It allows users to download on the fly, title details from the Bowker's web site using a varirty of search criteria: ISBN, title, author, series and keyword with additional filters of binding, publication date, supplier, status, market and price.

Bowker web services
 his allows users to download on the fly, title details from the Bowker's web site using up to threef search criteria: ISBN, title and author.

Note: You will need a subscription to Bowker Books in Print to use these services.

Setting up
System Options
To set Bookscan up to use this feature, open the Titles tab of System Options, click on the Set Up button for Bowker BIP and enter your Bowker on line login code and password.

Binding and Status mapping
You should map your statuses and bindings to Bowker's.
From the main, open the Bindings and Status tables: Tables/ Authority tables/ Bindings and Tables/ Authority tables/ Status.
Open the edit window on each line and select the related Bowker mapping from the drop-down box. This will take about 5 minutes.

Using Bowker Books in Print
The search is activated:
·	Via the Title Find window for title master searches.
·	After an unsuccessful ISBN search in the Title Find window, the program will offer to do a web search to look for the title.
·	Via the Details tab of the Title master (click on the Web (F9) button).
·	Via the Receiving window – scan the barcode of a title not in the database and the program will offer to search the web site for the title.
·	Via EDI receiving – titles not in the master file display "Not in the master file" in the browser. Select one of these titles, open the context menu and select "Add ISBN" to search the web for the title.

When the search is activated, the Web Search window is displayed:


In the example above, the user has created a title search for 'wodehouse'. The search has found 47 titles.

Creating your search criteria:
·	Enter the search data in the relevant fields (e.g. the title, "South Pacific").
·	Click the Search (F12) button to do your search. Bookscan will contact the Bowker Books In Print web site and begin loading a browser with the results of your search.
·	Title (and other text) searches are embedded. The example above will find any title with "South Pacific" anywhere in its title field.
·	Bookscan will prompt you if many titles meet your search criteria (if you get 2000 hits, you may wish to backtrack and add extra criteria); otherwise it will just begin downloading. You may set your own prompt level in the window – the default is 20.

Search results

·	As the results are downloaded you will see them added to the results browser.
·	You can click the Cancel button, or press the Escape key, at any time to cancel the download. So you can watch for the title you require and after it appears in the browser, press the Escape key and the download will stop.
·	After downloading, if you have too many titles in the browser, you can click the Show Search (F11) button to edit the search and re-do the download.
·	In the browser, the second column, On File, shows whether you already have the title in your master file. Selecting such a title in the browser will cause the title master to display that title.
·	You can filter the results browser to just show items with local pricing by using the "Local Only (F7)" check box.
·	Set the "Keep Open (F6) checkbox to true if you want to download multiple titles, otherwise the search window will automatically close when you down load a title or group of titles.
·	Note that the search window is not modal to the Title master; so you can switch back and forth between the two windows.

Single title import
In the Results browser, highlight the title you wish to import and click the "Update Master (F12)" button. Or just double click on the title in the browser. The title will be added to the browser. You can then return to the title master window and edit the departments and other fields you want.
Alternatively you can set the program to display the Extra Fields window (see 'multiple title import' below), where you can set the Department and other fields before importing. This flag is set in the Web Search Defaults window (se below).
If the "Keep Open (F6) checkbox is ticked search window will remain open after you have added a title, allowing you add multiple titles. If it is unticked, the window will automatically close.

Multiple title import
You can import multiple titles to the master file by highlighting all the titles you wish to import and click the "Update Master (F12)" button.  The Extra Fields window will open where you can select the department, supplier and a few other details.


You can set the minimum stock add the titles to the draft..

Setting web search defaults
The Web Search Defaults window can be opened from the Web Search window by clicking on the Set Defaults (F8) button. If you have already started your search and the search results are showing you can open the window using the context menu.

You can set the following:
·	Don't download jacket images.
·	Don't download blurbs.
·	Set a default department (useful when updating by double clicking in the browser).
·	Set a MISC as the default supplier (useful when updating by double clicking in the browser).
·	Always show the fields selection ('Extra Fields') window for single title downloads.
·	If you have a TitlePage account, you can have the program go to the title page site to get the price and availability data.


.topic 1770
Customer data is stored in the customer.dbf table. Customer details can be viewed in the Customer Master window which can be opened in many places throughout the program:
·	From the main menu: select Tables/ Customer Master.
·	By using the 'person' icon on the toolbar (second from the right):



·	By using the F3 button in many windows in the program.


·	In many windows, clicking the Alt-F3 button, will load the last customer you used.

Customer Master window
The Customer Master window has 10 tabs displaying different information about your customers:



Account details tabs
·	Details - customer's key and name fields, and account flags.
·	Address – customer's addresses, phone numbers and email address.
·	Finance – for debtor customer's, the account details are displayed here.
·	Categories - shows all categories which contain this customer.
·	Abstract – a free text form where you can record your notes about the customer.
·	Contacts - shows all contacts listed for this customer.
·	List – contains a browser displaying all your customers.

Sales tabs
·	Specials - lists all current special orders for the customer.
·	Laybys - lists all current laybys for the customer.
·	Purchases - itemises all the customer's purchases.
·	Backorders - displays a list of titles the customer has on backorder (distributor/ publisher configuration only).

Displaying tabs
To open a tab (or close an open tab), use the right mouse to open the context menu and then select the required tab. Alternatively, use the shortcut keys listed at the bottom of the window. E.g. Alt F5 (i.e. hold the Alt button down and press the F5 function key) will open (or close) the Specials tab.
On each computer, you can save which tabs you want to open by default. With the window displaying only the tabs you want, and with the tab you want displayed first showing uppermost, use the right mouse to open the context menu and then select 'Save as default view'.
Note: to speed up the opening of the customer master, it is best to only have the tabs you regularly use, open by default. Note that not all these tabs will be relevant to all users.

Tabs – detailed notes

Details tab
Key
The customer key is the field used to uniquely identify a customer. Usually the key comprises the first 5 letters of the surname (or company name) and a number generated by the program e.g. KELLY006. In System Options you can vary how the key is set up.

Change key
You can change the customer key here. Just click on the Change Key button: A pop-up window will open where you can type in the new key. The program will then update the more than 40 tables contains the customer key.

Frequent shopper key
The frequent shopper key is an alternate way for the users to identify a customer. The field is 16 characters and can be used to record a barcode if you are issuing customer cards to your customers. In the Find Customer window you can select the frequent shopper search and scan the customer barcode into the search field.

Names
Individuals
With customers who are individuals as opposed to companies, you should break down the customer name into parts: surname, first name etc. The full customer name is also stored in the Name field. (The Build Name button will build customer's name from the other name fields).
While it is not necessary to break down the name in to parts, it is very advantageous to do so. It allows for searches by surname and provides greater flexibility for customer merge and export files.
If you have imported a customer table from elsewhere without this name break down, there is a grooming tool in Bookscan to help you: see 'Customer Grooming'  below.

Companies
For companies, you should enter the company name in the Surname field and the Name field. (The Build Name button will copy the surname into the Name field). Other name fields should be left blank.

Contacts
For companies, you can record your various contacts in the contacts table. The primary contact is displayed next to the Contacts F7 button. To view, add or edit contacts, click on the button. The will open the Contacts window:



Notes
·	Use the arrow buttons on the left to navigate through the various contacts.
·	Use the arrow buttons on the right to navigate through the various addresses for this customer.
·	The main contact is set using the Main Contact (F8) check box.
·	To edit a contact, just start typing. The Add button caption will change to 'Save (F3)' and the Exit button to 'Cancel (Esc)'. Use the cancel button to undo any changes.
·	To add a contact, click the Add (F3) button. The fields will clear and you can type in the contact details.

Holds
You can place a product on hold for a customer. To do this, click on the Holds (F7) button. This will open the Holds pop-up window which will display all products on hold for this customer.



Holding a product for the customer:
·	If you want to print a Holds docket, ensure the Print checkbox is ticked.
·	Set a 'hold till' date.
·	Click on the Add Hold Item button.
·	In the pop-up window that opens, scan the barcode and press the Enter key.
·	In the next pop-up window that opens, enter the quantity and press the Enter key.

Releasing a held product:
·	Select the item in the browser.
·	Click on the Release Held Item button.

Comments
You can enter a short comment about the customer that will display below the customer name in the various sales windows.
For very important comments, tick the 'Pop up comments' check box. In the POs and the Order Entry windows, a pop-up window will display, showing the comments, when the customer is selected. This will force the operator's attention.

Flags
·	Debtor – set the customer as debtor customer. After setting this flag you should enter the account details on the Finance tab.
·	Student – this is for the pre-pack schools module only.
·	Mailorder – allows the customer to be invoiced through the Order Entry window but unlike a debtor, they will need to make a payment with the order. Use this flag to set up your web customers.
·	No statement – for debtors set them to not receive a statement.
·	Special order letters – set the program to print a letter when their special order comes into stock.
·	No backorders – in the order entry window, backorders will not be created where there is insufficient stock to fill the order (distributor/ publisher configuration only).
·	On shipment labels – a special setting for Coles Myer.
·	No account export – prevents the customer, and their transactions, from exporting to MYOB.

Optional groupings
·	Area – used to group debtor customers in the "all transactions" report.
·	Customer type – when using the pricing matrix, the settings allocated the customer to a discount group for each product. So product 9785464644644 may have a 47.5% discount for customer of type 'B' (distributor/ publisher configuration only).
·	Sales reps – the sales rep 2 field is used if you have a mixed book and music company where you have separate sales reps for books and music.
·	Commission rate – you can set your commissions for your sales reps by customer. Enter the percentage amount in the Commission Rate field.

Address tab
The customer master allows for a single postal address and multiple delivery addresses. To add extra delivery addresses click on the Alternate Addresses (F7) button.



You can print a customer label by clicking one on the Print Label buttons.

Finance tab
The Finance tab displays account details for customer who are debtors:



·	Bill to – if the customer is a branch or division of a company, you can have their invoices billed to a central customer key. Enter that key in the Bill to field.
·	Invoice sort order – some customers want to specify the sort order of their invoices. The choices are ISBN, title, author and natural (or 'as entered').
·	EDI - if the customer is using Pacstream, you can send electronic invoices to them. Tick the EDI checkbox and enter their Pacstream number in the text field.
·	Supplier number – if the customer has allocated you a supplier number you can enter it here. It will be quoted on their invoices.
·	Terms – set your terms of trade for this customer here.
·	Credit limit – for retail users the program will warn operators when the credit limit is being exceeded. In the distributor/ publisher configuration, customer over the credit limit will have their orders backordered.
·	Stop – setting a customer on stop will prevent their orders being processed in the Order Entry window.
·	No reward – customer is not eligible for any rewards schemes.
·	No auto allocation of credits – when crediting customers by appending either accredit note or a returns authorisation in to the Order Entry window, the credit will automatically allocate against the original invoice. To turn off this feature, tick the No Allocation checkbox.
·	Open item – when this flagged is ticked, the customer is set as an open-item debtor. When unticked, they are 'balance brought forward' debtors. The open-item setting is recommended. Here payments and credits are allocated are invoices making it easier to track accounts and settle account disputes.
·	Standard discount – the two Standard Discount fields are default settings for the pricing matrix.

Categories tab
Customers can be allocated to categories for variety of purposes. If, in System Options, you set the program to track categories of purchases, then as customer purchase products they are allocated to the categories that product is in. For example, if the title 'Julius Caesar' is in categories 'biography' and 'history', a customer buying that title will also be allocated to those two categories. You can then use these customer category lists to create accurately targeted marketing campaigns. To continue the example, if you had an electronic brochure on biographies, you could load all customers from the biography category into the Mail Out window and have the program email the brochure to them.
You could also create a mailing list manually. You could create a category called say, Newsletter and add you newsletter customers to the category. Each month you could use the Mail Out to create mailing labels for these customers.
You can manually add or remove customers from a category in the Categories tab.


Associated pages:
	Add a customer
	Search for customers
	Customer grooming

 To add a customer to the customer master, click on the New (F11) button on the Customer Master window. The New Customer window will open:



The fields are similar to the fields in the Customer master window. Some fields that may need more information:
·	Customer key field - enter the first five letters of the surname or company name. (Your Systems Option setting may allow more). The program will allocate a number to make the key unique.
·	Full name field - as enter the first name and surname, the name field will be built. For companies, enter the company name in the Surname field and leave the other name fields blank.

.topic 1800

In many instances, when you open the Customer Master window, the Find Customer window will open above it; so you can immediately search for the customer you want. Otherwise, open the Find Customer window by clicking the Find (F2) button on the Customer Master window.



The search allows for five types of searches:
·	Surname - search by the customer surname.	
·	Full name – search by the customer name. This is useful for searching for company names.
If more than one customer fits the surname or name search criterion, the List view of customers will open in alphabetical order from the name you requested. Scroll down the list and click on the one you require. Then click the Detail tab or just double click the customer.
If the customer has been is identified uniquely, then the Detail view for the customer will open.
·	Customer Code - type in all or part of the customer key.
·	Frequent Shopper key	- scan in the barcode on the customer card.
·	Embedded - This is a powerful search that will look for a word or phrase in several fields of the customer master. Type your search string into the Find field and press the Enter key. The program will search for any occurrence of your string in any of these fields: name, invoice/postal address, main delivery address, short comments, customer abstract (long comments), Pacstream number, email address, phone number, mobile phone number.
If only one customer meets your search criterion, that customer will be displayed.
If more than one customer is found, a pop up browser window will be displayed from which you can choose the customer you want.
So an easy way to find a customer is to type their mobile phone number into the Find Customer window.

Return to:
	Customer Summary
	

.topic 197
  Customer masters created in Bookscan or other software may need to be groomed to allow you to use all of Bookscan's features.
Bookscan provides a window to speed up grooming your customer master. It has three separate functions:
·	Extract the surname from the (full) Name field. This allows you to search and index on the surname field.
·	Extract the post code from the second and third address lines.
·	Extract the state from the second and third address lines or calculate it from the post code.
Using the surname field allows you to do surname searches to find a customer.
Correct state and post code fields are necessary to make your mailing files comply with Australia Post's electronic sorting requirements.

Operating Instructions
The grooming window is opened main menu: select Utilities/Database Utilities/Groom Customer Master.

Click on the Load Database button.
This copies the customer master into a temporary file for you to work on. Any changes you make here will not be saved to the master file until you click on the Update Customer Master button.  While working in this window, you and other users should refrain from editing the customer postal addresses in the customer master window. Any edits will be lost when you save your temporary file back to your customer master file. You may still add new customers and their addresses.

Surname extraction
If the surname option is not already selected, click on the Surname radio button to select it.
The browser will display only those customer records with blank Surname fields.
Surname extraction consists of three steps, each step initiated by pressing in turn one of the three Go buttons. The order in which you do them depends on the format of your data:
If the majority of your customer records have the surname placed last in the Name field (e.g. 'John Smith' as opposed to 'Smith, John'), press the top Go button.
If the majority of the records are in the format 'Smith, John', you would run the middle option first.

Step 1
·	Click on the top Go button.
The system will copy the last word of the Customer field to the Surname field and check the Use column.
·	Scroll through the browser and uncheck (by double clicking on the checkbox) those records you do not want to update (e.g. company names). Unticking the Use field will clear the Surname field. If you make a mistake you can reverse it my double clicking the checkbox again; the Surname field will be re-populated.
·	You can use the right mouse menu to check all or uncheck all records and also to uncheck all above (or below) the currently selected record. This is useful if you only want to work on a portion of your database at a time.
·	You can now also manually edit the Surname field by double clicking on the entry you want to change and overtyping it.
·	Once you are ready click on the Update Customer Master button. This will save your changes to the customer master. Those records that have been updated will disappear from the browser, leaving only those records still to be done.

Step 2
If the some of the records are in the format Smith, John, initiate the second step by pressing the middle Go button. It none of your records are in this format you can skip the second step.
·	Click on the middle Go button to copy over first word of the Customer field to the Surname field.
·	Proceed as in step 1.

Step 3
The last step is used for company names. It copies the first 25 characters from the Customer field to the Surname field.
·	If the majority of your customers are companies you may wish to run this option first.
·	Click on the bottom Go Button.
·	Proceed as in step 1.
·	Any records not updated will need to be changed manually in the customer master.

Post code extraction
This operation extracts the first four digit number from first, the third address (shown in the Suburb/Town column) and then the second address field. You may wish to do the steps in the reverse order if most post codes are recorded on the second field. The system will only extract numbers whose preceding and trailing characters are a space, a comma or a full stop.
When the changes are saved, the customer master's post code field is updated and the post code (along with preceding and trailing spaces, commas or full stops) is removed from the address field.
Click on the Post code radio button to select the post code option.
The browser will display only those customer records with blank Post Code fields.
The Use column will move to be beside the Post Code column.
Post code extraction consists of two steps, each step initiated by pressing in turn one of the top two Go buttons.

Step 1
·	Click on the top Go button.
The system will extract the last possible post code of the third Suburb/Town field to the Post Cod field and tick the Use column.
·	Scroll through the browser and untick (by double clicking on the checkbox) those records you do not want to update (e.g. where phone numbers have been stored in this field). Unticking the Use field will clear the Post Code field. If you make a mistake you can reverse it by double clicking the checkbox again; the Post Code field will be re-populated.
·	You can use the right mouse menu to tick all or untick all records and also to untick all above (or below) the currently selected record. This is useful if you only want to work on a portion of your database at a time.
·	Once you are ready click on the Update Customer Master button. This will save your changes to the customer master. Those records that have been updated will disappear from the browser, leaving only those records still to be done.

Step 2
This step is the same as the step 1 except that the post code is extracted from the second address field.
·	Click on the middle Go button to extract the post code from the Address2 field.
·	Proceed as in step 1.

State extraction
The first two steps extract the state (e.g. NSW) from first, the third address (shown in the Suburb/Town column) and then the second address field. You may wish to do the steps in the reverse order if most states are recorded on the second field.
The third step uses the post code field to calculate the state. As state fields are usually optional you may not wish to use this option at all.  Also remember that this option will not extract the state from the address fields, as the first two steps do.
In the first two steps, the system checks for the characters listed in the 'Search For' box at the top of the window. This defaults to the standard abbreviations, Vic, NSW etc. You can add to this list in the 'Add State and Search Criterion' box. In the state field type the state's abbreviation e.g. TAS (use upper case) and in the Search For field type the search string e.g. Tasmania (case unimportant). Press the Enter key to add it to the list.
The system will only extract characters whose preceding and trailing characters are a space, a comma or a full stop.
When the changes are saved, the customer master's state field is updated and the search string code (along with preceding and trailing spaces, commas or full stops) is removed from the address field.

Step by step guide
·	Click on the State radio button to select the state option.
·	The browser will display only those customer records with blank State fields.
·	The Use column will move to be beside the State column.
State extraction consists of three steps, each step initiated by pressing in turn one of the Go buttons.

Step 1
·	Click on the top Go button.
The system will extract the last possible search string of the third Suburb/Town field to the State field and check the Use column.
·	Scroll through the browser and untick (by double clicking on the checkbox) those records you do not want to update. Unticking the Use field will clear the State field. If you make a mistake you can reverse it by double clicking the checkbox again; the State field will be re-populated.
·	You can use the right mouse menu to check all or uncheck all records and also to uncheck all above (or below) the currently selected record. This is useful if you only want to work on a portion of your database at a time.
·	Once you are ready click on the Update Customer Master button. This will save your changes to the customer master. Those records that have been updated will disappear from the browser, leaving only those records still to be done.

Step 2
This step is the same as the step 1 except that the state is extracted from the second address field.
·	Click on the middle Go button to extract the state the Address2 field.
·	Proceed as in step 1.

Step 3
The last step calculates the state from the post code field.
·	Click on the bottom Go Button.
·	Proceed as in step 1.
·	Any records not updated will need to be changed manually in the customer master.


Return to:
	Customer Summary



.topic 1820

Archiving titles removes them from the title master and stores them in the archive file. Earlier versions of Bookscan only saved selected fields from the title master to the archive. In the present version of the program all fields are retained.


All sales history is retained.

Why archive
By keeping the size of the title master down, you speed up searches. Archived title can be easily restored to the title master.  In the Title Master, select New (F11) once you enter the ISBN in the Code field and tab or enter, Bookscan will scan the archive to see if there is an archived record and ask if you want to recall it, if one is found.



Archiving
First re-index your master file and do your daily backup.
Open the Archiving window from the main menu: Utilities\Archives.
Select the date the titles were last sold.
Select the date the titles were last purchased
Select the date the titles were created.
Only titles with no stock, no special orders and none on order will be archived.






.topic 2770

Quantity Discounts is where you can set up prices for bulk purchase of a product. If say, you are selling pens at $1.50 each,
but also sell them in packs of 10 for $12.00, you can set up the pack price here.
Note that discounted price is only applied for full packs, not for quantities over the pack price. In our example above, buying 15 pens would see 10 charged at $1.20 each and five at $1.50.

Opening the Quantity Discount window
In the main menu, click on  Tables/ Pricing/ Quantity Discounts.

The Quantity Discount Offers window will open.

Adding a quantity discount
To add a quantity discount to a product, click the Add button and then enter the following details:

ISBN	Scan the barcode or type in the ISBN.
Quantity	Enter the pack size or discount quantity.
Sell price	Enter the sell price of the pack. Note - not the 	
	price per item.
Offer starts	If this is a limited offer, insert the start and end
/ends	dates. If no time limit, leave blank.

Click OK when done.

Other options

Edit	To edit a line, highlight it and click this button.
	You can change the quantity, price and offer
	dates.

Delete	Highlight the line you wish to delete and click this
	button

Hide/Show	Hides/displays offers past their end date.
Expired

Close	Closes the window .

.topic 1830

You can use the Promotions module to change temporarily both the selling price and the cost price of promotions stock.

When selling promotion stock  within the specified  dates, the selling price in the Cash Sales, Layby and Invoice screens will default to the promotion price.

Similarly, if the supplier is offering a special price, enter the price and the promotion dates in the Promotions screen.
Then, when receiving stock within those dates, the cost price in the Receiving screen will default to the special price.

Opening the Promotions window
In the main menu, click on Table, then point to Pricing and then click on Promotions.



The Maintain Promotions window will open.
This comprises two browsers - the upper one lists all the current  promotions and the lower one lists the items on the promotion highlighted in the upper browser.
To show expired promotions click on the Show Expired Promotions button.

Add a  new promotion
· Click on the Add Promo (F12) button  and fill in the promotion details:

Code	Unique six digit key for the promotion
	(e.g. XMAS04).

Name 	Full name of the promotion.

Start	Start date for the promotion.

End	Last day of the promotion.

Orders start	Start date for supplier's special prices.

Order End	End date for supplier's special prices.	

· When finished click the Save Promo (F12) button.

Edit a promotion
·	Double click on the promotion in the upper browser.
·	The various promotion fields will filled.
·	Edit these as required.
·	Click the Save Promo (F12) button to save your changes or  Clear Promo (Esc) button to discard them.

Delete a  new promotion
·	Right click on the promotion in the upper browser  to open the context menu.
·	Select Delete promotion.

Adding titles to the promotion
·	Highlight the required promotion in the upper browser.
·	Enter the product code or scan the barcode in the ISBN field.
·	Enter the Promotion Sell Price.
·	Enter the Promotion Cost Price.
·	Click Save Line (F12) when finished.

Edit a promotion line
·	Double click on the line in the lower browser.
·	The various promotion lines fields will filled.
·	Edit these as required.
·	Click the Save Line (F12) button to save your changes or  Clear Line (Esc) button to discard them.

Delete a  new promotion
·	Right click on the promotion line in the lower browser  to open the context menu.
·	Select Delete line from promotion.

If you have special prices for customers for certain titles, you can enter the details here. When entering invoices,laybys and cash
sales for this customer, the system will automatically select the special price.

Opening the Promotions window

The Special Customer Prices window will open.
