.topic 1840

Using the imprint matrix will allow you to set percentage discounts against imprints for groups of customers.
To use the imprint matrix, set the flag  on the Titles tab in the System Options window.
The discount and customer type are entered in the Imprint Maintenance window.
The customer type is set on the details tab of the customer master.
Once the matrix is set the system will automatically select this price for these customers in cash sales, laybys and invoicing unless there is a lower price, say from a promotion; the system will always default to the lowest available price.

Note that using imprint matrix pricing precludes you from using the standard matrix.

Setting up the matrix
Open the Imprint Maintenance window (from the main menu, select Tables, then Imprint.).
If you have set the imprint matrix flag in system options, a browser will be displayed, showing two columns: Customer type and Discount.
The browser will show the discounts against each customer type for the imprint highlighted in the browser on the left.
To set discounts against an imprint:
·	Select the imprint by clicking on it. in the browser.
·	Double click on the required customer type in the right hand browser. This will open a dialogue window.
·	Enter the discount in the New Discount field and then click on the OK button.


.topic 1850

Supplier data is stored in the supplier.dbf table. Supplier details can be viewed in the Supplier Master window which can be opened in many places throughout the program:
·	From the main menu: select Tables/ Supplier Master.
·	By using the 'three person' icon on the toolbar (on the end at the right):

·	By using the F3 button in many windows in the program.

·	In many windows, clicking the Alt F3 button, will load the last supplier you used.

Supplier Master window
The Supplier Master window has seven tabs displaying different information about your suppliers:

Supplier tabs
·	Supplier – the supplier code fields, name and phone fields etc.
·	Address – customer's addresses, email address, Pacstream number, SAN and web site.
·	Detail – supplier's currency and receiving and accounts export settings.
·	List – contains a browser displaying all your suppliers.
·	Movements – a summary by month of the last year's sales, receipts, returns etc for the supplier.
·	Abstract – a free text form where you can record your notes about the supplier.
·	Contacts - shows all contacts listed for this supplier.

Displaying tabs
To open a tab (or close an open tab), use the right mouse to open the context menu and then select the required tab. Alternatively, use the shortcut keys listed at the bottom of the window. E.g. Alt F5 (i.e. hold the Alt button down and press the F5 function key) will open (or close) the Movements tab.
On each computer, you can save which tabs you want to open by default. With the window displaying only the tabs you want, and with the tab you want displayed first showing uppermost, use the right mouse to open the context menu and then select 'Save as default view'.
Note: to speed up the opening of the supplier master, you may wish to have only the tabs you regularly use open by default.



Tabs – detailed notes
Most of the fields in the supplier master are self- explanatory. Below are the fields which may need explaining.
Supplier tab
Code
A code of four characters is used to uniquely identify a supplier.
Changing a  code
You can change the supplier code here. Click on the right mouse button to open the context menu and select the Global Change option. A pop-up window will open where you can choose to type in the new code for your selected supplier or where you merge the current supplier into another supplier.

Contacts
You can record your various contacts in the contacts table. The primary contact is displayed next to the Contacts button. To view, add or edit contacts, click on the button. The will open the Contacts window. You can also view contacts on the Contacts tab



Notes
·	Use the arrow buttons on the left to navigate through the various contacts.
·	Use the arrow buttons on the right to navigate through the various addresses for this customer.
·	The main contact is set using the Main Contact (F8) check box.
·	To edit a contact, just start typing. The Add button caption will change to 'Save (F3)' and the Exit button to 'Cancel (Esc)'. Use the cancel button to undo any changes.
·	To add a contact, click the Add (F3) button. The fields will clear and you can type in the contact details.

Bill to key
Bill to key: if the supplier is a branch or division of a supplier, you can have their invoices billed to a head office. Enter that head office supplier code in the Bill to field.

Tax number
In Australia this is the ABN. In New Zealand it is the GST number.

Alternate supplier codes
Bowker GBIP and Nielsen Bookdata both have their own codes for suppliers. You can map your suppliers to theirs' by entering their supplier codes here.

Address tab
·	The supplier master allows for a postal address and an address for returns.
·	Clicking one of the Print Label buttons to print an address label.
·	For suppliers who have a Pacstream account, set the EDI combo box to 'Pacstream' and enter their SAN number in the SAN field.
·	For suppliers to whom you wish to email purchase orders set the EDI combo box to 'email' and ensure an email address is entered in the Email field.

Details tab
On the details tab you can set up information on how the supplier invoices you. Suppliers may invoice you using different pricing methods. They may give you a set discount off the recommended retail price or just charge you a set price. Foreign currency suppliers will charge you an amount in their currency; your cost price will vary with changes in the exchange rate. Most suppliers will include GST on prices on their invoices; some will only show it as a total. When setting selling prices, you may want to work on a standard margin or markup. To assist you when you are receiving products into stock, you can set up suppliers to reflect how they invoice you.

Currency field
Set this field to the currency the supplier uses on their invoices. If this currency is your local currency, the supplier will be set as 'local supplier'; otherwise the setting will be "foreign currency". By setting the supplier as foreign currency, the money amounts displayed in the Creditors windows and on Credit Claims will be the supplier's currency.
Note: you will need regularly to set the exchange rates in the Currency window as the Receiving window will use these to calculate your cost and sell prices. The Currency window is opened from the main menu: Tables/ Authority Tables/ Currency.

Standard discount
If the supplier gives you a standard discount, you can record it here.

GST inclusive
If the supplier's invoices show you cost price as inclusive of GST, tick this checkbox. The pricing fields in the receiving window will display prices with or without GST depending on this setting:



It you have this setting right, then in the Receiving window you can just copy in the price of each line from the invoice. This is much simpler than having to calculate the GST on each line.

In the above example:
·	The GST inclusive flag is set to true.
·	The pricing method is 'discount off retail' (see below)
·	Your standard discount is 45%.
·	
In the Receiving window:
·	The prices display 'Inc' to show they are GST inclusive.
·	The operator would type '29.95' into the Retail field.
·	The program would calculate the cost price of 16.47 which is inclusive of tax.
·	When the receipt is posted into stock, the cost price to be stored in the master will be 16.47 less the tax.

GST exempt
If the supplier's invoices do not have GST charges, tick this checkbox.

Pricing methods
See the Goods Inwards chapter for a full explanation of the effect of the pricing methods.

No Auto Calculations
With the checkbox 'No Auto Calculations' ticked, the edit line of the Receiving window will display according to the pricing method set, but it will not automatically calculate the Cost or Sell fields; you will need to overtype them when required.

Not Set
This option is the default option and will suit most suppliers.
In the Receiving window, you will need to manually key in the Cost, Sell and Retail Price where necessary. The 'No Auto Calculations' setting has not effect in this option.

Discount off retail
The Receiving window will display the Retail field as the first of the price fields. With 'auto calculations' set, the cost price will be calculated at retail less the supplier's standard discount.

Discount off sell
Note that this setting is very seldom used.
The Receiving window will display the Sell field as the first of the price fields. With 'auto calculations' set, the cost price will be calculated at selling price less the supplier's standard discount.

Markup on Cost
The Receiving window will display the price fields in the order: Cost, Markup, Sell, Retail. With 'auto calculations' set, the selling price will be calculated by applying the markup percentage to the cost price. The default markup used is the setting in the 'Discount' field.

Margin
The Receiving window will display the price fields in the order: Cost, Margin, Sell, Retail. With 'auto calculations' set, the selling price will be calculated by applying a set margin to the cost price. The default margin used is the setting in the 'Discount' field.

Foreign currency
The available pricing calculators for a foreign currency supplier are:
·	not set,
·	markup on cost,
·	margin

When a foreign currency supplier is selected in the Receiving window the display will change as illustrated below:



The exchange rate from the currency file is used to calculate the (local currency) cost price from foreign cost price. Using the margin or markup calculator is the same as for local currencies.

Other settings
·	Market country code – this setting is only used in creating Onix export files, so as to comply with the Onix standard.

·	Minimum order value – if the supplier has a minimum order value, you can enter it here. It will display in the Draft Orders window.

·	Lead time – the usual time in weeks that a supplier takes to deliver. It will display in the Draft Orders window.

·	Allow returns – if the supplier does not allow returns, tick this checkbox. By default, the supplier will be excluded when you regenerate a returns list.

·	Prompt for specials – if this flag is set, the program will display a pop up warning window when you attempt to create a special order with this company. If the supplier has a minimum order value or charges a small order surcharge, you should set this flag.

·	Exclude from accounts exports – exclude the supplier, and transaction for this supplier, exporting to MYOB.

·	Exclude from Onix exports – excludes products from this supplier from exporting to Onix files.


.topic 1860

Adding a new supplier
To add a supplier to the supplier master, click on the New (F11) button on the Supplier Master window. The pop-up window will open where you can enter the new supplier code. The program will check that the code is unique. Then type in the rest of the supplier's details directly into the tabs of the Supplier Master window. As each tab is completed and click the Save button.


Return To: Supplier Master Summary


.topic 1890

In many instances, when you open the Supplier Master window, the Find Supplier window will open above it; so you can immediately search for the supplier you want. Otherwise, open the Find Supplier window by clicking the Find (F2) button on the Customer Supplier window.



The search allows for five types of searches:
·	Name – search by the supplier name.
If more than one supplier fits the name search criterion, the List view of suppliers will open in alphabetical order from the name you requested. Scroll down the list and click on the one you require. Then click the Supplier tab or just double click the supplier.
If the supplier has been is identified uniquely, then the Supplier tab for the selected supplier will open.
·	Supplier Code - type in the supplier code.
·	Embedded - This is a powerful search that will look for a word or phrase in several fields of the supplier master. Type your search string into the Find field and press the Enter key. The program will search for any occurrence of your string in any of these fields: name, postal address, returns address, short comments, supplier abstract (long comments), email address, phone numbers and main contact.
If only one supplier meets your search criterion, that supplier will be displayed.
If more than one supplier is found, a pop up browser window will be displayed from which you can choose the supplier you want.


Return To:
	Supplier Master Summary

.topic 1910

Categories allow you to group both titles and customers and can be a very useful marketing tool.
A customer's categories are updated with each sale while a title's categories are manually added, usually at title set up.

Categories and Titles
· Categories can have a variety of uses. They can be used to search for titles, to select titles for say, inclusion in booklists or  for use on a web page.

· Titles are manually added to a category either during title set up or in title or category maintenance.

· A title can be in as many categories as you wish.
   For example, Kurma's Indian Cooking could be included in the Cooking, Indian Cooking and Vegetarian Cooking categories. It could also be included in the category Christmas 2002 Catalogue.

Categories and Customers
· The categories used for titles can also put against customers to allow you to track their interests. For example Needlework may be used for both titles and customers.

· You can also create categories for use just with customers for say mailouts.
	For example, you might have a category called Teachers and another Librarians. You could also have one called Invite which you could use to produce invitations lists.

· When you enter the Customer key during a cash sale or when creating laybys, invoices and special orders,  the system checks the categories of the items purchased, and if they are not already attached to the customer records, adds them.
   This feature can be turned on or off in System Options
.

· You can also manually add  customers to a category  in Category Maintenance.

· A customer can be in as many categories as you wish.

Using Categories
For assistance on using categories, click on the relevant link below:

Managing Categories	Adding, deleting and changing categories.
	Globally manipulating categories.
	Adding and removing titles from categories.
	Adding and removing customers from categories.
	
Reports by category	Sales - Best sellers
	Sales - Single category
	Sales - Multiple categories
	Titles by category.
	List all categories		

.topic 1920

Using the Category Menu
From the main menu click on Tables, then click on Categories.

This opens the Maintain Categories window, displaying a list of all the categories on the system.

The window is split into 3 browsers: Categories, Master Links and Customers Links.



Categories
This is located on the left side of the window and has displayed the listing of all Categories within the system in alphabetical order. The categories Code and name are displayed in the browser. To the left side of this are the buttons to manipulate the categories when clicked, these are:


Report
Print a list of all categories in alphabetical order.

Find	
Search for a category either by category code (fast search) or by name.
Then click OK.

Add	
To add a category link. When prompted enter:
Name:	Category name
New Code:	Category Code (max. six digits).
Then click OK.

Edit	
Globally manipulating categories.
Double clicking on the category's line in the browser will also allow editing.

Delete		
To delete a category that is selected (one at a time).


Master Links
This section of the window displays in its browser the titles linked to the selected category on the left side of the window.
In the browser only the item ISBN and Title are displayed.
The buttons to manipulate title links are:

Master Detail	
To display the Title Master detail of the selected title
Double clicking on a Title in the browser will also display its details.

Add Link
To add a link to a title either scan its barcode in the ISBN field when the Add Master Link window displays or click to search for the title (Add link for searching using the button it shows)

Delete Link
This will delete a link to any of the title links displayed
Highlight the title you wish to remove.
You can select more than one customer by holding down the Control (Ctrl) button and clicking on titles.
You can select a range of titles by clicking on the first title you require. Then holding down the Shift button, click the last title in your range.
Click on the Delete Link button
Click OK.

Customer Links
This section of the window located at the lower right side displays in its browser customers linked to the selected category on the left side of the window. The Customer browser only displays the Customer Key and customers full name ( Customer ).
The buttons to manipulate Customer links are:

Customer Detail	
To display the customers details from the Customer Master
Double clicking on the line will also do this.

Add Link	
To link a customer to the category click the Add Link buttin and select a customer from the pop up window that opens.

Delete Link
To remove a customer from a category.
Select the customer you wish to remove.
You can select more than one customer by holding down the Control (Ctrl) button and clicking on the customers you require.
You can select a range of customers by clicking on the  first customer you require.
Then, holding down the Shift button, click the last customer in your range.
Click on the Delete Link button
Click OK.


Exit	
To exit the categories master


Note: Titles can also be assigned to or removed from categories in the Title Master's Detail tab.

	     Return to Using Categories

.topic 1930

Clicking the Edit button in the Managing Categories menu opens the Edit Category Detail window.

Here you can globally:
	· Rename a category.
	· Change a category code.
	· Moving one category  for another.
	· Cloning a category from another.
		· Replacing a category with a new category.



Renaming a category
· In the Name box, overtype the category name and click OK.

Change the category code
· In the New Code box enter the new code (max. six digits).
· Click the Replace button
· Click OK.

Moving one category to another
· In the Category list select the category you are copying from.
· Click on the Edit button.
· In the Edit Category Detail window, overtype the category code to which you are copying in the New Code box.
· Click on the Move button.
· Click the appropriate radio button to update: 	
	· Titles only
	· Customers Only
	· Both titles and customers
· Click OK.
· The titles and/or customers will be appended to the second category and deleted from the first.

Cloning a category
· In the Category list select the category you are copying from.
· Click on the Edit button.
· In the Edit Category Detail window, overtype the new category code in the New Code box.
· In the Name box, overtype the new category's name.
· Click on the Clone button.
· Click the appropriate radio button to update: 	
	· Titles only
	· Customers Only
	· Both titles and customers
· Click OK.
· The new category will be created with the titles and/or customers appended. The original category will be unchanged.

Replacing a category with a new one
· In the Category list select the category you are copying from.
· Click on the Edit button.
· In the Edit Category Detail window, overtype the new category code in the New Code box.
· Click on the Clone button.
· Click the appropriate radio button to update: 	
	· Titles only
	· Customers Only
	· Both titles and customers
· Click OK.
· The new category will be created with the titles and/or customers appended. The original category will be destroyed.
· The new category will take its name from the original one.

	    Return to Using Categories

Type in your changes.
Note: the Rep Code cannot be changed.
Click the Save button. This will save the changes.
Otherwise click Undo button, which will leave the sales rep unchanged.

.topic 1990

The bindings section of Bookscan allows for the storing and maintaining of the bindings you wish to contain within your database. These bindings can be included in the details of a title. To maintain the bindings click on Tables,then point to Authority Tables and then click on Bindings



To be ONIX compliant:
To load and use the ONIX compliant bindings, press the F12 key on your keyboard, then click Yes. This will load all the ONIX bindings automatically. Using the correct ONIX bindings will enable this to be correctly applied to any titles imported/updated from Title Page.
If you do not have the ONIX bindings, when you import titles from Title Page, it will leave the field blank for these titles.  Alternatively, you can use your existing bindings and map them to ONIX codes by double clicking on the binding or selecting one & clicking on the Edit (F4) button as per the Edit entry below.

To manage the bindings: (custom bindings/become ONIX compliant if currently not or to not be onix compliant. Use the following)

Add
To add a new binding click on the Add button.
Next fill in:
Code (2 characters)
Name
Group classification (Select from drop down) optional
ONIX code (select from drop down) optional

Click the OK button.

Edit
To make changes to an existing binding, click on the binding and click the Edit button. You can make changes to:
Name
Group classification (Select from drop down) optional
ONIX code (select from drop down) optional

Click the OK button.

Global
To change all items marked as one type to another. E.g. Code AA to Code BB.
Note: Must be existing codes.

Click on the binding you wish to change from.
Click the Global button.
The from Code will be displayed in the Absorb Binding Code field.
Type in the code you wish to change it into in the Into Binding Code field.
Click the OK button.

Delete
To remove an existing binding click on the record.
Then click the Delete button.
Then click Yes.

Close
To close the bindings section click on the Close button.

.topic 2560
Browser Fields
Pos
Position, from the left, in the tendering window where the transaction type button will show.
You can change the position of a transaction by doulble clicking on it in this column.  Moving up or down is determined by the setting of the radio buttons at the bottom right of the window.

Code	
Abbreviation for the transaction used by the system. E.g. CHQ for cheque.

Description	
Full Name of the transaction. E.g. Visa.

NCD
No cash drawer. Stops the cash drawer from opening during a counter sale.

EFTPOS
EFTPOS transaction.

CO
Allow cash out in EFTPOS transactions

Type
Options are:
Standard
Require name - a standard transaction but in the Tendering window  the operator has to enter a name or code against the transaction.
Tracked voucher - tracked vouchers, numbered & stored in the system
Untracked Voucher - an untracked gift voucher worth the amount recorded on it
Cheque
Instant Rew. Vou. - If using the rewards at point of sales, this will allow use of the customers current reward or part thereof.  If they only use part, they may fall below the threshold set in the reward program & be unable to redeem the rest until they once again reach the minimum.  This option will cause the tender type to appear at the far right of the tendering window regardless of the number of tender types.


Surch
Used for applying surcharges on credit cards.

RO
Rewards voucher.  A voucher issued by generating customer rewards through the rewards program.

Hide
Obsolete transaction type, will not display in the Tendering window, but is available for transaction history reports.

	


Default tender types
There is the ability to set which tender types are used by default in areas of Bookscan. You can set these for Cash Sales, Order Processing (Invoiving, Special Orders) and Debtor Payments. Set these by selecting what they are via the drop-down list in System Options - Financial tab.

Click OK button to save the settings.


.topic 2600
Many users want to apply a surcharge on certain types of credit cards which charge higher fees. In the transaction window set percentage charges against the relevant credit card types, say 1.5 % on Diners Club and 2.5% on Amex.
When you select these transactions in the Tendering window, the surcharge is added to the total price. You can overwrite the percentage in the Tendering window if you wish.

.topic 2030

The Status section of Bookscan allows for you to maintain the different Status you may want to use within the system. (E.g. Active). The status' are a field used within the Title Master that can be set for each title to indicate the status of that title.
To maintain Status' on the main menu click on Tables,then point to Authority Tables and then click on Status.



ONIX Status'
To load the ONIX standard Status codes, press F12, this will populate them.
If you already have your own custom status codes, simply use the Global functionality as described below to merge your old codes into the ONIX ones.

Status Fields
Code -  3 characters code to uniqely identify the status
Description - the description of the status
B/O - Stock that is allowed to be back ordered. (wholesale)
No Sell - Stock that cannot be sold (e.g. Harry Potter, prior to release date, may have a status of 'Awaiting Date', to stop it being sold accidently). This will not allow the item to be sold anywhere in the program.
Web - Stock thats not to be exported when using the Web Export module
No ONIX - Stock that's not to be exported when creating ONIX files using the ONIX export.


Manage Status Codes

Add
To add a new Status, click on the Add button.
Enter the following:
Code (3 characters)
Name
ONIX code (select from drop down list) optional. This allows you to map the ONIX code, to your own codes. This is helpful when importing titles from title page etc.
Allow backorders (check box to select) - a tick means to allow.
No Sell (check box to select)  - a tick means to exclude from sell able stock
Allow web sales (check box to select) - a tick means to exclude

Click the OK button.

Edit
To edit an existing Status, click on the Status and click the Edit button
You can make changes to:
Name
ONIX code (select from drop down list) optional
Allow backorders (check box to select) - a tick means to allow.
No Sell (check box to select)  - a tick means to exclude from sell able stock
Allow web sales (check box to select) - a tick means to exclude



Click the OK button.

You can edit 'No Sell', 'No ONIX', 'B/O', and 'Web' can be edited from the list view by  double-clicking on the tick boxes themselves.

Global
To globally change one status codes links into another.  Doing this will run through and anywhere that the old code is stored, it will replace it with the new code.
Click the Status and click Global.
Absorb Status code : The one you are changing
Absorb Into Code: The code you are changing to.

Click the OK button.

Delete
To delete an existing Status, click on the Status and click the Delete button.
Then click the Yes button.

Close
To exit the Status section, click this button.

.topic 2070

The import window allows you to import titles into the Bookscan Master database in one of the following 4 formats. CSV, ONIX, BISAC and Bookfind TAGGED format and CSV.

The import window from the main menu is located in Utilites-->Import.

Obtaining Import Files

To import files from one of the supported web sites, right click the mouse and you will be offered a context menu with each of these sites. Select one and Internet explorer will open at the corresponding web site. Follow the web site instructions and download a file in one of the 3 formats. Then follow the previous step to import the file into the import browser. Opening the Import window will create the directory "c:\import" automatically for you. Use this directory for a consistent place to download files from the web sites and import them into Bookscan.
Files can be imported from elsewhere too. (as shown below)



Importing/Loading Files

To load a file on a disk/local hard drive simply click on the file cabinet in the file detail box and select the drive, directory and filename.
Then click on the "import file" button. If the file is in one of the 4 formats Bookscan will automatically detect which one and prompt to import the file into the import browser. At this stage you will then be viewing the contents of the import file (nothing has been saved at this stage). CSV files require special setups to be done, so before using this for the first time, speak to barcode solutions.

When the file loads and displays into the browser, if the title is already in your master file, it will have a tick in on the left-hand side in the 'I' column.
Note: If a title is already in your database, if you select it and use the 'Import Selected Titles', it will not touch this title.

Adding to Draft PO when Importing

The column "DPO Qty" can be edited to allow the direct importing of titles into Draft Purchase orders for titles being imported for the first time and when updating existing titles.
Double click on the DPO Qty square for the rows and you can enter a quantity that will be added to the DPO when the title is imported/Updated. Clicking on the "DPO Qty" column header caption will also filter titles so you will only see titles that you have entered a DPO Qty into.

Options for Importing
 Price/Status Update - With this ticked, when updating titles already in your master database, it will only change the Retail Price and Product Status. This will be ticked by default and will enable any grooming that has been carried out to remain in place.
Get Images - This will allow Bookscan to obtain from the internet any images that have been included in an ONIX import file (generally an internet address of where the image is that our program will download) and store under the Publisher tab in the title master. This will slow the process down a little and requires internet access.
Manual Edit - The manual edit checkbox if checked, will also open the normal Bookscan title master window on each title to allow further editing of other master title fields. Once you have edited the title click the "select" button to accept the changes or the "cancel" button to discard the changes and optionally abort the rest of the import.


Selecting Titles to Save/Update

Once the contents of the file are listed, you can use the "Find ISBN" button to find a particular title by BARCODE / ISBN.
To import these titles into Bookscan highlight the ones you want using standard windows selection / blocking commands.

To select individual titles hold CTRL and click the left mouse button.
To select a block of titles hold SHIFT and left mouse click the first title in the block and then click the last title that you want to select.
To select all titles use the "Select All" button to highlight all titles to be imported / updated.

Importing New Titles

Once you have highlighted the titles for import you must select a supplier that these titles will be imported with from the supplier combo box.
If you also wish to have the Publisher information updated at the same time (Stored in Tables-->Authority Tables--Publisher), select from the drop-down list for Publishers.
Next click the "Import Selected Titles" button.  Then click 'Yes' to confirm.
That's all there is to it. Once you have imported  titles they will change colour to red to indicate that you have already processed them. It will also tick the 'I' column.

Updating Exisiting Titles

Once you have highlighted the titles for import (For updating, you do not need to Select a Supplier or Publisher)

The fields that will get updated are(Unless 'Price/Status Update' is ticked) : Title, Author, RRP, Edition, Year, Binding, Status, Supplier ( if one is selected in the supplier combo box, otherwise will not change ), publisher  ( if one is selected in the publisher combo box, otherwise wont change ) , and all the fields visible on the publisher tab page of the Bookscan master title window.

To Update, click "Update Selected Titles" button. This will update all titles that are selected, that are currently in your database. (If a title is not ticked, it will not add it).





Most of the columns in the import browser can be sorted to make it easier to find the titles you are looking for, especially if there are many titles in the import file. Simply click on the column header caption to re-sort the titles by that column.

Importing Images

Click on the browse icon & select a text file with just a list of ISBN's only.  One ISBN per line.

Click on Images.  Bookscan will import images for the ISBN's listed in the text file from either Nielsen or Bowker, depending on your settings, in the sizes you have selected in the Title Master - Web Search - Set Defaults window.



.topic 2080

The export functionality allows for titles stored on the database be saved into a standardised format, sent to another location and imported.
 This can be handy between stores where one location does not have online access and another one does.
The store who would be receiving it would then use the import functionality , click link for details on this.

The format of exporting can be one of two options:
·	ONIX
·	BISAC

Note: After picking the method the rest of the process is the same.
To export in one of the formats on the main menu click Utilities, move the mouse on Export, then click on either ONIX or BISAC.

Note: If using the ONIX export, titles must have the following fields populated, otherwise they will be excluded:
	(Details Tab) Title, Author, Retail Price, Status, Supplier, Binding, Imprint.
	(Publisher Tab) Publisher, Pub Date, BIC Subject, Blurb/Review (optional).

Note: If a titles status has in the status master been set to 'No ONIX', those titles will not be included.



As shown in the diagram there are a number of options on the left-hand side that you can select to pick the titles to export.

Step 1: Select Retrieval method
Select on the option you wish to use to select titles to export by clicking an option on the left-hand side.
For full details see Retrieval options section below.

Step 2: Enter a file name
In the field Export to (file-spec) field either type in the full path of the file including the file name.
Alternatively click on the file icon button on the right side of this field.  Select the save location, and type in the file name.

Step 3: Zipping
To Zip (Compress) the file, check the Package into Zip file by clicking on its box.

Step 4: Exporting
Click on the OK/Export button.
The file will be saved in the location you selected ready for external use.


Retrieval options
Exclusion Options
·	On-hand items only
Will include only titles that are currently on-hand in stock if checked.
This option can be used in conjunction with any of the Selection Options

Status exclusion
If in Tables-->Authority Tables-->Status, the 'No ONIX' field has been ticked for a status, titles with that status will not be included in the export.

Selection Options
·	All in database
Includes all titles if checked

·	By records-number
Will display two fields:
·	First record to retrieve
·	Last records to retrieve
These are the order in which they were added to the database, NOT the order they appear in the Title Master List.
The default setting is all titles 1 - title count.
Key in any changes if required.
e.g. 553 records, wish to export last 100 added.
	First record to retrieve: 453
	Last record to retrieve: 553

·	By record entry date
will display two date fields:
·	Start of record entry
·	End of record entry
This will included any titles added between and including the dates entered.
Select using the date icon.


·	By year
This is to export any titles that fall within a year.
Type in the year in the Year Code field.
e.g. 2004

·	By imprint
This will export any titles that belong to a specific imprint.
Select the imprint from the imprint drop down list. Using a drop down list, click link.
You can only select one imprint.

·	 By supplier
This will export any titles that belong to a specific supplier.
Select the supplier from the supplier drop down list. Using a drop down list, click link.
You can only select one supplier.

·	 By department
This will export any titles that belong to a specific department.
Select the department from the department drop down list. Using a drop down list, click link.
You can only select one department.

·	 By customer
This will export any titles that have been purchased by a specific customer.
Select the customer from the customer drop down list. Using a drop down list, click link.
You can only select one customer.

·	 By category
This will export any titles that belong to a specific category.
Select the category from the category drop down list. Using a drop down list, click link.
You can only select one category.

·	By individual selection
When this option is checked you will be able to individual select the titles to include to export.
Multiple selection is allowed.

Upon checking this option the Find Title window will display, behind that will be the list of all titles.
Click on the desired titles. You can select multiple by holding down the Ctrl key while clicking.
If the find window is closed you can use it again by clicking Find Title button.



Exception List
Titles that are excluded from the export due to missing fields will display a window following the export, listing the product code(s) and an abbreviation of what is missing;
Titl: Title (name)
AUTH: Author
Impr: Imprint
BIND: Binding
Price: Retail Price
PubDate: Publication Date
BIC: BIC Main Subject
Stat: Status
Pub: Publisher
Supp: Supplier




This list can be printed out by clicking the 'Print' button, so these can be corrected for next time, or corrected, then re-exported.





See also
	Importing

.topic 2240


The Copy Out Tables is used to resolve intermittent issues with comment fields in database files.  A re-index will follow the Copy Out.
 Ensure that all terminals are back to the main menu.
#Note: Running this on the server machine will allow for this  to run as fast as possible.
#Note: Some of the windows will say 'Structure Update' on them, but it is doing a Copy Out.

On the main menu click Utilities/Database Utilities/Copy Out Tables.
A message will display requiring you to confirm you have exclusive access. In accordance with the above note, click Yes.
Click Select All (you can also do individual database files if required)
Click Copy Out. This will run the Copy Out procedure, then run a re-index. This may take some time depending on the size of your database. Purging of old files and 'Packing' regularly will help improve the speed of Bookscan.
When the process is finished a message 'Copy Out Completed' will show. Click Ok, then Close.


.topic 213

Packing the tables is run to ensure that any files within the database that records that have been marked for deletion are deleted. This will help free up an data that is not required and help speed up the system and increase the free space on your computer.
This needs to be run on the server machine and at a time when Bookscan is not being used on any of the machines.

 Ensure that all terminals are back to the main menu.

On the main menu click Utilities/Database Utilities/Pack Tables.
Click OK to acknowledge that you have closed all other windows of Bookscan on all machines down.
Click Select All button (to run this procedure for all tables).
Click Pack button.

When complete a message will display indicating this, click OK button.


You can resume using Bookscan on all machines.

.topic 2110

WARNING:  To back up your files you will require exclusive access to Bookscan.
 Ensure that all terminals are back to the main menu.

The backup data files functionality allows for your database to be saved as a restore point in the event serious problems.

·	From the main menu, click on Utilities/Database Utilities/Backup Data Files.
·	This will open the backup window.
·	The backup will be saved to the data directory. This will overwite the previous backup. To save to another drive select the drive from the combo box.
·	Click on the Backup button.
·	All your data files and your reports will be zipped up and saved as the file ebbackup.zip. This may take some time depending on the size of your database.
When finished, a line will be selected in blue 'Zip Archiving Complete'.
·	Click on Exit when done.

.topic 2100

It is important to regularly re-index to ensure the integrity of your database. You should also do it after a system crash or if any anomalous data appears.
 Ensure that all terminals are back to the main menu.

· From the main menu, click on Utilities/Database Utilities/Reindex Tables.

· Highlight those tables you wish to reindex (by clicking on them)  or click on Select All.

· Click on the Re-index button.

The time it takes to re-index will depend on the speed of the computer you run the re-indexing on, the speed of your network and the  size of your databases. Running this on the server machine, if you have one will allow it to run the fastest. Regularly purging and 'Packing' your files will considerably speed up re-indexing time also.

.topic 211

The Structure update is what takes place when changes have been done to the systems database structure, typically when new versions are installed. When new versions are installing, upon opening Bookscan for the first time this will run automatically.
 Ensure that all terminals are back to the main menu.
Running this allows for these changes to be made to you existing database to reflect the new structure.
Running this can also be used to repair problems with the database where the structure has been affected.

 To run a structure update on the main menu click Utilities/Database Utilities/Structure Update, click OK, Click Yes.

This will then run the update.

Note: A structure update can take some time depending on database size. The time can be reduced by regular purging of un-necessary records and also regular 'Packing' of the data files.
Note: Running a structure update will also run a re-indexing on the database too.


.topic 2310
Archiving sales records

The Archive Maintenance window is located from the main menu of Bookscan, Utilities -> Archives or through Utilities -> Database Utilities -> Archive Maintenance.

In this window you will be able to Archive/Purge/Retrieve most of the following, but it is important that you read carefully how these work and the best way to do it.

Invoice Header Records - Invoices headers for invoices older than they need to be readily accessible
Invoice Line Records - Run following archiving old invoice headers to remove the corresponding lines of the invoice
Sales Lines Records - History of the product sold and all details of when the item was sold (including sell price, date etc)
Sales History Records - Histories of sales of stock.
Stock History Records - Histories of stock inwards, returns and transfers (in/out)
Titles  - Archives titles older than 1 year that are currently have zeros (for onhand, on order, layby, Special Order, Approval, Consignment) and have not been sold/received in 1 year or more. Important Note: Currently there is no way to get these titles back out of this archive, so be careful when using this.



Archiving

To archive old lines within a backup storage file, that have past a useful time frame is a good way to keep the system running at it's best. It also shows on the more current information that you need.

Select the Archive file type. Select from the Archive Records the date you wish to archive on or before.
Click Archive Records (F12). Confirm by clicking Yes button.

Note: If archiving a file type for the first time and wishing to archive many years of data, it is advisable that you do so in 1 year lots (working backwards from the present).

This will run for as long as it takes depending on the number of records that need to be moved. When it is done, it will fill the window with records listen, these are the records that have been moved into the archive. If when you first load the window and select a file type, if records are already listed this is because it has been run before and the records shown are being loaded from the archive (This helps if you wish to retrieve records from the archive).

Note: After running the archiving, you will need to run a pack (Utilities -> Database Utilities -> Pack Tables -> Select All -> Pack. )

This works the same for all the options within the Archive File menu.

Retrieving sales records

This window will also allow you to retrieve back into the main database files records that have been archived previously.

Select a Archive File type. This will then load records shown in the archive.

Select either a date range (to pull back records between two dates) or in the list of records below, hold Ctrl and click on individual records you wish.

Click Restore Records (F11). This will then move the old records back. (This may take some time depending on the number of records).

Purging sales records

You can also purge records that have been archived (so that over the time the archive files themselves do not have data that's too old).

Select a Archive File type. This will then load records shown in the archive.

Select either a date (of which to delete where older than) or in the list of records below, hold Ctrl and click on individual records you wish.

Click Purge Records (F9). This will then delete the records permanently. (This may take some time depending on the number of records).

Title Archive

Archiving titles removes them from the title master and stores them in the archive file. Earlier versions of Bookscan only saved selected fields from the title master to the archive. In the present version of the program all fields are retained.

All sales history is retained.

Archived title can be easily restored to the title master.  Go to Sales / Enquiry / Archive Enquiry and search by Author, Title or ISBN.  You will be shown a list of matches from which you can select



Archiving
First re-index your master file and do your daily backup.
Select the date the titles were last sold.
Select the date the titles were last purchased
Select the date the titles were created.
Only titles with no stock, no special orders and none on order will be archived.



Supplier Purge

This functionality will remove suppliers from the Supplier master that are not being used. It determines this by using a supplier and if no products are linked to this, it will delete them (the supplier).

Right click in the archive window, then select "Purge un-used suppliers". This may take some time to run depending on the size of your title master. It is a good idea if you do this following running the Title archive.


.topic 2250
The Build Blank Database function allows you to create a 'blank' copy of your database to use at another site. This is handy if you are opening a new branch of your business or wish to create a blank copy of your database to send to someone else.

What does a 'Blank' database contain;
Titles (Including Names, Authors, suppliers, prices, publisher details, categories, department, abstract, comments)
Customers (Including Names, Addresses, Phone numbers, email address, financial details)
Suppliers( All details, except stock movement/history)
Bindings
Currencies
Imprints
Departments
Location
Publishers
Return Codes
Sales Reps
BIC Subjects
Transport

What does it not contain:
Titles (Sales/Purchasing/Layby/movements/approval/backorders/consignment/specials history, stock figures[all])
Customers (Specials, laybys, backorders, purchases)
Suppliers (Stock movements/history)

Creating a Blank Database
To open the Build Blank Database, got to the main menu and select Utilities/Database Utilities/Build Blank Database.

Select a path to save the copy of the database to.
Click OK button. The time to run will depend on the size of your master files.
When finished a message 'Process Complete' will display. This should say '15 database created'. Click OK.
Click Cancel.



Using the Blank Database
To move the newly created blank database you will need to burn it to a CD/DVD and take/send it to the new site. Copy this folder onto C:\ of the server machine. Bookscan at this new site will need to be installed and point to this folder as the data directory.

If you are giving the blank database to another store and you wish to not pass on your customer details, simply go into the  blank databases folder delete the files 'Customer.dbf' and 'Customer.fpt'. The same can be done for your sales Reps also, but following the same process.


.topic 215

The Standby functionality of the Bookscan allows for terminals that have their databases remotely, (on a different machine on the network), to operate when there is temporary loss of connection to that database.

The standby functionality will activate when Bookscan is started and there is no connection to the database.  It will prompt the user to initiate standby when this is the case.
When connection to the database is reinstated and Bookscan is restarted, it will also prompt the user to ask if they wish to transfer all transactions that took place while in standby back to the main database.  If you choose not to, they will be stored and you will be asked again the each time Bookscan starts until they have been uploaded.

You can also choose to enter/exit standby by using the Utilities - Set/Unset Standby option.  Again, when you exit standby, if any sales have been made, you will be prompted to upload them.

Preparing for standby
Running in standby mode can only work if preparations are made at regular intervals (Suggest weekly).
This preparing will create a copy of the titles, customers and suppliers etc currently in the database onto the local machine in C:\standby directory.

·	To run the preparations on the main menu click Utilities, then click Create Standby.
·	Read the information.
·	Then click Start button.
·	This will then create a backup of the information required to run in standby mode, then run a re-index on the backup database.
·	When this finishes click OK button.

.topic 2120

Once you have set up  Bookscan  be very careful of what features you change in Systems Options. Some of the setup features are purely technical and should not be altered except in consultation with Barcode Solutions. Others control the way the system (and your business) operates and you may need to change them as your business practices change.
The following is a guide to those features:

To open the System Options set of windows, click on Utilities in the main menu. Then click on System Options.

System Options windows
Below is a summary of the system option windows. Click on the
window name for details.

Licensee 	This is where the basic details of the shop are stored.				

Stores	If you have multiple stores, you can set up how they will interact here.

Dockets	These fields control how your computer system is set up.

Copies	This window allows you to set the number of copies you will print of each document and set some other system defaults.

Modules & Options	In this window you can select which Bookscan modules you will be using.

Financial	In this window you can set a variety of financial defaults.

Counters	In this window, you can set the numbering sequences for your documents.

Titles	In this window you can set up the defaults for your title master.

Purchasing     	Here you can set the defaults for the purchasing screens.		

Sales	This window has various system options which you can set, particularly relating to sales.

Sundry	This window allows you to set various system options and allows you to access purchasing budget figures.




.topic 2750
These are local settings stored in the Windows registry and need to be set for each workstation.



Path for standard Data Files
This field shows the path of you Bookscan data files.

Monitor Ht:800
Select if you have a widescreen monitor (16:10 or 16:9)

Docket printer configuration
Almost all users can just use the Windows driver settings here. If you do wish to use Citizen or Epson OPOS drivers, you will first need to install the drivers.
Unless you have "No docket printer" selected, you will need to enter the name of the docket printer in the Printer Name field.  You can find the printer name by clicking on the [F2] button and selecting the printer from the drp down list.

Cash drawer
If there is a cash drawer attached to this workstation, you will need to select the appropriate radio button here depending on your printer.

(In Label printers quadrant)
Docket Cutter
If your docket printer has a cutter, you can activate it here.

Docket cutter command
If the default command does not work, you can enter the appropriate command code here.

Barcode Printer Configuration
Barcode printer type
Select the barcode printer type from the drop down list.
Barcode printer name
Enter the printer's name in the Barcode Printer Name field. You can find the printer name by clicking on the [F3] button and selecting the printer from the drp down list.


Carton label printer
If you wish to print carton labels from this workstation, enter the name of the carton label printer here. You can find the printer name by clicking on the Start button in Windows and selecting Printers & Faxes.

Ticket printer
If you are licensed to use the Ticketing module and wish to print tickets from this workstation, enter the name of the ticket printer here. You can find the printer name by clicking on the Start button in Windows and selecting Printers & Faxes.

EFTPOS: If you are using integrated EFTPOS, set the type EFTPOS equipment you are using. Bookscan supports Ingenico, Payment Express and Tyro.

	Note that you require a license to use this feature. Contact Barcode Solutions if you are interested in using online EFTPOS.

Proxy Name List:  If you use a proxy server, set the name here.

Proxy Bypass List: Set the name of the server used to bypass the proxy server here.


.topic 2760
In this window you can see the contents of the audit log.  Print a date range from it and purge old audit records.

When an operator performs a task that creates transaction, the operator code is recorded against the transaction ( e.g. an invoice or a cash sale). However not all tasks result in the creation of a transaction, for example deleting a special order or closing sales for the day. In these cases a record is created in the audit log and the date, time, operator, register and the details of the task are recorded.




If it gets too large you will be prompted to purge it for better performance any window that writes to it.  (Particularly noticed as lag between making a sale & the docket printing.)

To Purge the archive of old records, select a date at least one month in the past, then click on the Purge button.  This will mark all the records in the file up to that day as deleted.  To remove them fully run Pack Tables from the Utilities / Database Utilities menu.  This removes the deleted records and will restore the system performance.

To Print records, select a date range and then click on the Print (F9) button.

To see transactions made by a particular operator, select their code from the drop down list.  You must have Use operator IDs selected in the Sales tab of the System Options to use this.

print preview allows you to preview the report and choose whether or not you print it.

.topic 2200
You can set up security in four different ways:

Full security
The full security module allows you set  restrict access for each operator to every part of the program.
Operators must login (optionally with a password) to use the system.
The system will maintain an audit log of all operations such as record deletions and additions and price changes. This log can be viewed in the Security window (Utilities\Security).
Additionally the operators' initials are recorded against each transaction.
Contact Barcode Solutions if you wish to purchase this option.

Prompt for operator initials.
This option forces the operator to enter his ( two character) initials before using any of the sales screens.
In cash sales, the operator will need to enter his initials before each transaction.
The operator's initials are recoded against each transaction.
To turn on this option, open System Options from the main menu, select the Sales tab and set the Prompt for operator initials flag.
This option can be used in conjunction with the full security module.

Use encoded initials.
This option is a variation of the Prompt for operator initials option, adding a higher level of security.
Instead of entering  operators entering their two character initials they instead enter a three character which is set against each operator in the Operator window.
This options stops an operator from logging in using another user's initials.
To turn on this option, open System Options from the main menu, select the Sales tab and set the Prompt for operator initials flag and then the Use encoded initials flag.

Master Password
If you are not using the security module you can still password protect the System Options window. The password can be set on the Licensee tab by clicking on the Set System & Password (Alt F9) button.

Setting up Operators
Operators are set up in the Operators window. From the main menu select Utilities\Security\Operators.

Viewing the Audit log
When an operator performs a task that creates transaction, the operator code is recorded against the transaction ( e.g. an invoice or a cash sale). However not all tasks result in the creation of a transaction, for example deleting a special order or closing sales for the day. In these cases a record is created in the audit log and the date, time, operator, register and the details of the task are recorded.
You can view the audit log in the Security window. Go to the main menu and select Utilities\Security\Audit window.
In the window you can:
View all operations performed on Bookscan except transaction creation.
Select an operator from the combo box to view just that operator's actions.
Print an audit report filtered by date range (and optionally filter by operator).
Purge old audit records.

Stock adjustments
If you are using the full security module, you can restrict which operators can perform stock adjustments. Stock is adjusted up in the Receiving window by "receiving" stock against a supplier called "Stock Adjustment". Similarly stock is adjusted down by creating a "return" against the same supplier. After you have created the stock adjustment supplier (usually supplier code STK, supplier name Stock Adjustment), open the Purchasing tab in System Options and nominate that supplier  as the stock adjustment supplier by selecting it in the Stock Adjustment Supplier combo box there. Those operators without the authority to do stock adjustments will not be able to receive from or return to this supplier.

.topic 1960

Using the Security system has three major benefits. It
assists in maintaining the accuracy of your data by recording the operator initials against each transaction they create.
improves the security of your system; you can restrict user access to only those parts of the system that they need to use.
maintains an audit log of additions, deletions, globals and changes to your database.

Setting up the security system.
You can set up security in four different ways as outlined in Setting up security:

Operator access
From  main menu, click on Utilities\Security\Operators.
In the System Log in Box that opens, type in your initials and password. The Maintain Operator Data window will now open.



Adding a new operator
·	Click the Add Record (F3)  button  and enter a unique Operator ID and the operator's Name and (optionally) a Password.
If you are using the Use encoded initials option, enter a three character code in the Sign In field (not displayed if this option hasn't been set.
·	Then click Save Record (F3).

Operator system access
If you are running the full security module you can restrict an operator's access to only those parts of Bookscan that they need.
System access is set using the three list boxes in the lower half of the Operators window:

Operational groups
This list box lists the four main groupings of system access:
sales	cash sales, invoicing, approvals etc.
purchasing	all the stock control functions - receiving, returns, stock adjustments etc.
modules	stocktake, warehousing, prepack and other modules.
utilities	system options, database utilities, security etc	
Click on any of these groups to display the current users access in that area.
			
Accessible areas & operations
This list box shows the current operator's system access for the area selected in the Operational Groups list box.
Click on an area in this list box to see its definition in the field below the Operational Groups list box.

All areas & operations
This list box shows all the available areas and operations for the operational group selected in the Operational Groups list box.
Click on an area in this list box to see its definition in the field below the Operational Groups list box.

Setting up an operator's system access
Select the operator in the Operator browser
Click on operational group you wish to amend in the Operational groups list box.
All the areas in this grouping will be displayed in the All areas & operations list box and the operator's current access in the Accessible areas & operations list box.
To give access to an area, select it in the All areas & operations list box and click on the <--- button. Use the Shift and Control keys for multiple selections.
To remove access to an area select it in the Accessible areas & operations list box and click on the --- > button. Use the Shift and Control keys for multiple selections.
To save your changes click on the Save (F8) button below the Operational groups list box.
To cancel your change click on the Reload (F9) button.

Shortcuts to setting up operator access
The Operator's window provides a few shortcuts to speed up setting user access to the system:

Cloning an operator
The most useful of these shortcuts is cloning an operator's access.
Select the operator whose access you wish to set in the Operator browser.
Click on the Clone (F12) button.
A dialogue window will open with a combo box listing all operator's. Select the operator whose access you wish to copy and click on the Clone Profile (F12) button.
The first operator's accessible areas will be overwritten with the second user's access.
Note that all areas for all operational groups will be overwritten not just the visible one.

Pre-set profiles
Operator's can quickly be given one of three pre-set profiles:
Manager	Allows access to all areas of the program.
Warehouse	For wholesale setups, sets up a storeman's restricted access.
Sales basic	Sets limited access to basic sales functions.
When using pre-set profiles, the system access in the Accessible areas & operations list box will be automatically set. You can then make any changes to the access that you wish before saving the profile.

Step by step instructions to set these accesses up:
Click on either the Manager, Warehouse or Basic sales radio button.
Click on the Load Profile (F11) button.		
Note that all areas for all operational groups will be overwritten not just the visible one.
Manually make any changes you may wish to make to the operator's access.
To save your changes click on the Save (F8) button.

Pre-set area profiles
Operator's can quickly be given access using pre-set area profiles using the check boxes in the Profiles box.

In Sales and Purchasing you can set up three basic profiles:
Enquiry 	Operator can perform on enquiry functions in either sales or purchasing and cannot enter transactions or make changes.
Processing	Operator can create transactions, add and delete records and access the relevant tables but has no access to global functions.
Housekeeping	Operator has full access to either all sales or all purchasing functions.

In the Other functions you can quickly set up access to:
Modules	All the optional modules you are running.
Utilities 	System options, database utilities, pricing and security.
Tables	Database tables.	

Step by step instructions to set these accesses up
Click on the General radio button.
Check the area check boxes you require e.g. Sales Processing, Purchasing Enquiry and Modules.
Click on the Load Profile (F11) button.		
Note that all areas for all operational groups will be overwritten not just the visible one.
Manually make any changes you may wish to make to the operator's access.
To save your changes click on the Save (F8) button.

Editing operator access
Click on the user that you want to edit in the Operator's browser .
·	To amend the operator's name, just type your changes in the Name field and then click Save Record (F3).
·	To delete an operator click Delete (F4) button.
·	To change a password click Change Password (F7) button. .

	Use pre-printed label -  This option can only be selected if 'Special pricing label' is being used. This additional setting allows  	you to print the specials onto a label that are already pre-printed e.g. ('Special', 'RRP and 'Our Price' ) Or ( 'Sale', 'Was' and 'Now').


Click the Print(F12) button to print. Or press the F12 on your keyboard.
Once sent to print, the window will clear, ready for the next product to be scanned/keyed

.topic 2
	Report Writer  Overview


	Report Writer extracts reports from a Bookscan or a
	PlusScan database, supplied by Barcode Solutions.


Report Writer Properties 			:  Configuring Report Writer.
Report Processing 				:  How to use Report Writer.
Report Parameters 				:  Filtering data into reports.  (Lists all.)
Modifying Inbuilt Reports 			:  Adapting predefined report formats.
Creating And Modifying Your Own Reports 	:  Developing new reports.  Mainly tips.
ReportPro Designer Help			:  Help for the built-in report designer.








Version 1.0

.topic 3
Report Writer Properties


Report Writer's properties can be view and/or modified from an entry on the File menu.

Report Writer uses the data-files stored in your Bookscan database folder.   Report Writer performs a quick test on these values each time that it is run.

Once Report Writer  finds the data, it assumes that its inbuilt report forms will be found in a sub folder called Reports.

The Properties window also contains three options pertaining to the Preview window (available immediately after reports are generated and before printing).  The first is a checkbox by which previewing can be omitted or not, as a default.  (This default can be overridden for any report which begins by opening a parameters window.  If the right mouse-button is clicked over the window, a popup menu makes it possible to switch previewing on/off as required, and the selection made will remain in force until a change is made by either the same means or through the Properties window, or through quitting Report Writer.)

You can also set a default size for the preview window, as well as a default zoom factor.

.topic 4
Report Parameters

Most of Report Writer's built-in reports can be run to reflect various subsets of the full, available data, e.g.: many reports can apply a date filter, or be run for different departments, suppliers and so forth.  These selections are the report's parameters.   They are made selectable as soon as you opt to run almost any report.   Report Writer will trap errors made in the selection process.

The available parameters vary from report to report. To learn about the particular parameters of particular reports, make a selection below:

	Customer reports
	Departmental reports
	Supplier reports
	Purchasing reports
	Sales reports
	Creditor reports
	Debtor Reports
	Title Reports
	Stocktake Reports
	Other reports


See also:	Selection Controls;  Executing ReportSCAN from the command line.


.topic 5
How Report Writer Works

As well as extending to end-users the ability to design their own reports from scratch, Report Writer contains numerous predefined reports for users of Bookscan and PlusScan software.   Users familiar with those reports should soon come to terms with the layout and operation of Report Writer.

Report Writer's reports can be redesigned, using the same tools that are available for the creation of the reports (but taking advantage of Report Writer'sfiltering mechanisms at the same time).  The main report-design tool is a package called ReportPro,  which is a standard component of Report Writer itself.

ReportPro stores report-definitions (usually called report-forms) in .rpt files which you can create, modify and run at your leisure, via Report Writer's windows.  Report Writer's other contribution to reports is to select, gather, sort and summarise data for certain report-forms which are an integral part of the system.  In designing your own reports from scratch, you have to find a way to make ReportPro perform these extra services (which are usually within its power).  When you are working within the Report Designer you will have access to ReportPro's own Help services.

When Report Writer is preparing data for one of its integral reports, it puts a picture (something like a radar-scanner) on the monitor, to keep you informed that something's happening  -  because certain reports may keep you waiting for a while.   You can kick off another report or function while Report Writer is already working on one.

See also:	Selection Controls;  Executing ReportSCAN from the command line.

.topic 124
Purchasing Reports:		Supplier Draft POs
					Command-line execution enabled.

	Purpose:

	Parameters:
Supplier(s).

	        /DRAFT_PO    /<WantPreview>    /<SupplierCode> /<SupplierName>
	DRAFT_PO		-	Identifies the report to be run.
	WantPreview		-	/Y gives a preview, /N avoids it, /? will let the user choose.
	SupplierCode 	-	The supplier code.  Selection will be made available, if omitted.
					"ALL" (no quotes), as <Supplier> reports all Draft POs on file.

.topic 153
Purchasing Report:		Reprint GRN(s)


	Purpose:
Prints goods received notes, for goods which have not yet been posted into stock.

	Parameters:
Supplier(s).

	Output (subject to system settings):
ISBN, Title, Qty, PO#, Inv Cost, Sell Price, Discount and tax components.
	        Subtotals are by supplier and invoice-number.
	        Section 2 reports Titles Not Supplied / Unfilled Special Orders.
	        Section 3 prints requisition slips.
	        Section 4 prints special order slips.
	        Section 5 prints (special order) letters to customers.
	        Author, Binding and more can be readily added  (see Modifying System Reports).

	Report-form Variables reset at runtime:
cTaxName	  	-	GST or VAT (used in column-labelling).
	         Also, various flags determining which sections of the report may be omitted.

See also:	Selection Controls.

Sales Report:	Reprint  Invoice/Credit Range	
			Command-line execution enabled.
	Purpose:

	        Option to bypass the print-preview.

	        /INV_REP    /<WantPreview>    /<StartingInvoiceNo>    /<LastInvoiceNo>

	INV_REP		-	Identifies the report to be run.
	WantPreview		-	/Y gives a preview, /N avoids it, /? will let the user choose.
	StartingInvoiceNo	-	Low (or only) invoice-number in range to be printed.
	LastInvoiceNo	-	May be omitted to print only one invoice, else last in range.

	Output:
Conventional invoicing details.

See also:	Selection Controls,  Executing Report Writer From The Command Line,

.topic 7
Creating And Modifying Your Own Reports

New reports can be created quite readily.   For an elementary exercise, try the following:   Select New from the File menu, then Standard from the submenu.   (At this point Report Writer's menus are replaced by the ReportPro Designer's.)

From the list of data tables, select BINDING.DBF.  You are then given a blank report-form.   Select the Text tool (from the Tools->Drawing Tools submenu) and define a text output area above the Header band, by clicking, dragging, then releasing the left mouse-button.  Type "BINDING CODES REPORT" into the box created.  This will be printed as the header on each page of the report.

The data from the BINDING.DBF table will be formatted in the Body segment/band of the form.  Select Field from the Tools->Drawing Tools submenu (or click the Field icon on the toolbar).  Drag/drop a field onto the "Page - Body" band of the form and the Expression Builder will pop up, to help you define what output you want.  Double-click on "Binding.Code" from the fields list, then OK.  Create a second output field for "Binding.Name", positioning it alongside the Code field.   The report is now ready to show you the Binding codes alongside the relevant descriptions.

HINT:	To move and resize the fields you have created, first select the Pointer as your Drawing Tool.

To run the report, you can use Print Preview from the Designer's File menu (or Open from Report Writer's File menu).

For more complex forms of data access you may wish to look at the report-forms for the inbuilt reports (which you can do by pressing the Modify button on the main selection window).

Reports worth making for yourself probably need access to the system data, i.e.: the data controlled by Bookscan.  ReportPro  (the system you use to create reports  -  or to redesign them), has been configured to grant that access by  default.

The system is quite complex and there are lots of file-and-field references required before certain classes of information can be extracted in a fully reliable fashion.   If you are determined to generate complex reports of your own, and looking over the internal reports doesn't  provide all of the answers that you need, you can always talk to the helpful and friendly support team from Barcode Solutions.



.topic 10
A report-form is an external (ReportPro) file containing the instructions concerning the placement and presentation of all data on any output page.  The file-name has a .rpt extension.  If you have the full ReportPro package you can edit the file simply by double-clicking on it from the Windows Explorer.  Otherwise you can load the ReportPro Designer through the options on Report Writer's File menu.

.topic 6
Modifying System Reports

To change a report, highlight it in the main selection window then click the Modify button.   Only one user at a time can modify any given report.

Before you see the normal "Parameters" window (if the report has one) you will be presented with some information about the effect that any changes will have and what  variables will be using the report-form's default settings.  (NOTE:  these defaults can be changed, to vary the preview's performance, without upsetting the runtime  which, itself, overrides these particular defaults.)

Report Writer generates data for the report, as usual.  This is so that you will be able to make modifications to the report-form without receiving messages about undefined data sources.   If it generates an empty file it will give a warning before loading the report form (an empty file will usually emasculate the Print Preview available within the Designer).

You can modify the data displayed in the report, the font(s) employed, column-orders, spacings, titles and almost anything else
"fixed" about the report.  Certain variables (other than the database values) can also be obtained by any report.    And you can easily add a picture if you need a report to include the corporate logo or somesuch.

If you use Print Preview (on the File menu, whilst modifying the report-form) do not take the output literally.  Although correct in most ways, it can be distorted by the report-form's Variables (which are not set to match the report data when in Modify mode).   Run the report as usual to get a true picture.

If you have saved changes to the report-form and later wish that you hadn't, look on the Report Writer's File menu for:  Restore Original Report Writer Report.

To see more about individual reports, their variables and output, make a selection below:

	Customer reports
	Departmental reports
	Supplier reports
	Purchasing reports
	Sales reports
	Creditor Reports
	Debtor Reports
	Title Reports
	Stocktake Reports
	Other reports

.topic 147

	Select a report to view details of selection-options, purpose, output and variables:

	Consignment	-	Report stocktake consignment quantities.

lMovement  -	determines if the report is concerned with stock movements generally.
lOrders	-	determines if the report is focussing on orders.
lAbs	-	formats certain output either with or without sign  (+ or -).


See also:	Selection Controls.

	On Hold			-	All or nominated titles on hold.
	On Order			-	Customers with nominated title on order.
	Buyers				-	Purchasers of a specific title.
	Sales				-	Reports all sales.
	Valuation
		All			-	Values all on-hand stock
		Departments (all)	-	Values all stock by department
		Department (single)	-	Values stock for selected department
		Supplier		-	Values nominated supplier's stock
		Errors			-	Reports values outside nominated ranges
		Aged Stock		-	Identify (and/or mark down prices on) non-selling stock.

                        SellMarkRate	-	Mark-down rate for Sell price.
	        dStart		-	The earliest date for the report. (See next.)
